# Platform issues

Last updated: 2026-10-14 (final-window workflow review complete: 178/178; month-wide review stopped at user request)

Living tracker for user-reported problems, production incidents, code-review findings and proposed improvements. Add new issues with stable IDs; update existing entries rather than duplicating them. Do not mark an issue fixed solely because code exists locally: record verification and deployment separately.

## Evidence and scope

The final user-confirmed window is **12 October 2026 00:00 Dubai to 14 October 09:54:11 Dubai** (`2026-10-11T20:00:00Z` inclusive to `2026-10-14T05:54:11Z` exclusive): 178 tasks/80 users/491 new runs. All **178/178 selected workflow histories are reviewed, with zero unchecked**; source-payload/native-verification limits are explicit. Coverage is tracked in the [final-window ledger](output/platform-audit-2026-10-14/coverage.md); the [consolidated report](output/platform-audit-2026-10-14/report.md) distinguishes measured baselines, current defects, hypotheses and unresolved evidence. The earlier month review stopped at **655/1,229**; it is not complete. Current cache coverage is **17.81% of input tokens**, while 87.41% of calls report some cache hit. All causal attribution is not established. [SYSTEM_PROMPT.md](SYSTEM_PROMPT.md) is a proposal only, incorporating Berry's existing rules plus the DeepSeek and new Claude references; it is not deployed or model-validated.

The stopped monthly audit used **2026-09-13T05:03:58Z inclusive to 2026-10-13T05:03:58Z exclusive**. It covers 1,229 retained tasks across 167 users, with earlier history where needed. All histories were exported; full review stopped at 655/1,229. Consult the [monthly coverage record](output/platform-audit-2026-10-13/coverage.md) for actual progress and the [monthly report](output/platform-audit-2026-10-13/report.md) for findings under investigation. Existing historical counts below must not be read as current monthly findings. This phase changes documentation only.

The completed earlier weekly review used a fixed window of 2026-09-02T05:52:57Z–2026-09-09T05:52:57Z: 384 active tasks, including their earlier histories (21,465 messages; 1,612 runs). All 384 exports are present. The conversation review is **complete**: 383 available histories read end to end, plus one task verified to have zero messages and runs. The message/run totals include earlier history. Full raw payload and artifact verification remains separate. The [coverage ledger](output/platform-audit-2026-09-09/coverage.md) distinguishes reviewed conversations from pending tasks and detailed source/trace follow-up. These counts differ from the earlier rolling aggregate below.

The September audit scanned 1,391 runs across 103 users and 380 tasks in the rolling week approximately 2–9 September 2026. It examined failure records and selected relevant conversations. **It did not manually review every complete task conversation.** A completed run does not prove that its answer or artifact was correct.

Evidence labels:

- **Observed:** recorded production failure or conversation example. User reports are distinguished from independently reproduced defects.
- **Code finding:** behavior found in the implementation; its contribution to production incidents is not established unless stated.
- **Proposal:** improvement hypothesis requiring measurement or reproduction; not a confirmed production defect.

Weekly incidents are historical evidence, not proof that the same problem remains on today's deployment. Baseline production revision: `99964f18d52a27a587c3b3880b4511cbe8b3a9cc`. Sources: [weekly audit](output/platform-audit-2026-09-09/report.md), [test instructions](output/platform-audit-2026-09-09/testing.md). Avoid storing employee email bodies, credentials or sensitive business figures here.

## Details

### H-001 — Queue delivery and steering

- Evidence: 30 runs / 14 users / 18 tasks failed with `session_entries_run_id_fkey`; 8 occurrences on the audited current revision. None of 27 weekly queue entries delivered; all had zero attempts.
- Confirmed cause: journal insertion referenced the next run before that run existed under a non-deferrable foreign key.
- Additional local findings/fixes: projection ordering, completed-answer rollback on promotion failure, idle resume, stream reattachment, actual queued-item steering, duplicate retry protection, budget admission and reservation identity.
- Verification: 8 PostgreSQL scenarios and 4 Chromium scenarios passed; focused tests, type checks and web build passed. Browser tests use a deterministic model and local HTTP adapter.
- Remaining: deploy corresponding API/worker/web changes together and verify synthetic production queue/steering flows. Do not replay expired historical requests automatically.
- **Implementation (14 October):** FK failure reproduced against `99964f1` with real PostgreSQL and the real worker (completed run rewritten to `failed`, queue row `paused` with `attempt_count=0`, no follow-up run). Prepared fix reviewed and committed: run row inserted before its journal entry, promotion after the predecessor projection inside a savepoint, strict FIFO, idle promotion, stable queue budget identity, session-bound idempotency, pause-on-cancel, and web retry/steer fixes. Isolated verification: typechecks, 126 focused unit tests, 8 PostgreSQL and 4 Chromium scenarios. Status: committed locally, **not deployed**; API, worker and web must ship together. See [IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRESS.md](IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRESS.md#h-001--queue-delivery-and-steering-p0).
- **Monthly revalidation (13 October):** 83 terminal FK errors / 67 tasks / 42 users. Running compiled promotion still writes the journal before the next run exists. Of 107 retained monthly queue records, zero delivered; 56 expired, 49 cancelled, 2 paused. Stored attempt zero can result from rollback and does not prove no delivery was attempted. Do not count all cancellations as failures. Prepared local queue changes remain undeployed. See [monthly plan](output/platform-audit-2026-10-13/report.md).

### H-002 — Rewrite changes voice, sender or intent

- Evidence: D. Kelly's reviewed conversation changed a first-person recommendation into impersonal wording and added an unintended approval/confirmation request. Task `46afb840-0b64-4d22-b623-40ae204f1fd7` independently contains a correction to write as the user rather than the quoted email author.
- Change: preserve author, audience, perspective, facts, uncertainty and intended action; distinguish quoted authors from the sender.
- Status: faithful-editing prompt hotfix was deployed on 2026-09-24 and is present in the running worker inspected on 2026-10-13. Prior provider evaluation covered six synthetic cases on the default route; that is not broad semantic validation. Monthly real-use review is checking remaining additions, perspective changes and prompt adherence. See [deployment evidence](output/rewrite-prompt-2026-09-24/deployment.md).
- Acceptance: synthetic rewrite/reply examples pass factual, sender and intent checks across supported models, including explicit requests to change perspective.
- **Monthly revalidation:** deployed prompt still permits material meaning changes. Task `3a082f15-a260-4c1a-b690-97d31bed7b39` softens certainty/risk; task `ef8d5943-93df-4fe5-aa4b-0e02716fdda8` adds a priority order. Other reviewed polish tasks add variants, subjects and long change lists. No complete post-hotfix first-person/third-person prevalence claim is made. Add default grammar/structure-only polish mode, preserve follow-up intent and evaluate real examples across supported routes. Drafting a reply is a broader intent than polishing supplied text.
- **Daria current example:** task `1aa1f0a7-118b-4ef3-b743-cfe2aa89dec1` (September28) changes could accommodate to can accommodate and adds a travel location, then explicitly says it made the response definite. Requested colour selection is unavailable in the plain TXT source. Preserve modality and distinguish inferred source selection from actually visible formatting.

- **Additional post-hotfix correction:** October6 task `c62a5ca8-3837-4470-b1bc-709062be171d` adds an invented reason for a delayed proposal when asked only to make the email warmer; user explicitly removes it. Scope regression should include text extraction, where October12 task `0c31c38e-6004-40ce-aea6-024a54da9472` needs a second turn to obtain only the requested text.

### H-003 — Consistent error classification and recovery

- Evidence: common worker failure handling returns an error string with a repair-attempt prefix. Some subsystems already have typed errors; this is not a claim that all classification is absent.
- Proposal: a common error contract with category, retry eligibility, recovery action and outcome certainty. Separate missing resources, invalid arguments, auth/policy blocks, transient failures and unavailable providers.
- Acceptance: deterministic failures do not cause blind retries; safe transient failures recover within bounded limits; uncertain mutations are not replayed.
- Source: `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts` tool failure handling.

### H-004 — Repair loops across changed arguments

- Evidence: 3 terminal duplicate-tool-call-loop failures in the week. Separately, code matches repair failures by tool type, argument fingerprint and error text; argument changes can escape that particular counter. Other progress/loop guards already exist.
- Not established: those 3 production incidents were caused by this counter behavior.
- Proposal: count repeated underlying failures and lack of useful progress across cosmetic argument changes; give one bounded repair followed by an evidence-based strategy change or blocker.
- Acceptance: changing paths/quoting without resolving the same failure cannot sustain a long repair loop; valid iterative work remains possible.

- **Revalidation (14 October):** the code finding holds (the identical-failure counter keys on argument fingerprint), but the no-progress budget, 200-iteration limit, deadlines and spend limits still bound runs, and no trace shows a loop sustained because arguments changed; the reviewed long repairs mostly succeed. No change without a targeted reproduction; see IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRESS.md.

### H-005 — Provider availability

- Evidence: HTTP 402 caused 22 failed runs / 7 users / 11 tasks, all on the audited current revision. Exact upstream billing cause was not recorded. Image creation separately had 20 failed tool steps / 4 users explicitly reporting insufficient quota or exhausted credits.
- Action: verify current balances/routing, expose actionable health, alert on sustained failures and avoid repeated calls to unavailable routes. Fallback must comply with tenant model/data policy.
- Acceptance: a simulated billing/quota failure produces one clear blocker rather than repeated model-led diagnosis.
- **Monthly revalidation:** 103 HTTP402 terminal runs / 47 tasks / 24 users, October 6–8; no later monthly occurrence. Three-model retries in tasks `7eb80919-36b0-4603-85f4-8aa1911e4446` and `5bbb51be-9111-4a38-91db-577cc8232f4f` repeat the same blocker. Current provider outage is not asserted. Add shared-route readiness/circuit breaking and an administrator-facing action; preserve prompt/files. The separate budget-initialization fix is deployed and should not be reopened.

### H-006 — MCP readiness, network policy and authentication

- Evidence: BerryCrawl scrape had 46 failed steps / 10 users for definitions changed after review; search had 36 failed steps / 15 users for a domain absent from the allowlist. Task `8576270d-fe63-4a9e-8d56-44f0729edebf` reports invalid-token/reauthentication trouble.
- Existing safeguards: MCP health callbacks, approved-definition checks, network checks and deferred schema discovery. These checks should remain enforced.
- Action: reconcile current manifests/policy/credentials; clearly expose unavailable tools and exact administrator or reauthentication action. Prevent model repair loops on non-repairable configuration blocks.
- Acceptance: schema drift, denied domain, auth expiry and transient disconnect have distinct outcomes. No automatic approval bypass or indiscriminate network expansion.

### H-007 — Skill dependency staging

- Evidence: lazy activation stages selected resources; it does not express a general dependency graph for scripts, sibling modules, templates and other skills. Historical `office_converter` import failures occurred, but the audit did not prove dependency staging caused them or that they remain unfixed.
- Proposal: declared entry-point dependencies, staged and verified as a set; smoke-test entry points against the published skill package and sandbox image.
- Existing: resource inventories, content hashes, sandbox-aware staging state and some automatic resource recovery already exist.
- Acceptance: activating an entry point supplies required local imports/assets without the model guessing filenames. Memo fixes are excluded from reopening.
- **Monthly confirmed staging gap:** 166 office_converter missing-import steps / 148 tasks / 63 users, through October 13. Selected renderer resource imports an unstaged sibling; separate activation repairs it in traced tasks 1226/1228. Current package code has no transitive entry-point dependencies. These counts describe repaired/failed steps, not 148 failed tasks. Update the staging contract and published-package smoke tests; memo fixes remain excluded.

### H-008 — Python execution contract

- Evidence: pinned environment, interpreter wrappers and artifact smoke tests already exist in `deploy/e2b-aesg`. Nonzero bash exits already become tool failures.
- Proposal: return the verified interpreter, package capabilities, working directory and skill paths; distinguish missing package from unstaged skill module before recommending installation.
- Status: no blanket claim that Python execution is broken. Verify remaining production failures and exact image/skill versions before implementation.
- Acceptance: missing import, syntax error and bad input receive different diagnostics; entry points execute in the actual published runtime.

### H-009 — Sandbox recovery

- Evidence: 8 terminal sandbox-not-found failures across message variants; 3 on the audited current revision. Code already has ensure/resume/restore and command keep-alives.
- Code findings: actual operations can fail after handle acquisition; broad resume catches can fall toward replacement without proving disappearance.
- Action: classify lifecycle failures, invalidate stale handles, reconnect/restore and restage required inputs; bounded retry only for safe operations. Test expiry during model waits and follow-ups.
- Acceptance: injected disappearance and transient timeout yield different handling; no blind replay of uncertain shell/external operations.
- **Monthly revalidation:** 48 plain-message sandbox-not-found runs and 15 bracketed unavailable runs, with overlap not yet unioned. Existing ensure/resume/restore paths are present. The diagnosed cached-handle-after-pause reconnect change from October 7 remains local, not deployed. Verify actual operations after reconnect and distinguish policy/auth/transient errors from true disappearance. Image-only attachment preparation currently stages a sandbox unnecessarily; speed benefit remains to be measured.

- **Implementation (14 October):** the diagnosed cached-handle-after-pause reconnect fix is committed (force reconnect for follow-ups and inactive persisted state); new provider/attachment-staging regressions fail on the old sources and pass with the fix. Not deployed. Other missing-sandbox variants and typed lifecycle classification remain open. See IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRESS.md.

- **Text-only finalisation (14 October):** when the sandbox cannot be ensured at finalisation and the run executed no file-writing tool, the delivered answer now completes instead of failing (task 176); runs that wrote files still fail visibly. Not deployed.

### H-010 — Snapshot timeout cancellation

- Evidence: `withTimeout()` in `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts` rejects a wrapper promise without cancelling the underlying operation. Preservation cleanup can proceed while that operation is outstanding.
- Not established: a weekly production incident was caused by this behavior.
- Action: propagate cancellation/deadlines to capture and storage operations; prevent late completion from racing cleanup or publishing stale state.
- Acceptance: delayed snapshot reads/writes cannot continue to mutate state after timeout and cleanup.

### H-011 — Input and payload failures

- Evidence: 6 terminal Unicode-escape failures / 5 users; 6 HTTP 413 failures / 5 users; 17 HTTP 400 failures / 7 users, mostly historical Gemini requests. Failed steps also included malformed edit JSON and repeated extraction timeouts.
- Action: separate encoding, schema and size failures; validate against actual tool/provider constraints; use bounded extraction and relevant pages/chunks. Preserve source numbers and content.
- Acceptance: representative reproductions fail early with useful diagnostics or recover without submitting the same invalid/oversized payload again. Do not classify every HTTP 400 as the same root cause.
- **Monthly revalidation:** 15 terminal Unicode runs / 13 tasks / 7 users and 36 HTTP413 runs / 17 tasks / 4 users. Reviewed `.skill` ZIP and PPSX reads take a UTF-8 path; compiled extension detection lacks those types. Read by verified format/MIME/signature, handle binary diagnostics safely and review the prepared image guard, which is not deployed. HTTP400/schema failures require their own tracing; do not combine all input failures into one cause.

- **Implementation (14 October):** the prepared model-image guard (bounded previews, omission notices, final 4 MiB serialized-request gate, explicit 413 message) is committed with a lockfile regenerated for the worker's `sharp` dependency only. Not deployed. Encoding/binary dispatch and malformed tool arguments remain open.

- **Implementation (14 October, binary reads):** confirmed cause: unknown-extension and missing-type reads (`.skill`, `.ppsx`) went through the UTF-8 reader, and the resulting U+0000 made the JSONB journal write fail (reproduced on PostgreSQL with the production error). The in-sandbox reader now decodes UTF-16LE (BOM or NUL pattern) and reports other NUL-bearing files as binary; ZIP-based packages and Office variants use the existing extractors; and every tool result or tool error replaces U+0000 with U+FFFD before journaling. Not deployed. UTF-16BE, non-Latin BOM-less UTF-16, content-sniffed `.xls` and malformed tool arguments remain open.

### H-012 — Deliverable correctness

- User-report evidence: task `a2a2e640-1c61-4995-9282-db827787e4f9` reports missing costs/circular references; `2a486b91-d8d4-4687-b970-b4d90bdc9302` reports 180 days changed to 28; `a5b1958b-0d84-4662-a8ac-d15803b33626` and `f8051092-76c7-4ca2-bced-166ee8f52cce` report opening problems.
- Confidence: these are observed complaints, not independent validation of the original files or source document.
- Action: reproduce, then add format-specific opening/content/formula checks and source-linked numeric verification where missing.
- Acceptance: a successful run cannot claim a usable artifact when required validation fails; existing format validators should be reused.

### H-013 — Preparation speed

- Code findings: model attachment preparation can stage into a sandbox before loading image bytes from object storage; Office reads extract before text slicing; snapshot capture reads candidate files sequentially and checks content equality afterward.
- Proposals: lazy filesystem staging, extraction cache keyed by content/version, incremental snapshots and bounded transfer concurrency.
- Confidence: no measured latency saving or attribution of weekly slow runs yet.
- Acceptance: benchmark time-to-first-token and staging/extraction/snapshot durations before and after, with correctness and policy preserved.

### H-014 — Recovery-required continuation

- Evidence: 9 recovery-required runs / 7 users: 3 ambiguous external operations, 3 ambiguous non-idempotent operations and 3 without a populated reason.
- Action: persist clear reasons and completed/uncertain work; provide safe continuation or outcome verification. Existing protection against replaying uncertain mutations must remain.
- Acceptance: each recovery state has an actionable explanation; simulated response loss after an external mutation cannot cause duplicate execution.
- **Monthly revalidation:** 31 recovery-required runs / 25 tasks / 22 users. Metadata-only bash can inherit broad non-idempotent classification. Deployed web assets route generic continuation to retry and replace the specific error with an interrupted-response label. Use operation-specific Retry/Reconnect/Change input/Admin/Wait actions; a modal is appropriate for uncertain external writes, with exact operation evidence and mark-complete/cancel options.

- **Source alignment (14 October):** the deployed 30 September late-result fix (known outcome of a non-abortable mutation kept after its deadline) is now committed unchanged with its four regression cases. Byte parity with the deployed module hash could not be reproduced from Git plus the two recorded hotfixes, so diff production source before a Git deployment. Operation-specific recovery UX remains open.

- **Continue intent (14 October):** continuation instructions are now phase-aware (no response yet / partial text / after completed tools); with no response the pending request is answered instead of "continuing" an older answer (task 176). Unit regressions fail on the old source. Not deployed.

### H-015 — Expired queued text

- Evidence: 12 weekly queue entries expired. Current queue listing excludes expired items; expiry is 24 hours by default.
- Proposal: expose expired text for explicit copy/recovery, explaining that it was not sent. Preserve intent and attachments where available.
- Acceptance: users can recover their text without automatic execution of stale instructions.

## Additional findings from the ongoing end-to-end review
- **Monthly revalidation:** 56 retained queued items expired. Expired text remains in database but the active-item UI filters it away. Confirm the deployed web path and expose editable/copyable retained text; do not auto-replay historical work.

### H-016 — Shell success can mask an earlier failed operation

- Observed: task `48f28292-369f-4424-a0ac-61fe3bb5ea9e`, run `e33ca1ee-bddb-480d-9347-b05d5fcc82aa`, shell call `1eab87f3-b252-4f2e-ad14-cbeeb65b8d5b`: curl fails with exit 35 but a trailing echo leaves shell exit 0 and the tool reports completed.
- Proposal: structured execution helpers for common download/extraction operations; require checking produced files and each pipeline status. Avoid claiming all shell command strings can be safely reinterpreted.
- Status: observed; current fix unverified.

### H-017 — Source freshness and answer claims diverge

- Observed in the same task: final message `a27d8e37-54f8-41c8-b93e-eab489f089b1` says the official source was just pulled, although the current fetches failed and the successful source was from earlier history.
- Proposal: carry retrieval timestamp, source identity and fetch outcome alongside cached evidence; require claims to distinguish prior evidence from current verification.
- Status: observed; current fix unverified.
- **Monthly current-source defect:** task `f5ecec32-6e94-4f93-a697-f8258bd2db94` uses repealed South African dismissal authority and overstates protected-ground rules without retrieval. Official Notice 3470/2025 replaces Schedule 8 and ties automatic unfairness to dismissal reason. [Primary source](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/202509/53294gen3470.pdf). Add dated/jurisdiction-aware primary-source checks, conditional-rule tests and versioned source caching.

### H-018 — Skill saving reveals validation requirements one failure at a time

- Observed: task `042494ca-3a93-4c37-8204-58c51ddf34ef` needs four save attempts: missing YAML, missing name, invalid name, then success.
- Proposal: provide a valid scaffold and validate the complete skill schema before saving; return all actionable validation errors together.
- Status: observed; exact deployed validator and current fix need verification.

### H-019 — Validation claims do not distinguish structural and visual checks

- Observed: task `757d162d-579e-4e77-9e3b-229ddf8b6c95` fails rendering because of fonts, then reports validation passed after structural inspection. Task `01af895d-3754-468a-a211-4804c4634bdc` publishes after an office_converter import failure.
- A separate observed false positive in task `b7df5d1b-4f40-4fdc-8b12-c7ca0f07230f` flags the legitimate field label Client Name as a placeholder.
- Proposal: explicit check results for structure, content, rendering and visual review; distinguish template labels from unfilled values. Final claims must match checks actually completed.
- Status: observed; independent artifact verification and current fix remain open. Memo work stays excluded.

### H-020 — Text-only work unnecessarily enters the sandbox

- Observed: task `9ddaa7be-3466-444d-b835-aab50d5e3894` performs three directory listings, two failing, for a five-word spell check. Task `9e096ee1-b62c-44d7-8226-644cea030fc4` tries editing a nonexistent file to remove em dashes from a chat paragraph.
- Proposal: show an explicit attachment/artifact inventory; use direct text transformation for supplied prose unless a file is actually requested. Only create a sandbox when execution is needed.
- Status: observed; savings must be measured, not assumed.

### H-021 — Long model retries strand completed intermediate work

- Observed: task `f1bb2668-ed04-465a-b172-ad8f199d0cb7` creates images then fails after roughly twelve minutes with Model request failed after 3 attempts. Task `57dced38-6e3e-442b-bfe0-95f6c96fa578` similarly fails after image reads without delivering its table.
- Proposal: preserve usable partial results, show progress and actionable failure independent of model availability, and inspect retry/model timing before changing timeout policy.
- Status: observed symptom; root cause and current resolution unverified. This does not reopen user-confirmed timeout fixes without evidence.

### H-022 — Explicit corrections and output constraints are not applied reliably

- Observed: task `2a486b91-d8d4-4687-b970-b4d90bdc9302`: user says the Fourth clause is 180 days, not 28; the next answer still says 28 in Fourth and changes First instead.
- Task `e08dab1f-1358-42d1-a28d-16e5796a62fb` includes sector names despite a no-sectors requirement. Task `b1c31318-3bfc-42f5-b061-30b721440447` adds an introduction despite paragraph-only output.
- Proposal: retain explicit correction targets and constraints; synthetic evaluations must assert the final passage, not acknowledgement wording. Use deterministic checks for count/format constraints where appropriate.
- Status: observed; source-document correctness is separate from the confirmed failure to apply the requested correction.

## Maintenance rules

1. Record new user reports as reported/unverified until reproduced or supported by logs.
2. Keep production occurrence, suspected cause and proposed fix separate.
3. Track local implementation, tests, deployment revision and post-deployment verification independently.
4. Close issues with evidence; retain history and stable IDs.
5. Memo work is excluded as user-confirmed fixed. Do not assume other historical failures remain unresolved without checking.

### H-023 — Current date and deadline anchoring

- Observed: task `f0fc3e37-70b0-441b-b64c-6fb2786cea01` on September 7 described a September 4 deadline as tomorrow. Task `66f28986-22f1-44c8-b9eb-95ef1d24f0f6` on September 8 requested a presentation for tomorrow; final output reported a September 5 cover date.
- Proposal: provide current date/time per resumed turn; distinguish source document dates, issue dates and user deadlines. Check relative dates against the current turn and flag inconsistent weekdays.
- Verification needed: inspect rendered dates and prompt date injection on the deployed revision. Historical conversation discrepancies are confirmed; deployment cause is not.

### H-024 — Connector download to sandbox handoff

- Observed: task `2743dd77-227e-4343-b50b-2d0a4ea164a5` successfully called Drive file read, then repeatedly searched for downloaded bytes and ultimately asked for reattachment or a helper. The user's request to open a random file was not fulfilled with an inspected preview.
- Proposal: connector download returns a stable file handle plus a documented way to stage it into the active sandbox; downstream read/vision tools accept the same handle. Avoid asking users to re-upload a file already retrieved.
- Verification needed: inspect raw connector result and artifact staging code before attributing the gap to missing infrastructure rather than model misuse.

### H-025 — Unsupported technical verification claims

- Observed: task `3676ed9b-f08c-4dd0-a83f-8e50e072d86f` answered repeated requests for an ISO acoustics conversion without any retrieval. Its formulas and numerical examples contradict each other; later answers increase certainty and provide section citations without evidence.
- Related pattern: task `ef0b6d67-c536-49a8-934d-6b00d36179bf` cannot retrieve primary references because of domain policy, then presents document citations as exact source mappings. Distinguish what a supplied report cites from what was independently verified.
- Proposal: when challenged on a technical claim, retrieve primary evidence or explicitly preserve uncertainty; recompute formula examples and units. Do not turn a failed search into a claim of verification.
- Verification needed: independently check relevant standard passages before recording the corrected technical formula. This is a harness/prompt evaluation case, not a claim that all technical answers are wrong.

### H-026 — Ground platform answers in tenant configuration

- Observed: task `6c00b687-396a-43b2-b26b-1f4733d8c99e` gives capability and data-handling assurances without establishing the deployment/provider configuration. Public provider policies are not the tenant contract. This is not evidence of a privacy breach.
- Proposal: supply approved tenant facts for available tools, model routing, storage, retention and data handling; answer unknowns explicitly and point to the configured owner.
- Acceptance: synthetic platform/privacy questions match actual tenant settings and never invent access, guarantees or contractual terms.

### H-027 — Detect folder and empty-file uploads before starting a task

- Observed: task `32717ccc-e4e7-4d85-989b-338352efc642` receives seven zero-byte attachments twice while the user is trying to provide folders. Production metadata and sandbox checks agree that content is empty. The exact upload cause is not yet established.
- User impact: repeated reattachments and requests produce no review; the assistant invents a sync-stub explanation and extrapolates coverage gaps from folder-like names.
- Proposal: distinguish directory drops from files, recursively import supported folders or offer ZIP guidance, and show empty-file diagnostics before submission. Preserve relative paths and report upload counts/bytes. Empty inputs must not become substantive negative findings.
- Acceptance: folder drag/drop, cloud placeholders, legitimate empty text files, failed uploads and nested ZIPs have distinct tested outcomes.

### H-028 — Changed artifacts suppressed as duplicate publications

- Observed: task `f8051092-76c7-4ca2-bced-166ee8f52cce` republishes two files, rebuilds their contents after an opening-error complaint, then receives “Duplicate tool call suppressed” on both publication attempts. The assistant nevertheless says the rebuilt files are ready.
- Code finding: `persist_artifact` uses `block_after_success`; `toolRepeatSignature` hashes tool name and arguments, and `assessRepeatedToolCall` suppresses a matching completed operation without checking whether source bytes changed. An unchanged source path is therefore insufficient evidence of a duplicate publication.
- Proposal: bind publication identity to source content/version and destination metadata; preserve idempotency for identical bytes, allow changed bytes to produce a new artifact version, and return explicit published-version provenance. Do not solve this by weakening protection for unrelated external mutations.
- Acceptance: publish A, modify the same path to B, publish B, and download the latest artifact to confirm B. Repeating B must remain idempotent. Suppressed/failed publication must not produce a new-version-ready claim.
- Status: observed production sequence and matching local code; not implemented or deployed in this audit.


### Complete-history evidence update (371/384 reviewed)

- **H-002/H-022 — Tone edits invent facts:** Task `dfa847b7-ba4e-4e12-be36-2295ba96b74c` changes unknown correspondence history into “we’ve chased by email a couple of times” when asked to humanize an email. Add an evaluation that preserves unknowns/placeholders, sender voice, amounts, tense and commitments during tone changes. The same task treats a March deadline as upcoming in September (H-023).
- **H-012/H-019 — Preserve workbook contents beyond edited cells:** Task `a0d24f6f-4975-4e41-9094-abcf3aec79c2` loses cached external-formula values after openpyxl saves. The assistant repeatedly dismisses user screenshots before switching to XML edits; the user confirms the repair. Later edits remove formulas, omit total caches and restart from the original, erasing previous changes. Provide a preservation-aware edit helper, immutable source/version references, and checks of formulas, caches, drawings, package parts and the allowed edit range. This is an observed task repair, not proof of a deployed general fix.
- **H-004/H-007/H-019 — Successful tools can still form a non-progress loop:** Task `0e140ad3-1c2d-4402-b6e3-fab352362119` spends approximately 64 minutes on a CV before cancellation without publication. Wrong headshot extraction, missing sibling modules, missing render probe and template/validator font disagreement lead into repeated code inspection and crops of the same layout. Task `5910b7ee-2e69-4d9e-bfb0-5939087c725d` spends approximately 33 minutes on a CV with section suppression. Track repeated hypotheses and artifact state, not only tool errors or identical arguments; provide a bounded QA/repair route. Check current CV package changes before reopening repaired defects.
- **H-006/H-024 — Recovery matters:** Task `4a400db3-0b99-4e2b-b563-09cb7c9c427c` has reviewed-definition scrape failures, then succeeds after the user reports a repair. Task `136ce6ea-c570-4fc7-8b5c-2511bf68ca6b` confirms later successful scrapes but cannot transfer image bytes into the sandbox because of TLS and truncated base64 results. A connector-to-sandbox file handoff would address the latter separately from the historical server repair.
- **H-017/H-025 — Source claims must survive rewriting:** Task `ddd42c6c-b8f2-4635-b1cb-d480fdb7c4a0` assures the user that technical narratives come entirely from a report, admits some inferences, then again describes shortened drafts containing additions as strictly sourced. It also contains repeated queue foreign-key errors (H-001). Preserve provenance and distinguish requirements, proposed measures and verified installed/design facts.
- **H-012 — Validate the meaning of metrics:** Task `a855c564-e306-4adf-9174-a1092495e7f7` labels the proportion of high-effort bids as coordinator utilization, despite the request to reflect workload volume and progress. Make model-selected metric assumptions explicit; validate that duplicating workload changes a capacity metric. This is a semantics problem, not a Python execution failure.
- **H-025/H-022 — Reconcile plans with constraints:** Task `b8f126d0-5c21-4074-9894-71720f1917a4` silently replaces a conflicting supplied train entry with an invented connection in a “confirmed” schedule, omits an intercity transfer, and gives universal name-acceptance guarantees without verification. Its listed ticket prices also exceed its stated total. Detect conflicts and compute schedules/totals from structured facts before drafting.

The final 13 tasks are now accounted for (see the completion update below). Targeted raw-trace, artifact and current-deployment checks remain open where specified. These are observed historical sequences; proposals above are not represented as implemented or deployed.


### H-029 — Unsupported authorship and verification guarantees

- Observed: task `baef42b8-6c15-44ad-aad5-80d9c45c0d5c`, September 4–6, repeatedly guarantees that writing is human and will not trigger AI detection, using typos as proof. It also treats phrases from its own earlier draft as evidence of human authorship and suggests adding errors to appear human.
- Proposal: distinguish editorial feedback from verified authorship or detector results. Do not claim a check was performed without an actual check or guarantee outcomes from prose style. Preserve this rule in rewrite/review evaluations; a longer system prompt alone is not proof of a fix.
- Acceptance: self-authored text returned for review, human text with polished grammar, and text with typos receive calibrated style feedback without invented detector verdicts.

### H-030 — Legitimate asynchronous polling triggers loop termination

- Observed raw trace: task `a6225f3c-bbc1-4b13-9b3e-017f87f6d6b3`, run `23ab25ac-47c4-4320-a705-b5a179b99849`, September 2. `berrycrawl_get_job` returned RUNNING at 12:20:34 and 12:20:40 UTC. Both calls had `repeatPolicy=compare_result`. The next poll was suppressed at 12:20:47; another at 12:21:21 ended with `duplicate_tool_call_loop`.
- Code finding: `assessRepeatedToolCall` in `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts` suppresses after two identical completed results under `compare_result`; a subsequent suppression terminates. The tool contract explicitly supports reading an asynchronous job's progress. A repeated nonterminal status is not sufficient evidence of a useless loop.
- Proposal: explicitly classify asynchronous status tools for bounded polling, with backoff, elapsed-time/attempt budgets, cancellation and terminal-state detection. Preserve the job ID across resumes. Do not exempt all read-only tools from repeat protection.
- Acceptance: RUNNING, RUNNING, RUNNING, COMPLETED succeeds; never-completing jobs exit with a recoverable job reference and bounded requests; cancellation stops polling. Historical production revision was `6cc95ac0f7efb19a8d84bf74d472126e70c0d812`; matching local logic remains, but no production fix is claimed.

### Complete-history review finished — 384/384 tasks accounted for

- **H-009/H-021/H-013 — Durable work and progress:** Task `810f3060-b6ed-4dea-b410-ef47dfdb34ba` runs approximately 132 minutes across a timeout and continuation. No prose progress appears for approximately 115 minutes of tool activity. After resume, working files are missing and the model reconstructs the final dataset. Add durable intermediate results, deadline-aware partial publication and measured progress. This is a continuity improvement, not a request to increase timeouts. Most search calls succeeded; their volume alone does not establish a loop.
- **H-004/H-007 — Preserve repair state:** Task 50 (`edb5e6d6-bb8a-4266-9646-44c8bbfc62af`) spends approximately 73 minutes on a damper workbook with repeated argument/schema repairs. Its later PV task ends with raw tool markup rather than the expected artifact. Keep a compact record of attempted repairs, known tool schemas and remaining work across compaction; inspect the exact provider/adapter path before attributing markup leakage to the model alone.
- **H-025/H-012 — Source claims and units:** Task `a6225f3c-bbc1-4b13-9b3e-017f87f6d6b3` supplies an unsupported hotel benchmark and specific reference locations; the user says they shared the figures with a teammate, then the assistant explicitly admits fabrication. Subsequent answers still mix estimated and source-backed values, baseline/proposed labels and inconsistent energy totals. Require claim-level source provenance and deterministic unit/total checks; failed retrieval must not become a verified citation.
- **H-012/H-022/H-008 — Generated code and runtime ownership:** Task `9011784a-5cb3-4d8d-8f17-977c483a9a99` shows repeated installation/code failures on the user's ArcGIS machine, not Berry's sandbox. Advice switches between pip and conda without verifying the active interpreter. Later component-filter code includes background label zero via `big[lbl]` without clearing `big[0]`, potentially marking background as a retained area. Validate generated code with small meaningful cases and identify the user's runtime before prescribing installation commands. Preserve classification thresholds and distinguish display issues from numeric results.
- **H-007/H-019/H-005 — Skill and document paths:** Task `a5b1958b-0d84-4662-a8ac-d15803b33626` includes a DOCX containing literal dictionary-like text despite validation, a user opening-error complaint, missing cross-package helpers/resources and a later HTTP 402 before final publication. Validate executable skill entry points and the actual deliverable; report provider unavailability without implying the artifact was delivered.
- **H-019 — Redaction verification:** Task `f1a14ffb-04b9-4207-ae33-6318e8143aed` spends approximately 55 minutes repairing a PDF and publishes after warnings about missed or collateral content. Use geometry-aware redaction helpers and verify both removal and preservation against the original; exact output quality still requires artifact inspection.
- **H-022/H-025 — Context and attachment coverage:** Task `468fe986-be21-4e55-81f1-693f5df43d28` carries hotel context into a stadium brief. Task `e35b5fc8-d694-4bc3-b5de-a6b498ddddc2` contains conflicting building descriptions and an eight-attachment request with only five unique attachment reads. Track project/source identity, surface conflicting facts, and state actual attachment coverage before claiming a complete review.

Completion refers to conversation reading, not independent verification of every fact, source or generated file. Existing memo fixes are excluded. Historical Python, network, MCP and skill incidents are not automatically reopened: some later tasks show successful recovery. Each unresolved cause/deployment check remains explicit above. No additional production changes were deployed during this review.


### H-002 deployment update — 2026-09-24

Faithful copy-editing instruction deployed to ai.aesg.com through AWS SSM on all six workers. It preserves speaker, meaning, uncertainty, facts and user corrections; tone edits cannot invent commitments or change perspective. Explicit third-person requests remain supported. Initial synthetic evaluation found two semantic failures, which prompted stronger examples. All six final synthetic cases passed manual review on the production default model; 870 worker tests and type checks passed, followed by 110 focused tests after refinement. This is limited behavioral sampling, not a guarantee across all models. Worker health and exact prompt hashes verified after restart.

Deployment and rollback details: [deployment record](output/rewrite-prompt-2026-09-24/deployment.md). This is a prompt-only hotfix over the existing production revision; local source changes remain uncommitted and must be included in future Git deployments. H-001 queue fixes were not included. Broader runtime, skill and artifact issues remain separate.

**Source alignment (14 October):** the deployed hotfix constant was still uncommitted; it is now committed unchanged (source hash `de72fe1a…f6255` matches the deployment record), so a Git deployment cannot drop it. Meaning-drift fixes remain open under H-002.

### H-031 — Blob deletion receipt inference failure

- Evidence: 834 monthly deletion outbox dead letters; at least 4,805 matching failures within the window among the latest 5,000 retained Redis failed jobs. Live Redis trimming means this is a lower bound.
- Confirmed cause: stack traces reach `acknowledgeBlobOutbox` after remote deletion; its nullable `$4` is untyped. Read-only session PREPARE reproduces the error; explicit text typing prepares successfully. EXPLAIN alone did not reproduce it and is not sufficient verification.
- Status: active current-worker defect. Audit executed no deletion or receipt mutation.
- Proposal: type the receipt parameter, test the real PostgreSQL transaction, then separately review bounded reconciliation with immutable object version, live references, tenant and lease checks.
- Acceptance: null/cancel notes, duplicate delivery, lost lease, reference race, already-missing S3 object and crash after remote deletion; durable receipt without deleting newly live content. Estimate 1–2 days plus reconciliation review.

- **Implementation (14 October):** reproduced with a local PostgreSQL 16 PREPARE; the nullable note is now typed `$4::text`. A real-PostgreSQL regression (`scripts/test-blob-receipt.mjs`) fails with the production error on the old SQL and passes now. Not deployed. Historical dead letters were not touched.

### H-032 — Prefix cache token coverage and measurement

- Evidence: 33,269 main usage events, 4,279,690,411 input tokens, 1,388,721,156 cache-read tokens: **32.45% token coverage**. DeepSeek any-hit rate is 97.84%, but token coverage is 33.30%; Qwen token coverage is 15.27%. Auxiliary vision/image usage is measured separately.
- Define the 85–90% target as warm, eligible prefix-token coverage where supported, with a separately reported all-traffic denominator. Unreported cache is unknown, not confirmed zero. Current worker cache flag is enabled.
- Causes/proposals: dynamic grounding at system-prefix start, deferred tool-catalog changes, image-tail changes, misleading eligibility/settlement denominators; hosted-provider expiry/affinity and telemetry loss still need controlled verification. Previous 39-call investigation did not prove a short TTL.
- Separate cache: vision-observation hits are 13/3,005 successful inspections (0.43%), with no focused-mode hits; unique images lower the attainable total. Evaluate content/question keys and concurrent coalescing without stale answers or cross-tenant reuse.
- Acceptance: reconcile raw provider usage per physical call, actual backend, warm/cold cohorts, prompt/tool/image hashes, TTFT/cost and task quality. Estimate 2 days instrumentation +3–5 days experiments/design; no guaranteed savings yet.

- **Implementation part 1 (14 October):** `cacheEligible` no longer ORs in observed hits; hit counts are independent of eligibility; dashboards lead with cached input-token share. Not deployed. Prefix-stability work continues below.

- **Implementation parts 2–3 (14 October):** usage events now record the router-attributed serving provider/model and long retention is judged against 24 h. Per-turn context (grounding, routed tool guidance, intent, skill guidance) moved out of the system message into a Berry-labelled turn-context message after the request; an offline replay of real request assembly raises the reusable prefix of follow-up turns' first calls from 6.6% to 69.4% (realistic synthetic session) with within-run reuse unchanged. Live provider coverage/quality evaluation is pending; MCP tool-membership changes and provider affinity remain open. Not deployed.

### H-033 — Release provenance lost in telemetry

- Evidence: 1,924 monthly runs say revision unknown. All inspected worker environments use a build revision containing `+production-hotfixes`; `sourceRevisionFromEnv` rejects `+`.
- Proposal: record base SHA, release identifier, image digest and hotfix manifest, plus prompt/skill/sandbox versions. Do not infer running code solely from `/opt/berry` HEAD.
- Acceptance: actual production identifiers survive validation; new runs link to exact release. Estimate 0.5–1 day.

Monthly provider-attempt evidence:36,154 reports cover33,532 logical groups;1,243 groups/468 runs have multiple reports and1,174 eventually succeed. This is model-call recovery, not task completion. There are1,382 repeated run/logical/ordinal keys and one exact duplicated aborted payload, so2,622 additional reports cannot be called exact physical retries. Same-route stream-to-buffer fallback is also reported as fallback. Extend H-033 with unique provider-request attempt IDs, retry-layer/transport/actual route, and reconciliation tests (1–2 additional days). Do not blindly deduplicate ordinal collisions.

- **Implementation (14 October):** the shared revision pattern now accepts `+` build metadata, so `<sha>+production-hotfixes` is recorded instead of `unknown`. Not deployed.

### H-034 — Unsupported refusal counted as completion

- Evidence: task `4b8d583a-2d56-49a9-ab5b-61a4cbb23b16` on October 7 requests PDF-to-Word conversion; after a provider error the assistant refuses because it did not author the third-party document. No file is delivered, but the run completes.
- Status: observed behavior, no policy basis established for this benign transformation. Frequency beyond this example pending full review.
- Proposal: distinguish benign transformation from prohibited requests; require artifact-existence/publication checks for file tasks and a separate user-outcome status.
- Acceptance: ordinary third-party file conversion produces the requested artifact or an accurate actionable input blocker. Include model-route regression and file-open verification. Estimate 1–2 days for initial contract/evals.

### H-035 — Rapid duplicate submissions

- Evidence: five same-user/text/attachment pairs within five seconds, including five image tasks created within thirteen seconds. Intentional resubmission versus frontend race is not established.
- Proposal: trace per-submit intent/idempotency keys, show immediate admission progress and preserve intentional reruns. No automatic merge of distinct user tasks.
- Acceptance: double-click, lost response and reconnect create one task for one intent; intentional rerun creates another. Estimate 1–2 days if UI cause confirmed.

### H-036 — Tool result omitted after shared history budget

- Evidence: deployed `boundedToolResultContext` can return an empty string once the shared tool-result budget is exhausted. A monthly production occurrence is not yet proved.
- Proposal: preserve a structured durable reference and latest relevant result; expose truncation explicitly, with source version/ACL checks. Avoid simply increasing context without measurement.
- Acceptance: threshold multi-tool histories retain retrievable decisive results and accurate answers inside provider limits. Estimate 1–2 days if reproduced.

### H-037 — Search query operators lost

- Evidence: task `05c42230-dffa-403e-bd2e-0ade0539d719` sends quoted Autodesk terms; returned query is `FOV OR` with irrelevant medical results. Matching local BerryCrawl implementation parses/removes quoted/site operators and forwards only the remaining formatted query.
- Confidence: production symptom confirmed; matching local cause high; remote BerryCrawl revision unverified.
- Proposal: preserve supported operators or reject unsupported syntax clearly; cover quoted-only, site, exclusion and escaped-term queries. Keep network/approval policy enforced.
- Acceptance: upstream query preserves requested terms/constraints and returns relevant results. Estimate 1–2 days after remote code verification.

### H-038 — Gemini tool-call stream identity

- Evidence: 12 concatenated native-tool failures / 5 tasks on Gemini; task `fe764378-e1ff-4328-9589-d5e4e48b9a7f` repeats activate_skillread four times. Other unsupported aliases are not counted as this defect.
- Cause: Google gateway parser restarts tool indices for each event; deployed worker appends ID/name/arguments by index. Local synthetic SSE reproduces the exact merged name and concatenated JSON; same calls in one event remain valid.
- Status: implementation defect and production symptom confirmed; raw historical chunks and remote gateway build not verified. No model call or production mutation made.
- Proposal: stable stream-wide call IDs/indices, protocol-aware field normalization and validation before durable dispatch; avoid guessing/splitting merged mutations.
- Acceptance: split events, parallel calls, repeats, fragments, interleaving, missing indices, signatures and ending usage fixtures through both adapters; one correct dispatch per intended call. Estimate 2–3 days.

### H-039 — Vision truncation is silently accepted

- Monthly exact diagnostics: 1,535 completed `inspect_images` steps have final `finishReason=length`, across 362 tasks and 106 users, through October 13. This is 51.30% of 2,992 completed cache-miss observations with diagnostics, not a wrong-answer rate.
- Current focused cap is 1,024 tokens. Any nonempty text is accepted and cached; cache hits lack completeness metadata. Task 977 lists only 16 of its claimed 35 employees before truncation; the count itself needs source verification. At least three same-image workflow tasks contain independently verified digit errors, which are not automatically attributed to truncation.
- Proposed fix: explicit completeness/coverage, concise structured extraction, bounded adaptive budget or targeted continuation, and cache only results whose completeness is established. Cache-format migration needs deliberate handling of older entries.
- Acceptance: long lists, cropped/blurred identifiers and forced length finishes; no silent partial success or cached partial result. Measure extraction accuracy, recovery cost and latency. See [monthly plan](output/platform-audit-2026-10-13/report.md).

- **Implementation (14 October):** a final `length` finish now returns `vision.complete: false` with an explicit incomplete-observation notice, and such observations are never cached; complete ones are cached as before. The prompt version moved to v4 so older entries of unknown completeness are not reused (no deletion; existing retention ages them out). The stable-intent focused key was kept (unobserved collision risk). Not deployed. Adaptive budget/targeted continuation still needs live measurement.

### H-040 — Memory and retrieval contamination

- Task 202 `38ab8952-7458-4c83-b3ac-cd3e2de2765b` receives a rewrite of an earlier same-user task when no source sentence was supplied. Stored grounding directly contains that earlier assistant result and a temporary training question treated as a profile memory. Frequency: one fully traced example so far; no cross-user access finding.
- Current retrieval takes nearest vector candidates without a relevance threshold and ranks by ordinal scores. Personal recall merges legacy memories with Mem0. Extraction prompts already reject temporary details, so a prompt-only restatement is inadequate. The specific memory remains readable in current Mem0 with null expiry, and the related task-outcome source remains indexed with authority0.8. Its historical snapshot shows a vector-only match. Full-month checking found zero other-user task-outcome injections.
- Additional concrete example: task973 guesses a5mm measurement, later admits it does not know; task978 retrieves memory16fd6cfb-7ae9-4fcf-b8e1-c2491d85a67b claiming the user confirmed3–8mm /5mm. Exact linked user message only says “it is few mm.” Task978 reuses the unsupported estimate as previously confirmed. Source-support and retraction handling are required alongside relevance; processor passes both roles while provenance points to the user message. Image measurement itself remains unverified. The current compiled processor bytes confirm both user and assistant text are ingested with user-message provenance. A scoped production Mem0 GET returns this exact memory with HTTP 200, confidence 0.75 and null expiry; it remains unresolved.
- Proposed fix: relevance/project gates, raw similarity telemetry, explicit source authority, missing-input edit guard, user-supported durable-memory extraction, expiry/provenance and view/correct/forget controls.
- Acceptance: irrelevant projects, generic titles, missing text, quoted content, one-off requests, explicit durable preferences and authorized recall. Preserve tenant isolation and useful recall; measure contamination and supported-memory precision.

- **Implementation (14 October):** Mem0 personal-memory inference now receives only the user's message (mem0ai 3.1.2 extracts from both roles, which produced task 978's "user confirmed 5 mm"). Explicit memories and project extraction are unchanged; existing memories are untouched. Not deployed. Retrieval relevance gates, task-outcome authority, quoted content and expiry remain.

### H-041 — Image shape and metadata contract

- 234 completed image calls / 71 tasks without explicit size override report dimensions inconsistent with the requested supported ratio. One immutable PNG independently verified: task333 image `c67cb377-71bf-46b0-83b8-4050a34bfb90` is 1536×1024 (3:2) despite 16:9 labeling. All-file pixel verification is not claimed.
- Current worker maps several distinct ratios to identical native sizes and assumes returned dimensions from the request. Shared/local paths repeat the mapping.
- Proposed fix: provider-aware shape support, actual byte/MIME/dimension/alpha inspection, requested-versus-actual metadata, explicit unsupported-ratio recovery. Do not silently crop engineering references.
- Acceptance: all advertised ratios, explicit size override, provider size mismatch, MIME mismatch, transparency and edit references; metadata must match returned bytes.
- **Implementation (14 October):** the worker's `create_image` now reads width, height, format and transparency from the returned bytes, stores the file under its real format, labels the nearest allowed preset, reports ratio/size/transparency mismatches to the model and rejects non-image data. Not deployed. Provider-aware size selection and the desktop/local-agent paths remain.

- **H-026/H-006 capability example:** task `f66812f6-432b-446f-97cc-22f2e10d3626` asks to add a calendar event. Discovery exposes only read tools, and list_events fails expired OAuth. The final says it tried to add and promises creation after reconnect. No creation call occurred. Show read/write connector capabilities and report the actual attempted operation; reconnection alone does not establish write access.

### H-042 — Transparent image content mistaken for a blank upload

- Task `f65e8afa-0be9-4bcd-923f-61a7d31aa3db` (September 29), run `1d7dca12-80ab-4cc7-9cbc-7297294facfc`: a valid 4,697×131 PNG was called uniformly black by vision. Two successful shell checks converted palette pixels to RGB, discarding transparency; the second ignored an explicit Pillow warning to use RGBA. The final answer asserted no readable content and asked the user to upload again.
- Independently downloaded immutable original is intact: SHA-256 `560661a89f84a08073475ecc9a57f0cb2871ca71e9fd38c58b1d266c4c4569d9`, 18 alpha values spanning 0–255. Compositing on white exposes three legible paragraphs. This is one source-verified task; population frequency is unknown.
- Current local image guard flattens only its JPEG branch; small PNGs can retain transparency. That guard is not deployed. Exact provider-side interpretation is unverified; the recovery's incorrect RGB-only blank test is confirmed.
- Proposal: preserve original bytes, normalize a separate model/display derivative against an explicit background, check RGBA/transparency before declaring an image blank, and teach the model to act on diagnostic warnings. Add transparent palette/grayscale/RGBA text, genuinely blank images, extreme aspect ratio, and engineering drawings to cross-model vision regressions. Approximately 1–2 days, plus provider validation; do not silently alter user artifacts.
- Acceptance: readable source is summarized accurately without reupload; alpha-aware diagnostics agree with visible content, genuinely blank sources remain correctly identified, and original files remain byte-identical.

- **Implementation (14 October):** model/vision previews of images with real transparency are flattened onto white, or onto black when the visible content is light; opaque images still pass through byte-for-byte and originals are untouched. Not deployed. Cross-model provider behaviour and the model's alpha-unaware shell checks remain unevaluated.

### H-043 — Presentation template font inheritance and publication checks

- Sixty presentation font-gate failures /36tasks /20users in the month, throughOctober13at04:48UTC;35shell steps incorrectly look successful because trailing commands/pipelines return zero. Counts include repaired work, not36failed tasks. Other Office/memo work is excluded here.
- Fully reviewed task1228 traces Arial theme→LiberationSans page-number fields despite installed Verdana. Cache refresh is ineffective; the model eventually patches theme and field runs and publishes successfully. Current local template retains Arial; generator updates field text only and normalizer changes body shapes only. Current published template and generator hashes match local files; the inheritance gap remains.
- Proposal: normalize inherited visible fonts during skill publication and smoke-render all supported layouts in the production image. Preserve meaningful font validation; distinguish structure, rendering and visual coverage. Approximately1–2days.
- Acceptance: fresh/pause/restore renders pass without manual cache refresh/XML repair; visible spans including page numbers use approved fonts. Track first-attempt render success and preparation latency.

### H-044 — Email copy discoverability and accurate failure feedback

- Task888 `82876273-9fba-4e62-ad5c-77781000347e`: user reports black background in pasted email, rejects extra steps, and remains confused after three follow-ups. Berry assumes email client/copy path and promises fixes without confirmation. Frequency one reviewed task; source clipboard contents/root cause not verified.
- Current writing-block button already writes plain text and has a fallback; do not duplicate it. Assistant-message Copy separately shows success before the clipboard promise resolves, a local code gap with no verified production occurrence.
- Proposal: clearer existing copy control/guidance, awaited success/error feedback everywhere, and supported-email-client reproduction of selection/button copy before deciding on rich-copy normalization. Approximately0.5–1day plus client checks.
- Acceptance: light/dark mode paste remains free of display background through documented control; denied clipboard access shows an actionable failure rather than Copied.

### Additional verified artifact and preparation evidence — task710

Estimate3–5days with artifact gates; test unknown-field rejection and narration survival.
- **H-007/H-008:** same task encounters missing sibling helpers and ffmpeg/video libraries, then policy-denied installations before GIF fallback/HTTP413. Later user changes to pictures/flowchart are intentional.

Verified source images and Decimal calculation give proposed PCI0.550631 and improvement11.7416% against the computed target. Auxiliary vision transcribes inputs correctly but asserts a false arithmetic consistency. Test quantity identity/units and numerator meaning alongside arithmetic; partly correct final output is not fully correct completion.

Add optional native draft length validation and define counting semantics; avoid sandbox startup/repeated candidate loops for this pure text check (approximately 1 day). Preserve content constraints and test Unicode/placeholders.

### Monthly audit: unsupported legal assurance and upload repair loops

- **H-017/H-025, confirmed unsupported assurance:** task 884 (`caa03993-d50f-429d-9e98-a882fce36fcc`, October 6) completes four Gemini runs without tools. The user explicitly says the proposed harm descriptors deviate from BS 9792:2025. Berry nevertheless calls the wording legally ready and says it limits liability, and suggests categorical exclusions for accelerated arson and other exceptional conditions. No standard or legal source was checked. England's [Article 9](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2005/1541/article/9) requires a suitable and sufficient assessment; [Home Office guidance](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/fire-safety-risk-assessment-sleeping-accommodation/fire-safety-risk-assessment-sleeping-accommodation-accessible) expressly considers deliberate ignition and securing flammable liquids. This does not adjudicate the specific premises or the inaccessible BS clause. Confirmed defect is unsupported assurance and source-free hazard exclusion. Add jurisdiction/version/source checks, preserve declared deviations, and separate language quality from legal validity. No targeted deployed fix verified; approximately 2–4 days for prompt/tool routing and representative evaluations. Test that polishing preserves uncertainty and does not invent liability protection, clause compliance or blanket exclusions.
- **H-014/H-024/H-008, confirmed historical recovery loop:** task 78 (`38a8c049-38b7-4a60-b589-ee07402f2d5d`, September 14) has two 75-second verification failures, followed by 18 calls that repeatedly probe the same inaccessible inputs and filesystem. Reupload produces another wait failure; final advice suggests ZIP behaves differently without evidence. No requested methodology is delivered. This belongs to the existing 25-run verification cohort and does not establish a current backlog or a new generic timeout defect. The deployed receipt-to-composer state gap remains confirmed. Preserve attachment IDs in a verifying/resume state, classify resource readiness before model admission, and cap no-progress recovery across different tools. Extend the existing 2–3 day proposal with delayed verification, original-file reuse and no duplicate admission tests; success means recovery without repeated searching or reupload.

### Monthly audit: constrained editing and actionable recovery
