It also narrows heavier glazing from helipad-facing façades to the western façade alone and drops pending-assessment caveats. Source text and all follow-ups are read; underlying acoustic design is not adjudicated. Extend constrained-edit evaluations to quantities, spatial scope, recommendations and provisional status. Offer bounded native word/character counting or a deterministic final-output check; define the count convention. Existing prompt deployment does not establish adherence. Approximately 1–2 days for constraint checks plus cross-model evaluation; measure exact-limit pass rate and factual preservation without a Python repair loop.
- **H-002/H-022, historical examples for regression tests:** task 244 (`ae58a2fd-c5e8-4175-88a9-ed29df0f0f1b`) requires repeated corrections to preserve two separately addressed sections, then still repeats information while claiming no duplication. Task 169 (`062992b7-feb9-4f92-acab-ccc5ee5fbf00`) rewrites a whole letter for a paragraph edit and drops the original three-day deadline extension from the body. Both precede the September 24 patch; replay them before claiming current recurrence or recommending the already deployed rule again. Preserve recipients, unaffected spans and body/subject consistency.
- **H-003/H-014, observed recovery friction:** task 1048 (`b7f61da5-c8e6-4896-94b0-120a091e48dc`, October 8) delivers the first request, then three attempts fail HTTP 402. The user pastes the error and asks how to fix it; the same provider-configuration message returns. Add role-appropriate provider-status guidance, an administrator contact/control and retained-input retry. This is already counted in the historical provider cohort; no current 402 outage is claimed. Do not ask ordinary users to repair credentials they cannot access. Test help/retry under provider rejection without invoking the blocked model again.
- **H-013, improvement hypothesis:** task 291 (`926d01df-51f8-4b01-a419-d3f9a51d7708`) successfully delivers an unchanged published Excel template in approximately 38 seconds using six tool calls. A permission-checked template retrieval control could avoid model drafting, extra skill loading and sandbox copying for this simple request. Approximately 1–2 days if the existing asset delivery path can be reused; validate published version/hash, organization access and tenant isolation, then measure latency and call reduction. Existing delivery is functional.

### Monthly audit: distinguish missing requirements from confirmed exemptions

- **H-025/H-020, confirmed source-status error:** task 562 (`d106d007-9d77-4381-a52b-7b055a3d623f`, September 28) claims its RFP review is strictly based on the three supplied files, yet turns unstated bid/performance bonds and site visits into not required. It also declares separate proposal packages unnecessary, then recommends asking whether separate packages are required. The retained full source extracts do not support those exemptions. Task 248 contains the same class of conversion. Distinguish stated requirements, absent information, confirmed non-applicability and inference; preserve scope and source references. No targeted deployed fix verified. Approximately 2–3 days for structured extraction/prompt rules and evaluation; test omitted clauses, explicit exemptions, conflicting pages and partial extraction. Benefit: fewer unsupported commercial commitments; tradeoff: visible clarification items rather than assumed answers.
- **H-040, current status checked:** the specific unsupported 3–8 mm/5 mm memory used in task 978 is still returned by scoped production Mem0 GET, with the original user-message provenance and null expiry. Current compiled memory processor confirms the implicated two-role ingestion behavior. Current storage and implementation parity are now verified; no memory record was changed during this audit.

### H-045 — Generated reusable skills need independent domain validation

- **Confirmed task/current evidence:** task 756 (`176b1bdd-7906-4bea-9413-f1d2ea08aa29`, October 2) installs an enabled Singapore redundancy calculator after a self-test with no source lookup. Its rule that compensation in lieu of notice attracts CPF contradicts [CPF Board guidance](https://www.cpf.gov.sg/service/article/are-cpf-contributions-payable-on-compensation-in-lieu-of-notice). Its five-retrenchments-in-six-months notification trigger contradicts [MOM's current trigger and deadline](https://www.mom.gov.sg/employment-practices/retrenchment/mandatory-retrenchment-notifications). These rules were already in force before the task. Current production Markdown and calculator hashes exactly match the authored package; it remains enabled. One traced installed skill, no inferred population rate or downstream payout incident.
- **Likely cause/confidence:** high confidence that self-tests check the authored formula rather than independently established requirements. Current save handler validates package/frontmatter and writes enabled/trusted with empty diagnostics; execution trust is not domain validation. [Save implementation](apps/worker/src/personal-skills/tools.ts:112). Current compiled persistence path checked. Other tax rules, salary formulas and real employee outcomes have not been fully adjudicated.
- **Proposed fix:** source-backed creation for consequential calculators, explicit jurisdiction/date/assumptions, independent expected-output fixtures with correct units, and separate package-valid/execution-trusted/domain-verified statuses. Review the identified skill and version any correction after implementation approval. Do not treat a passing generated self-test as legal accuracy. Approximately 3–5 days for initial workflow, metadata and evaluation; domain review effort varies.
- **Benefit/tradeoff:** prevent unsupported rules becoming durable instructions used in future tasks; source checks add preparation time and domain verification may need expert review. Keep low-impact personal skills lightweight.
- **Acceptance:** correct CPF distinction between actual wages during notice and compensation in lieu; employer headcount/notification trigger and deadline tested against primary sources; calendar versus working-day salary bases independently checked; contract/guidance/statute distinguished. Track verified-rule coverage and downstream calculation accuracy. No skill or production record changed in this audit.

### Monthly audit: template retrieval and validation contracts

- **H-013/H-019/H-025, confirmed current validation defect:** task 910 (`22cc6153-1915-4cd6-bb3c-ddcc44980cd7`, October 6) takes approximately 85 seconds to supply an existing PowerPoint template. It probes a virtual skill path after the staging result gave the real path, reads and reruns compaction even though the source already has the required structure, and runs validation intended for finished content. The validator rejects intended specimen placeholders and raises `AttributeError: no .rgb property on color type '_SchemeColor'`. Berry dismisses the exception with the expected placeholder warnings and publishes after package/structure checks; no rendering was performed. Its final description labels 10.833 × 7.5 inches as 16:9; the recorded dimensions are 13:9. One fully traced task and local reproduction, not a population frequency claim.
- **Cause/current status:** current published template and validator hashes match the local files. Read-only reproduction with python-pptx 1.0.2 confirms the same exception. [Colour access](deploy/skills/aesg-branding/scripts/validate_artifact.py:46) handles `ValueError` but theme colour access raises `AttributeError`; there is no template validation mode. High confidence in this current package defect. The template's native size itself is not being proposed for change.
- **Recommendation:** separate template, finished-content, structural, rendering and visual checks; resolve theme/inherited colours without throwing and report unresolved colour validation explicitly. Derive ratio labels from dimensions. Offer permission-checked direct retrieval of the approved source template, preserving its version and bytes, for requests that need no content generation. Approximately 1–2 days for validation changes plus retrieval work if the current delivery path is reusable. Benefit: fewer false failures and misleading verification claims, lower preparation latency; tradeoff: maintaining distinct gate contracts.
- **Acceptance:** untouched approved template passes template integrity checks while deliberately incomplete final decks still fail; theme colours do not crash; ZIP reopening is never reported as rendering; native aspect ratio is labelled accurately. Track model/tool calls and delivery latency against this 85-second trace and task 291's 38-second Excel retrieval, with organization access and tenant isolation checks.

### Monthly audit: bounded vision and preservation claims

- **H-039/H-008/H-013:** task 425 (`9c2ac3e8-0d5e-4fe7-be91-b2edb639139a`, September 22) takes five minutes to search an 11-page handwritten scan. Two full OCR passes are unreliable; three vision calls fail because shell-created `/tmp` images are outside the tool's admitted paths. Moving images into the workspace resolves those path failures. A five-page vision call still returns empty after two attempts; smaller batches recover, but several accepted observations have `finishReason=length`. The final whole-document absence claim exceeds the completeness evidence. Actual source pages were not independently adjudicated. This adds a concrete successful-but-expensive recovery trace to H-039, not a general model timeout complaint. Define one workspace temporary-path contract, report page-level completeness and adapt batch/output bounds after a truncated response. Test degraded handwriting, no-match grep exits and partial/empty vision without treating normal search no-match as a broken OCR operation.
- **H-019/H-025/H-029:** task 93 (`f436cb3e-e1e2-4c38-a66c-f553fe901464`, September 14) delivers all five requested upscales and correctly warns that thumbnails cannot yield true new detail. It nevertheless claims pixels remain untouched and JPEG quality 95 has no colour/detail loss, although the recorded pipeline resamples, sharpens and saves a lossy format. Final scene labels also swap Picture3/Picture5 relative to the path-ordered observation; actual scenes were not independently checked. Describe deterministic processing and semantic preservation with its actual limits; carry file IDs through batched observations. No specific current corrective deployment verified. Add this example to existing claim/association evaluation rather than treating successful delivery as a failure.

### H-046 — Image reads succeed without reading; stale image paths abort later model requests

- **Confirmed production example:** task 1103 (`53647734-9f9a-4b79-baa7-e976602a0b90`, October 9), run `7bb58cd3-0fde-4722-81e6-7e0feb7962fe`, creates and reads a 1920 × 1080 temporary image, then generates and publishes a replacement. A cleanup command deletes the temporary image. The next model preparation reopens its historical `visionPath` and fails in 0.77 seconds; the task ends failed despite the replacement already being delivered. This is an observed control-flow defect, distinct from the user's requested change.
- **Frequency (expanded scan):** 27 monthly runs end with a missing image-path error; 26 runs across 21 tasks and 11 users match the missing path to a completed same-run image read or generation result. Eighteen match `read`, eight match `create_image`, with no overlap. Only three run histories in this cohort are fully reviewed and causally traced here (1103 deletion, 774 move, 956 deletion); the other 23 require end-to-end attribution. A completed image `read` does not prove existence, and this scan does not prove all 26 share the cleanup cause. The earlier 18-run/15-task/9-user read-only symptom denominator remains valid as its narrower subset.
- **Second traced example:** task 774 (`4c993d15-0b60-44e7-8bab-4c392bd04fca`, October 2), run `e0e8adb4-d47b-48f5-91a6-f534e83184cc`, generates a portrait, composites the approved logo and checks the final file. Cleanup moves the generated intermediate image to `tmp`; the next model preparation fails on its former generation `visionPath` in 0.21 seconds. The initial image was published, and the branded file exists in the sandbox, but no explicit final persistence or final answer is recorded before failure. Final branded publication or permanent file loss is not established. Its actual Pillow dimensions also confirm 1536×1024 (3:2) while generation metadata says 4:3 (H-041).
- **Third traced example:** task 956 (`810f68f6-49e2-43e5-b774-05a7e60a22ab`, October 7), run `5add6336-6bcd-49fb-9aa3-0646b94ac3f1`, generates and publishes a replacement diagram, then removes the first generated image. The next model preparation fails in 0.20 seconds on that deleted generation path. Later user turns recover through further edits. Publication preserves the images, but the cleanup still leaves the run failed. Add same-path overwrite/version binding to the immutable-image-reference tests; later generations reuse a local filename with distinct persisted file IDs, with no confirmed incorrect-version answer here.
- **Cause/current status/confidence:** high confidence in the traced failure and current implementation gap. [Binary read branch](apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:939) returns success and a vision path based on extension without reading the bytes. [Image selection](apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:2057) retains past read/generation paths, and [model preparation](apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:608) uses uncaught `Promise.all` reads. The inspected deployed worker (`sandbox-continuity.js` SHA256 `0978f3bb5e56366b4fbde40a3211a76ac241b74a94d8c9bae72729c3a7e80759`) contains the same behavior. Current local image size guards also do not fix path lifetime. No production change applied.
- **Proposal/benefit:** validate image existence/content at the requesting tool boundary; keep bounded, scoped immutable inspection content or a durable source reference. Make implicit stale visual context an explicit omission/recoverable preparation result instead of aborting the whole turn. Explicit inspection must still report unavailable evidence and must never claim a successful visual check. Use scoped artifact IDs to recover published sources where possible; do not repeatedly depend on temporary file names. Approximately 2–3 days including regressions. Benefit: model can repair a wrong path, cleanup cannot retroactively break a completed inspection, and delivered work retains an accurate state. Tradeoff: extra bounded I/O/storage and attachment-lifetime rules.
- **Acceptance:** missing initial image produces a repairable tool result; read → rename/delete → next model turn preserves acquired inspection content or clearly reports omission without a terminal crash; published replacements remain accessible; explicit unavailable inspection is not silently skipped. Include cancellation/resume, sandbox replacement, large-image limits, task-scoped caching and tenant isolation. Measure image-preparation failure rate, repeated reads and latency separately from genuine unavailable user inputs.

- **Implementation (14 October, with H-060/H-071):** image reads now confirm the file exists; historical images that were moved/deleted become explicit omission notices instead of failing model preparation; explicit inspection of a missing path still errors. Regression tests fail on the old source. Not deployed.

### Monthly audit: technical troubleshooting needs claim-level sources

- **H-017/H-025, confirmed unsupported troubleshooting rule:** task 717 (`536eb7dc-5070-4773-981e-deee253cca3b`, October 1) finds and opens the official IESVE FAQ, but then states that mixing boxes cannot accept temperature control and tells the user to change to fraction control. The linked [IESVE controller table and explanation](https://help.iesve.com/ve2019/general_settings_1.htm) explicitly allow dry-bulb temperature, percentage flow and enthalpy for a mixing damper set. The actual user's module type/network/version remain unknown, so this audit does not prescribe the exact node-7 fix. The categorical restriction and citation support are wrong; the general FAQ only says to inspect the log and permitted variables. Read the relevant table before recommending a configuration change, distinguish diagnosis from possibilities, and request the full log when the screenshot omits decisive detail. Add this case to H-025's source-supported diagnostic evaluation; no targeted current fix verified. Measure claim/source agreement and resolution without unnecessary rewiring.
- **H-025/H-037, unresolved technical recommendation hypothesis:** task 516 (`3cc78aa4-f132-44b4-be6d-38b362dc9618`, September 25) recommends EIFS serviceability Class 3 and assures the user that separate test requirements do not conflict. Its search loses exact terms and mostly returns irrelevant spring standards; no applicable technical source is opened. The final relative-strictness claim does not establish equivalence between the different test protocols. The exact project class and licensed standard provisions have not been adjudicated. Keep this as unsupported assurance and a research-routing case, rather than marking Class 3 itself wrong.
- **H-002/H-020, template facts need preservation:** task 1184 (`01d02698-1c39-4165-b681-f4ec64dde900`, October 12) copies a colleague's job title into the user's signature while adopting that colleague's email format. The supplied source gives the user's name but does not establish that role. Preserve style separately from identity and factual content; do not silently transfer template-specific roles or commitments. Actual user role was not independently established.
- **H-002/H-023, historical regression case:** task 421 (`db0c8f8b-0967-49e4-a579-ab1afb17b41d`, September 22–23) changes a proposal invitation into an appointment, requiring the user to correct it, and describes a September 16 site visit as scheduled after that date. This predates the September 24 prompt patch. Replay it across supported models before claiming current recurrence; third-person wording was explicitly requested and must not be classified as a voice-preservation error.

### Monthly audit: tool completion and application outcomes

- **H-003/H-006/H-017, measured result-contract gap:** the monthly archive contains 2,266 canonically named BerryCrawl steps, including 2,209 completed, 53 failed, three cancelled and one recovery-required. Parsing retained fenced JSON finds 222 explicit `data.success=false` payloads across 60 tasks and 43 users, all in completed steps. This is a lower-bound negative-result measure: 1,893 payloads explicitly report true, 20 omit the flag, 104 envelopes are not parsed and 27 JSON payloads do not parse. Of completed steps, 74 remain unclassified by this parser. The one malformed non-MCP alias is outside this named-tool denominator.
- **Observed categories:** 70 report no usable extracted text, 28 are 404, five report no configured stealth proxy, and tunnel/anti-bot/deadline errors appear separately. Seventy-two negative results report HTTP 200, confirming that HTTP or tool completion alone does not establish useful extraction. These are result symptoms, not 222 confirmed harness defects or equivalent task failures. Each task still needs recovery/outcome review. Task 1053 (`4fc56dca-4aa5-41bc-9a5b-014590eafc20`, October 8) nevertheless cites the GSA source after its failed scrape.
- **Cause/status:** current MCP worker emits a generic returned-results summary; the local-agent wrapper relies on MCP `isError` and preserves application JSON. The inspected compiled worker has the same path. The remote BerryCrawl release and whether individual historical network/proxy problems are fixed have not been established. [MCP result handling](apps/worker/src/mcp-tools.ts:162), [MCP error handling](packages/local-agent/src/mcp.ts:413). High confidence in the measurement/contract distinction; individual causes require server evidence.
- **Recommendation/tests:** normalize connector-specific outcome metadata (`usable`, `empty`, `not_found`, `blocked`, `auth`, `transient`, `unknown`) separately from execution state and HTTP status, with appropriate model/user guidance. Preserve original result and uncertainty; do not infer platform failure from arbitrary business-data boolean fields. Approximately 1–2 days for initial telemetry and BerryCrawl contract integration; UI recovery work belongs to H-014. Test HTTP 200 with no text, unavailable URL, auth, transient network, genuinely empty search, valid content and unknown schemas. Report usable-result rate and recovery latency with explicit denominators; no blind retry of unchanged blocked input or blanket reclassification of completed tasks.

### H-047 — Arabic language preference and bidirectional presentation

- **Evidence:** fully reviewed task 847 (`07359513-063b-4fa2-ad8d-19e92b348bbb`, October 5). The user says that mixing Arabic and English makes the response wrong and asks for all Arabic; the next response changes language choice and transliterates product names. One confirmed user complaint in the reviewed subset; no population frequency estimate or browser rendering defect established.
- **Cause/status/confidence:** the current local shared Markdown renderer supplies no direction attribute to either its streaming surface or settled container, and uses left-specific list, quote and table styling. The writing-block editor/display also lacks an explicit direction policy. This is a plausible presentation contributor, not proof of the complaint's cause or current production parity. Model language choice and the user's preference are separate from text direction. See `packages/desktop-ui/src/components/berry-markdown.tsx:178` and `packages/desktop-ui/src/components/writing-block.tsx`.
- **Proposal:** retain explicit language preference in the task, and assess paragraph-level automatic direction with isolated English terms, URLs and numbers; preserve original technical identifiers where needed. Match streaming, settled output, editing and copying. Prefer logical spacing/alignment where direction applies. No need for a disruptive error modal. Approximately 1–2 days after browser reproduction and deployment comparison. Tradeoff: automatic direction must not reorder code, tables or copied identifiers.
- **Verification:** replay the actual mixed-language complaint; visually inspect Arabic-first and English-first paragraphs, bullets, tables, dates, negative numbers, URLs, code and copy/paste. Include streaming-to-settled transition, selection, keyboard editing and screen-reader language. Record whether the issue is model language adherence, layout or both before declaring a fix.

### H-030 monthly and deployed revalidation — asynchronous extraction

- **Confirmed monthly recurrence:** task 917 (`e2fa2f72-2ab0-4d43-8135-f927829ca593`), run `33dd5243-b5ec-4b8f-9a83-7d3cc1b86e80`, October 6. A truncated shared-chat scrape leads to an extraction job. Job reads return identical RUNNING results; a third poll is suppressed and a subsequent poll terminates the run as `duplicate_tool_call_loop`. Four intervening sleeps consume about 170 seconds. Changing output format temporarily evades the signature without advancing the job. The user receives no final result/recoverable job handoff. The extraction service's eventual outcome is unverified.
- **Current implementation:** inspected deployed `turn-runner.js` lines 3925–4013 matches the local `assessRepeatedToolCall` path: `compare_result` suppresses two identical completed results and terminates after a suppression. Current `durableMcpToolPolicy` assigns `compare_result` to trusted read-only MCP calls. `bounded_polling` already exists elsewhere in the harness, so reuse and enforce that contract for an explicit asynchronous status-tool class rather than adding a blanket read-only exemption. See `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:4828`, `apps/worker/src/mcp-tools.ts:573`.
- **Fix/measurement:** roughly 1–2 days for narrowly classified status tools, monotonic job-state progress, bounded backoff, cancellation and a preserved job reference/recovery action. Test RUNNING repeated before COMPLETED, perpetually RUNNING, job failed, cancellation, restart and output-format changes; verify no duplicate side effects and no unlimited polling. Measure false loop terminations and usable job completions/recovery time, with task 917 as a current regression fixture.
- **Implementation (14 October):** repeats whose previous result reports a non-terminal job state are allowed as polling and bounded by the existing no-progress budget instead of being suppressed after two identical results. Task-917 regression fails on the old code. Not deployed.
- **Separate failure:** its preceding missing-link reply is delivered but finalization fails with `session_entries_run_id_fkey` (H-001). Visible assistant text alone is not evidence of a successfully finalized run. Guidance that Drive content requires public sharing also needs a connector-aware alternative (H-026).

### Monthly audit: repairs must identify the actual failed precondition

- **H-004/H-013/H-016:** task 538 (`d3035183-09c0-4026-a6aa-5d756157f49f`, September 25) eventually delivers the correct 58-page merge, but its `qpdf --empty` command lacks `--pages`. Distinguish executed, skipped and warning-complete stages, and check tool syntax before changing inputs or blaming versions.
- **H-008/H-019:** rendering under `/tmp` causes a predictable vision path rejection; copying to workspace recovers. Three sample pages are checked successfully, not all 58 pages. The final deliverable exists, so do not label this a failed merge. The run takes about 145 seconds versus about 85 seconds for task 541's successful similar workflow; this is an illustrative comparison, not a controlled speed measurement.

### Monthly audit: explain enabled models through platform facts

The user then successfully switches to MiniMax, which asserts it is a stronger reasoning upgrade without a task benchmark or cited comparative evidence. This trace preserves conversation context through the switch, a useful positive regression fixture. Personal-profile details are not judged fabricated because loaded grounding for this task was not checked.

### Monthly audit: contractor obligations must not become consultant commitments

Preserve actor and engagement boundaries when transforming procurement text; mark unagreed services as optional rather than committing the consultant to every contractor obligation. One confirmed correction, no overall frequency extrapolation. Replay the original request and correction as paired tests; approximately 0.5–1 day of prompt/evaluation work within the faithful-editing programme. ### Monthly audit: standard output directories and comparative image checks

### Monthly audit: short drafting requests and human-wait measurement

- **H-022/H-020, historical fixture:** task 198 (`584ae9c0-783d-4ed8-ac21-da1f8e0290b8`, September 16) asks for 1–2 short paragraphs but receives repeated alternative versions, notes and further questions. ### Monthly audit: a mistyped attachment ID is not a staging outage

- **H-024/H-004/H-013, confirmed reference error:** task 523 (`ad8eeff9-c444-4b79-9731-ab6e15854ec3`, September 25) changes two characters in the second CV's admitted file ID and repeatedly requests that wrong path, even after a directory listing gives the correct ID. Five failed calls and a whole-filesystem search/copy workaround precede success; the assistant incorrectly describes a staging defect. The actual file is present under the admitted ID.
- **Current cause/status:** the inspected deployed and local `stageInputFiles` branch emits only an unavailable-ID list when a reference does not match task inputs. See `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:1894`. It correctly refuses the unknown reference; current error text does not distinguish an invalid reference from a real staging/verification failure or offer the authorized canonical handle. High confidence in this task's cause.
- **Proposal/tests:** expose stable admitted file handles and return a scoped invalid-reference hint with the exact available path when the intended basename is unique within this task. Never resolve fuzzy IDs against another user's files or relax access checks. Approximately 1–2 days for initial handle/error integration. Test a two-character ID typo, same-name attachments, genuinely missing source, expired verification, unauthorized ID and duplicate names across tenants; measure repeated reference failures and repair calls. The expected user action is to choose/correct the file, not reconnect or ask an administrator to fix a nonexistent outage.

### Monthly audit: image dimensions must control the finishing pipeline

- **H-041/H-029, confirmed geometry change:** task 906 (`d09b294d-c07e-45af-8278-fae3fc817966`, October 6) generates two images labelled 16:9, while both actual Pillow checks return 1536×1024 (3:2). The model still calls them 16:9, then directly resizes the chosen image to 2560×1440. This increases horizontal scale relative to vertical by 18.52%; it is a stretch, not a proportional upscale. The final file is successfully published. Source-to-output architectural fidelity is not independently adjudicated, but the recorded geometric transformation is certain.
- **H-019/H-039/H-013:** four explicit vision calls take 94.72 seconds in approximately 272 seconds. One requested checklist truncates; signage is never confirmed against the source, yet the final response claims faithful lettering and exact architectural preservation. A later single-image observation is treated as proof that an apparent adjacent structure is the same building. These assurances exceed the evidence available from the recorded checks.
- **Recommendation/status/test:** within H-041's existing image contract work, use decoded dimensions, validate provider ratio support, and choose explicit proportional crop or padding where a requested canvas differs; ask only when that choice would remove important content. Avoid unrequested stretching. Add this task as a 3:2-to-16:9 regression and verify geometry, output metadata, required signage and claim/check correspondence. Approximately 1–2 days for a bounded finishing helper plus evaluation. The current deployed ratio mismatch is already established under H-041; no targeted fix is verified. Extra image checks should be comparative and limited to decisive questions, with latency measured rather than presumed improved.

### Monthly audit: a deterministic check must override a vision or model label

Unlike task 774, retaining the generated intermediate avoids the stale-path preparation failure. ### Monthly audit: current provider failures, faithful editing and pure-text latency

- **H-002/H-025, new October evidence:** task 758 (`6b8bd16c-11a4-48a5-b69a-44c9d0c7ef44`, all 18 runs reviewed) contains several accurate professional rewrites preserving first-person wording, numerical details and “may start” qualifications. It also creates an IT email claiming the user tested power outlets/cables and has a nearly depleted battery, although the user only reports a charging problem. A later certificate-transmittal draft asserts document contents and standards conformity without seeing the document; the commentary incorrectly calls those assertions placeholders. Add these to faithful-drafting tests alongside the positive examples. Polishing and useful structure should not invent completed troubleshooting or verified technical scope.
- **H-014/H-033/H-038, unresolved current symptom:** Gemini requests in task 758 fail with HTTP 400 on October 7 and October 13, followed by successful DeepSeek retries of the same editing/drafting request. The October 13 failure is run `f809bbdd-8131-4c7c-bfca-693b7ccc6f2d`; successful retry is `dda92d9c-9c9f-4768-abc2-d7fa9479dda7`. The retained attempt events confirm terminal 4xx failures in 1.204 and 1.398 seconds and omit numeric status, provider code and request ID. A subsequent read of the model-step `providerDiagnostics` confirms HTTP 400 with `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (1.218 and 1.414 seconds); numeric status and code are therefore retained at step level. The sanitized upstream error body, failing field and provider request ID are still unavailable in this captured evidence. User-facing guidance only says to check model/provider configuration. This is a confirmed current route-specific failure/recovery pattern, not a proven request-schema or thought-signature cause. The older October 6 HTTP 402 incidents remain separately classified. Preserve a redacted diagnostic code/request ID and valid recovery action; do not blindly retry permanent errors or claim the user can edit administrator configuration.
- **H-013/cache optimization fixture:** task 758's first and third Gemini editing runs make two model calls each around a nearly instantaneous writing-block update. Four recorded provider calls take 220.09 seconds with 93,764 input tokens and zero reported cached input tokens. One post-draft confirmation alone takes 91.355 seconds and reports 56 output tokens. This identifies an expensive extra request after a usable draft, without proving why the provider is slow or why cache misses occur. Evaluate a narrowly defined terminal writing-block completion contract and lower-cost/effort editing route where supported. Preserve multi-step drafts, validation and requested follow-ups; no automatic quality downgrade. Measure time to usable draft separately from final run completion, request count, provider token accounting, eligible-prefix token coverage and voice/fact preservation. Approximately 1–2 days for a prototype/controlled evaluation.
- **State/outcome controls:** its October 2 single-paragraph draft is visible before H-001 finalization fails; its October 8 spreadsheet request is cancelled before any tool executes. Neither should be counted as a destroyed draft or a completed spreadsheet. The correction in task 737 (`956fcfa9-b8f4-4050-8ccb-a0e13a8bc4c5`, October 1) also shows the need to enforce a short requested paragraph format before generating long phase lists.

### Monthly audit: deployed workspace initialization guarantee

- **H-008, confirmed current configuration/code gap:** a read-only AWS SSM query shows production uses E2B template `9zmlt4zyd37kl3ie84h2` and `BERRY_SANDBOX_CWD=/home/user/workspace`. The inspected deployed E2B provider (SHA256 `85f74665b4ce1a84bb410d658391780a6ce290325d3123bfa3870c320f60bded`) creates only the configured root directory; the continuity manager restores files but does not establish standard child directories. The local template prepares `/workspace/outputs`, `inputs`, `tmp` and other folders, and its smoke test uses that different root. Current template build identity/contents were not independently inspected, so do not claim that every current new sandbox is missing outputs. The recent task 964 and older task 249 nevertheless confirm actual missing-output-directory repairs.
- **Proposal:** define and idempotently prepare the required directories under the effective worker root when compute is first created/restored, with a versioned capability result; make the template smoke test exercise the actual configured root. Avoid a new sandbox or expensive full probe on every tool call. Reuse the existing ensure-sandbox machinery rather than adding a second lifecycle system. Approximately 0.5–1 day for directory-contract work; dependency readiness and Matplotlib prewarming require separate evidence.
- **Acceptance/tradeoffs:** create, reconnect, archive restore and alternate root all allow a first plot/image/document save without a model-issued mkdir; existing files survive initialization; permissions and task/tenant boundaries remain intact. Measure first-tool success and preparation latency. The checked-in template already warms fontconfig with `fc-cache`; that is not evidence of a warmed per-user Matplotlib cache. Cold/warm timing and current image parity remain outstanding before recommending prewarming.

### H-048 — Provider-error placeholders become model history and can masquerade as completed answers

- **Confirmed example/impact:** task 464 (`56b7b3d3-c564-4e6b-a9f5-19ceee5aba8d`, September 23–24) has eight Gemini HTTP 400 failures around a professional-registration fees question. After switching to MiniMax, run `9131bfca-f8eb-4290-97fe-c38175bbd320` reports a successful 2xx model call with 26,884 input tokens, 24 output tokens and a stop finish, but its entire answer is the earlier provider-configuration error. The run is marked completed and the actual question remains unanswered. The user asks whether the servers are down and retries the following day.
- **Frequency:** an exact-text scan of complete assistant messages in the fixed month finds one matching message in one task, this fully reviewed case. This narrow detector is not a general semantic-completion rate and does not establish how often other error paraphrases or incomplete answers occur.
- **Cause/current status/confidence:** current [history construction](apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:5583) forwards assistant text without preserving/checking `stopReason=error`; the provider adapter subsequently constructs an ordinary successful assistant-history message. The inspected deployed function has the same behavior. A deterministic offline fixture reproduces forwarding a failed provider placeholder as normal assistant text. High confidence in the history-contract gap and false completion; the precise frozen MiniMax request and underlying Gemini 400 cause remain unverified. Do not classify the successful MiniMax request as another HTTP 400 failure.
- **Proposal/effort/benefit:** keep typed platform diagnostics in runtime/UI events and a concise recovery state, outside ordinary answer history. Preserve genuinely useful partial assistant text, completed tool work and branch boundaries. For explicit retry/model-switch flows, retain the original user request and pending outcome. Assess whether a response that merely repeats that typed platform placeholder needs a recoverable “no answer” state. Approximately 1–2 days for context conversion and outcome regressions. Benefit: users do not receive historical infrastructure errors as fresh answers, and completion metrics remain meaningful.
- **Tradeoffs/tests:** avoid keyword-only suppression because users may ask to quote or rewrite an error message. Test provider rejection → model switch → substantive answer; repeated retry; partial valid response plus error; tool results/attachments preserved; alternate branches; and an explicit request to reproduce the same error text. Measure completed runs with diagnostic-only answers, successful recovery and answer-to-latest-request agreement.
- **Related source-verification fixture (H-017/H-025):** the earlier registration guidance gives unverified eligibility/dual-registration assurances before looking up sources. [Engineering Council's FAQ](https://www.engc.org.uk/our-role-as-regulator/faqs/professional-registration) distinguishes separate-institution dual registration from upgrading through one institution; it does not explicitly adjudicate the particular full-EngTech plus interim-IEng combination. [IFE's EngTech page](https://www.ife.org.uk/Home/Membership/Professional-registration/Engineering-Council-registration/Engineering-Technician) describes competence assessment. Verify the exact institutional rule before advising an application or fee decision; do not turn this audit into an unsupported definitive eligibility ruling.

- **Implementation (14 October):** reproduced end to end with real PostgreSQL. Error parts are now projected as typed diagnostics, and failed projections already in the journal are excluded from model history structurally (projection source + stopReason), not by keyword. Partial worker output, tool results and user text are unchanged. Not deployed.

### Monthly audit: drafting instructions and private rationale need separate treatment

- **H-002/H-020/H-022, confirmed repeated correction:** task 862 (`50b82e27-a527-4dc6-b90a-9709ebd47697`, October 6, all 13 runs reviewed) asks for a polite reply confirming uploaded files without saying remedial records are absent, explaining that the user would not recognize those records. The draft includes both the absence claim and the explanatory uncertainty. After the user says not to include that explanation, one variant strengthens the claim to definite absence. A third, newly supplied source sentence finally receives the requested “doesn't seem to include” qualification.
- **Proposed amendment/evaluation:** distinguish text to polish from instructions and background rationale; preserve prior prohibitions across follow-up edits. Removing a caveat must not silently convert an uncertain fact into certainty. Add paired tests for “use this reason to guide tone, but don't include it,” removing an explanation while retaining qualified wording, and first-person replies to a third-party message. Expected benefit: fewer correction rounds and fewer unintended client assertions. This belongs to the existing faithful-editing work, approximately 0.5–1 day for examples and cross-model evaluation; current prompt is deployed but this October recurrence shows adherence is unresolved.
- **Additional scope/length evidence:** the same task repeatedly asks for a short/basic museum brief after receiving dense detail. “Proposed” façade materials and “not considered feasible” access options become more definite. Evaluate concise summaries by requested information and retained qualification, not just paragraph/sentence count. Several other rewrites correctly preserve identity, voice, fee items and existing project context; two pre-tool cancellations followed by edited submissions are intentional workflow controls, not demonstrated task failures.


- **H-007/H-012/H-019 — verified spreadsheet output, October 9:** task 1137 (`b5704498-87ad-4645-addf-312aa177bd88`) requested the branding and spreadsheet skills on an existing workbook. Independent checks of both immutable files confirm 13 original activities, with all responsibility cells blank; the output fills all 13 with an unrequested team assignment. The assistant also claims an upper-left brand mark, landscape printing and a 55-unit description column. The delivered file has **no embedded images, portrait printing and a 45-unit column**. The original was landscape. The published generator's SHA matches the inspected local script: `add_logo` silently omits an absent logo; column widths are capped at 45; orientation is selected by column count. The model staged branding tokens/reference/validator/renderer but left the logo deferred. Structural validation passed, then rendering failed on the known missing `office_converter` dependency; a direct LibreOffice workaround only extracted PDF text. No page-image inspection is recorded. This is a completed run with a real file, not a missing-publication incident.
- **Recommendation and test:** make required branding assets part of dependency closure and return a specific missing-asset recovery action instead of silently declaring success; make effective print settings visible in generator output. For formatting requests, preserve blank responsibility/date/status cells and source qualifications unless the user explicitly asks to populate them. Compare source/output semantic cells separately from style validation. Require decoded package evidence before claims about logos and settings. Estimate 1–2 days for package/readiness checks and a focused preservation fixture. Acceptance: missing-logo input either stages the correct scoped logo or gives an actionable blocker; no silent branded-success result, unchanged blank responsibilities, truthful effective orientation/width. Explicit requests to assign owners remain allowed. Confidence is high for this output and unchanged published generator; frequency of silent-logo omissions beyond this task has not been measured.


### H-049 — Mixed PDF image pages are skipped before claims of full review

- **Confirmed example/impact:** task 383 (`1016ea87-7a0d-478b-b706-ab8811f32f8d`, September 21), run `2e8025b5-c42c-40ed-aab1-953f3413f33a`, completes after about 74.7 seconds. Four attachments are read as text; the assistant says all were read and cross-referenced and requests a supposedly missing environmental scope extract. The actual 10-page RFP contains that readable extract as an image on page 7. Independent SSM retrieval, matching SHA/byte length and visual inspection confirm it includes additional permit conditions and a two-part design/site-supervision role omitted from the review. The separate full contract and other referenced appendices were not uploaded; their absence must remain qualified separately. This is one verified source-coverage error, not proof every blank-text page is important.
- **Frequency:** monthly archive scan finds 103 mixed text/no-text PDF results across 35 tasks and 23 users, among 1,727 PDF text results. Of these, 102 results across 34 tasks and 22 users are input PDFs, among 1,610 input-PDF text results. Counts include repeated reads; empty-text pages may be genuinely blank, photographs or irrelevant diagrams. The cohort identifies candidates for review, not 34 confirmed incorrect tasks. Private evidence: `monthly-mixed-pdf-page-coverage-symptoms.json` and `task383-rfp-page7.png`; immutable PDF SHA `97debb0dd215b3ccd2dbe8e36dbfb8116213325155db19ef2ce5cd1f990ce443`.
- **Cause/current status/confidence:** [PDF read](apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:862) returns `extractedText=true`; [page formatting](apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:3646) puts “No extractable text” in the body but counts that page in `outputPages`, without a structured partial-coverage signal. The deployed compiled function has the same behavior. An offline fixture of three pages, with one empty-text page, returns `outputPages=3` without indicating incomplete extraction. The model ignores the inline warning and turns unavailable extracted text into absence from the source. High confidence in the verified omission and current contract gap; whether OCR would recover every affected page is unmeasured. The later rewrite prompt patch does not change this input-coverage contract.
- **Proposal/benefit/effort:** track text-covered, empty-text and visually reviewed pages separately. Flag incomplete source coverage and make targeted rendering/vision or OCR available for relevant pages before claiming a document fully reviewed or information absent. Preserve page numbers and original bytes; report uncertain or unreadable regions precisely. About 2–3 days for typed coverage, bounded visual fallback and focused regression cases. Benefit: fewer missed contractual requirements and fewer unnecessary requests for already-uploaded information.
- **Tradeoffs/test:** empty extracted text can mean a blank page or harmless figure, so avoid automatic full-document vision on every PDF. Use selective page inspection, bounded cost and a scoped content-hash cache. Test the actual mixed RFP fixture, entirely scanned files, true blank pages, sparse text with image tables, bilingual pages and interrupted extraction. Acceptance: page 7's scope is included; the full contract's separate absence stays explicit; no “fully reviewed” claim until relevant page coverage is established. Measure source-omission corrections, unreviewed relevant pages, latency and added vision/OCR cost.
- **Implementation (14 October):** PDF reads now return `textCoverage: "partial"` and `pagesWithoutText` for shown pages with no text layer, with a notice that those pages were not reviewed and how to inspect them. Not deployed. Selective automatic OCR/vision remains.

- **H-033 diagnostic reconciliation:** all 33,531 monthly model-step diagnostic records were retrieved in a bounded read-only SSM stream. They include 33,269 successes and 262 final failure diagnostics; 55 Gemini failures have HTTP 400 / `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, and the 36 payload failures carry HTTP 413 / `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE`. These are logical-step final diagnoses, not the separate 36,154 physical-attempt report population. Preserve existing step status/code in administrative diagnostics; add a redacted failing-field/body and request ID where safely available instead of claiming codes are wholly absent. The precise Gemini compatibility cause remains open.


- **H-012/H-019, independently verified Word preservation failure:** task 578 (`22d18ffc-eb45-4102-bb7a-69385fb6a4e8`, September 28) asks for a Company A/B label mapping. Source and published output hashes match immutable storage records. The generated XML helper concatenates descendant text across nested paragraphs/alternate shape representations and clears all but the first text node: six nonempty cover text-box paragraphs become one, and four header text-box paragraphs become one. Titles/labels are duplicated and moved; the recorded rendered PDF text also shows concatenated headers. Body replacement counts and zero-placeholder checks therefore do not establish preservation of layout. The task correctly retains an unknown HR address, repairs a nonexistent lxml API and numbered-header matcher, publishes a file, then fails on the known queue FK issue. Do not classify the final failure as an absent artifact or a general Python outage. A format-preserving DOCX replacement utility with paragraph ownership, split-run handling and separate alternate-content branches would avoid this repair pattern. Estimate 1–2 days plus source/output tests: nested text boxes, styled runs, numbered headers/footers, fields and deliberate unresolved placeholders. Compare text by location and render affected pages before claiming unchanged formatting. Current ad hoc pipeline has no verified corrective deployment; exact final visual severity remains unmeasured.
- **H-003/H-013/H-019/H-039, OCR recovery fixture:** task 748 (`72929835-35e1-4836-aa77-3dc61c68cf19`, October 1) takes about 560.6 seconds to extract seven workflow IDs from a 512×102 screenshot. It makes ten vision calls (six completed, four failed), multiple upscales/crops and unusable Tesseract passes. Several “completed” observations stop at the token limit and disagree on row counts/characters; one grouped result contains only 71 characters. An empty length-limited observation is incorrectly diagnosed as an image too large for the model, although there is no payload-limit evidence. The final claim that all passes agree is contradicted by the retained results. Independent source inspection confirms seven visible rows; exact digit correctness remains cautious given the tiny glyphs. Treat this as a confirmed recovery/claim-quality problem, not proof all final IDs are wrong. Add typed truncation/output-budget guidance, a concise transcription mode, source-quality/uncertain-character reporting and a bounded strategy change rather than repeated paraphrased inspections. Benchmark the original screenshot for latency, calls, complete row coverage and exact-character accuracy; allow “unreadable” instead of forcing consensus. Reuse H-039's completeness fix and H-004's cross-tool progress controls rather than adding another blanket timeout.


- **H-039 parameter clarity:** task 377 (`1abb76c7-64e5-45b7-a2a1-7df6820aa017`, two drawing conversions) supplies specific verification questions to four `overview` inspections. Current local and deployed adapters use a fixed reusable-digest prompt in overview mode and only pass the supplied question in focused mode. Make the effective question/mode explicit and warn or normalize incompatible combinations; preserve deliberately reusable overviews. One final verification ends at `length` but still supports a claim of preserved annotations/geometry. The source/output geometry was not independently compared, so this does not establish a wrong drawing; it establishes insufficient support for the guarantee and a parameter-selection improvement. Test targeted QA versus reusable description, partial output and source/output element counts.
- **H-004 guard revalidation:** current compiled vision code already prevents repetition of an identical failed argument fingerprint. It compares normalized question, mode and paths; a cosmetic question change bypasses that specific guard. Task 748's first two same-image HTTP 500 failures use paraphrased questions with no new image between them. Use error category and source identity when assessing unproductive rephrasing, while allowing a genuinely different crop/question and bounded transient recovery. Do not claim that vision currently has no repeat guard or that every later crop was pointless.
- **H-049/H-014 historical scanned-report fixture:** task 345 (`36dac08e-0f4b-4de0-964a-4dcfc8745984`, September 21) correctly detects partial text in an 88-page PDF, then renders 78 scanned pages and runs eight parallel OCR workers. An unsupported rendering flag is repaired; silent OCR runs end in recovery-required after about 431 seconds, with no requested extraction delivered. This predates the September 30 late-result fix, which is deployed. The old trace does not establish a current timeout defect or show whether a late successful OCR receipt existed. Keep it as a regression fixture for page-level OCR progress, bounded resource-aware concurrency, saved per-page results and resumable extraction. Distinguish method-statement pressures/spans from actual tested results. Do not simply increase all timeouts or automatically replay arbitrary shell mutations.


- **H-002/H-011, historical rewrite and argument fixtures:** task 397 (`0ea32632-2f2b-4965-b216-e7502bf356f8`, September 22–23) requires repeated corrections to letter style and which source draft to preserve; an explicitly attached-source revision then correctly retains its original six paragraphs. A later project polish changes “must incorporate” to “may incorporate”, adds approximation to an exact area and proposes uncertain proper-name/technical-term corrections. It flags those name guesses, but not the changed obligation. This predates the faithful-editing deployment; keep it as a regression fixture, not evidence of a new post-patch perspective failure. Two compose repairs reject an empty non-email subject and a misplaced variant title. Current tool documentation already says to omit non-email subjects. Evaluate safe normalization of only an empty irrelevant subject and provider-compatible conditional schemas; do not silently drop substantive supplied fields. Measure avoided repair calls and preservation of draft text.
- **H-048 additional history-attribution example:** task 76 (`e5af21a6-1e9a-4f34-9bae-6094bc4d9f36`, September 14) produces an amicable letter, then a continuation with **empty recorded input and no new user text** says the last message “pasted a database error rather than an instruction”. The prior error was Berry's own queue FK failure. This supports the current history-role/typed-error proposal; it does not add another diagnostic-only completed answer to the narrow one-message exact-match count. Two later 3 KB pasted-text uploads fail verification and continuation probes cannot recover them; the requested comparison is never delivered. Those failures belong to the existing historical verification cohort, not a newly demonstrated live backlog. Plain-text finalization is an explicit user preference, so the absence of DOCX/PDF is correct.


- **Input and branch coverage reconciliation:** all 4,581 retained runs associated with the selected monthly tasks have been reconciled against their projected user messages and retained journal branches. There are 4,110 exact nonempty matches, 331 matches after separating explicitly appended profile context, and 140 empty continuation inputs. No distinct retained nonempty user request remains unmatched. All 36 distinct appended profile blocks, totalling 53,256 characters, have now been read separately and hash-recorded. This closes a rendering omission for profile context; it does not turn unreviewed conversations into reviewed tasks. Current conversation/tool coverage remains 432 of 1,229. Empty continuations are a legitimate control path, so recovery must not describe prior platform errors as user instructions. Profile instructions repeatedly ask for faithful editing and evidence-backed work, which supports evaluating adherence rather than merely adding more generic prompt text.
- **H-017/H-025 qualification-preservation example, October 2:** task 760 (`8f5ddacc-2685-4bab-941d-c7263243ad00`) reads the complete 11-page EOI, including an explicit follow-up for page 11. Its source asks applicants to declare prior involvement in the relevant assessment and explain actual, potential or perceived independence issues and mitigation. The assistant strengthens this into an automatic disqualification statement. That is not supported by the quoted application question; this audit does not determine legal eligibility. Proposed acceptance fixture: preserve “declare, explain and mitigate” as such, distinguish an express exclusion from a disclosure requirement, and quote the controlling clause before deciding a bid is barred. This shares the existing source/qualification checks, approximately 0.5–1 day for the fixture and evaluation. Confidence is high in this source-to-answer mismatch; the current prompt cannot guarantee that this interpretation error is resolved. The same task is positive evidence for complete PDF pagination and correctly extracted submission deadlines. Company-certification/team claims were not independently checked against frozen grounding, so they remain a grounding question rather than a confirmed false claim.


- **H-017/H-014/H-008, unsupported technical-reference and recovery example:** task 82 (`f202152e-afeb-4c03-b76e-07e2b8c33334`, September 14, all seven runs read) answers a standards question after a search returns only an exit marker. When asked for documentary evidence, it produces its own PDF/PNG containing asserted SMACNA duration, interval and stability requirements, while disclosing that it never obtained the manual. The files genuinely publish, but making a formatted reference sheet does not verify its contents. The later IPC response assigns the water-supply test to §312.2; [ICC's 2021 IPC chapter 6](https://codes.iccsafe.org/content/IPC2021P1/chapter-6-water-supply-and-distribution) instead references §312.5 for that test. It also conflates drainage water/air sections and omits the uppermost-system qualification when stating the head-of-water rule; [ICC's 2021 South Carolina plumbing-code text](https://codes.iccsafe.org/content/SCPC2021P1/chapter-3-general-regulations) and [ICC's testing explanation](https://www.iccsafe.org/building-safety-journal/bsj-technical/plumbing-code-essentials-testing/) provide the relevant distinction. The jurisdiction and adopted project edition remain unspecified, and the SMACNA hold-time assertion was not independently adjudicated from the paid manual.
- **Proposed guard/evaluation:** after unsuccessful retrieval, state the source limitation and keep any practical estimate separate from a verified standard requirement. Do not convert an unsupported answer into an authoritative-looking reference artifact. Preserve edition, clause, conditions and exceptions in source-backed answers. Reuse H-017's retrieval/qualification fixture and measure verified-clause accuracy and unsupported normative claims, not merely successful file publication. This is one confirmed citation/grounding example; frequency is not inferred from all engineering questions.
- **Recovery/control details from the same task:** a subsequent 6.26 MB manual upload hits the historical 75-second verification failure. The model searches absent effective-cwd folders and alternate template folders, then guesses at an FTP/extraction delay and offers renaming/re-uploading without pipeline evidence. Recommend typed upload state and an explicit wait/resume action, preserving the existing file receipt and removing speculative cause explanations. The current active-cwd child-directory guarantee remains the previously documented H-008 gap; the upload incident belongs to the September verification cohort, not a newly demonstrated live outage. An empty local mount does not by itself establish whether a Drive connector is available; use the scoped capability/discovery state before claiming no integration exists. The copyright statement is overly broad for a request to locate or cite a manual, but no need to reproduce paid source pages is inferred.


- **H-002/H-020, translation changes decisions after the prompt patch:** task 602 (`3661e525-4bc4-4218-8988-403a5c91db58`, September 28, all seven runs reviewed) asks “in Arabic?” and supplies three English clarification questions about repairing hyperlinks versus restoring them from a backup and which relative folders to accept. The assistant answers those questions for the user, forbids backup restoration and broadens folder acceptance, rather than translating the questions. Later story-style prompts label these assistant-chosen options as “decisions already locked in”. There is no recorded user decision selecting them. This is a confirmed task-scope/translation error with downstream context amplification; no workbook was changed or macro executed. Add a post-patch evaluation where quoted questions remain questions, and distinguish instructions to translate from instructions to answer. Prior assistant suggestions must retain their proposal status until the user adopts them. Approximately 0.5–1 day within faithful-editing evaluations.
- **H-012/H-019 prompt/code specification consistency:** the same task's proposed transformation rules prepend a base only for numbered asset-folder prefixes, leaving the supplied full `.shortcut-targets-by-id` case without its required drive prefix. The examples and rule do not agree. A generated-code/prompt fixture should exercise every supplied input/output example and separately handle full shortcut paths, relative asset paths, already correct paths and unrelated text. This is a confirmed specification inconsistency, not an observed VBA runtime defect. The subsequent request for an English version is ordinary user steering and is correctly followed; it is not counted as an Arabic rendering failure.


- **H-002/H-019/H-029 source tracking fixture:** task 417 (`4c4cc89f-504c-408a-bd06-7130f3e31fc2`, September 22, all three runs read) edits two different banners sequentially. The second final reply carries over the first banner's “illegible laptop screen” caveat even though the second source description and output inspection describe a growth-arrows graphic without that element. Both image-edit calls correctly use their respective new source file; the unsupported caveat is in the response/verification summary. This is a pre-patch regression example, not independently verified pixel-level geometry loss or a fresh post-patch rewriting failure. Add an evaluation in which changed-source QA and the final answer are tied to the latest artifact identity. Exact-layout guarantees require an actual source/output comparison; inspecting only the output against a verbal description does not establish that nothing moved.


- **H-002/H-040/H-033, current request ignored despite technical completion:** task 992 (`5c150ee2-af70-406f-b147-9ba3b64e27a1`, October 7), first Qwen run `3dc440e6-af34-4a94-a231-22cb4d129cd9`, completes in about 13 seconds with no tools. The user explicitly asks whether an attached sewage PDF answers a reviewer comment, yet the answer says there is no specific request or task attached and offers unrelated prior project work. The request is present both in the durable run input and the projected user message. A narrow monthly text scan finds this no-request-denial wording in **one assistant message / one task**; it is not the frequency of all context mistakes. The frozen provider wire request was not captured, so omission during assembly/serialization versus model attention failure is unresolved. Recommend a context invariant and privacy-preserving manifest that records the selected current request identity/hash, final user-message hash, attachment references, branch parent and grounding version at provider dispatch. Add a latest-request-versus-memory evaluation and measure unanswered explicit requests separately from HTTP/run success. Do not use a keyword-only response filter or automatically add another full model call to every turn.
- **H-009 deployed parity confirmed again:** the same task's second run fails before a provider attempt with sandbox-not-found during preparation, then Continue succeeds on the **same sandbox ID** about six seconds later. This supports a paused/cached-handle recovery issue, not proof the sandbox was destroyed. Current SSM inspection of the actual resolved provider confirms SHA `85f74665b4ce1a84bb410d658391780a6ce290325d3123bfa3870c320f60bded`: `resume` returns a cached handle based on its local expiry and has no force-reconnect flag. The compiled continuity caller also lacks that flag. The local source and two attachment-staging test cases contain the reconnect change; it remains absent in production. This is additional evidence for the existing H-009 recommendation, not a second proposed ensure implementation. Validate the worker, provider and contract package together, test cross-worker pause and stale-cache reuse with preserved files/tenant policy, and benchmark the reconnect cost against failures avoided. No deployment or remote fault injection was performed in this audit.
- **H-017/H-025 copy-ready claims:** after correctly saying the PDF does not establish SEWA adoption or wastewater-testing frequency, the next draft states those unverified links inside the reply and relegates checks to notes below it. A later social-facilities reply also adds that all listed facilities are operational within prescribed service radii, beyond the provided school/exemption facts and without inspecting drawings. Place material verification gaps in the copyable draft itself, retain user-reported approval status as reported, and answer each reviewer requirement including accessibility. This is source-to-draft qualification loss, not a legal finding that the approvals or regulation are invalid.


- **H-049/H-039 positive recovery and extraction-quality fixture:** task 415 (`e36b0fce-7a38-4c92-9da2-626d7582f054`, September 22) notices that one Arabic PDF is scanned and another produces corrupted font-encoding text, then uses page images and focused crops to reconcile a digit disagreement against a second document. All requested fields are delivered. Two `/tmp` reads are rejected and promptly moved under the permitted workspace; several vision outputs still end at `length`, including the final name verification. Extend structured page coverage to distinguish text present from text usable, without treating every unusual script as corruption. Preserve the successful cross-document check as a positive control; do not classify this as a failed extraction or prove its final digits wrong without independent source review. Shared rendering/OCR preparation under the permitted workspace can remove the path-policy repair round.


### H-050 — Ready optional capability results can be discarded at the admission deadline

- **Confirmed code behavior/current status:** [optional admission resolution](apps/api/src/http/agent-api.controller.ts:347) immediately returns its fallback when the shared deadline is past, without checking whether the observed promise has already fulfilled. The captured deployed controller has the same function (controller SHA `50afe363999a287d5ec984de018095eef35f392db10c67267f18cb9f1dd435b1`). A pure offline fixture of that exact compiled function returns `null` and logs “timeout” for a previously fulfilled available result when the deadline has passed, while returning the same value before the deadline. No production operation or model call was executed by this fixture.
- **Related usage, causal limits:** task 535 (`81f83387-3ca2-4817-abff-0db350044b66`, September 25) completes two ordered DOCX-to-PDF merges, about 133.3 and 90.6 seconds. The first turn has an admitted vision adapter; the next turn has no vision cost/config and correctly omits `inspect_images` from allowed tools. The model still calls it from prior context and the worker rejects it. A monthly scan finds **54 such tool-step rejections across 36 runs / 29 tasks / 20 users**. These are guarded calls, not 29 failed tasks or proven deadline losses. The historical reason for task 535's missing adapter is not captured; governance, native-image capability and resolution failure can also intentionally produce no adapter.
- **Proposal/benefit/tradeoff/effort:** preserve already-settled successful optional preparation, start independent resolution early once identity/policy dependencies are known, and persist a scoped availability reason for timeout, lookup failure, configuration absence or policy denial. Keep the two-second admission SLA and current budget/authorization checks. When capabilities change between turns, give the model an explicit current capability note and let the UI explain whether to retry, reconnect or contact an administrator. Estimate 1–2 days plus deadline/concurrency fixtures. Benefit: fewer avoidable lost capabilities and less repair work; careless caching would risk stale permissions, so cache keys and invalidation must include tenant/user/policy revisions.
- **Acceptance:** ready-before-deadline but consumed-after-deadline, genuinely pending work, rejection, cancellation, policy denial and tenant separation. Measure retained-ready-result rate, actual admission p95, degraded reasons and unnecessary unavailable-tool calls. Do not increase the global deadline or silently enable blocked capabilities. Production causal frequency remains an instrumentation question.
- **H-019/H-016 quality details from task 535:** the second merge reports clean visual QA after the rejected vision call and a text-only check. Both `qpdf` checks report warnings that are described as an integrity pass; shell pipelines hide the validator exit. The source DOCX was never visually inspected separately, so the first TOC overlap cannot be conclusively attributed to the source. Report validation type, scope and warnings accurately. Both content hashes publish to distinct durable file IDs despite the reused filename, a positive counterexample to blanket duplicate-publication failure.


- **H-017/H-023/H-029, source interpretation and current-date checks in a completed review:** task 880 (cd07307b-ea09-470a-964a-0e346bd8a58c, October 6) completes a six-page scanned hotel-contract review in about 144.4 seconds, followed by an 8.5-second B2C explanation. All six OCR pages and the tool sequence were reviewed. The immutable source was independently downloaded through read-only SSM; SHA 9758ff8b0d659a4a6b85a68715792395dfa5ad50a661fc52221888ee8729f4cf matches its stored metadata. Independent page 1 and page 6 renders confirm three specific errors: (1) a claimed currency contradiction confuses the Blu hotel's resident-eligibility column/its immediately preceding-table notes with the separate “Radisson Residences” property table; (2) the answer describes AESG as already signed, although the second-party date/signature/stamp fields are blank; and (3) the September 30 acceptance deadline had already passed when the October 6 review was written, yet the action list treats it as an upcoming deadline. The answer later notices the blank fields, so this is also an internal consistency failure.
- **Cause/status/proposal:** these are source-to-answer and temporal-grounding failures despite successful tool execution, not evidence that OCR failed to read the relevant pages. This post-September-24 example shows the faithful-editing prompt alone does not ensure analytical source fidelity. Add scoped evaluation fixtures for similar property names versus eligibility labels, footnote/table association, signature-state evidence and dates relative to the run date. Require a claimed contradiction to identify the conflicting clauses and their scope; preserve evidence qualifiers in copy-ready conclusions. An elapsed deadline should prompt confirmation of prior acceptance or an extension, without declaring legal cancellation as an established fact. Estimate 1–2 days for fixtures and a targeted source-fidelity instruction; evaluate false alarms and missed risks without adding vision calls to every document. Current adherence remains unresolved; no patch or prompt deployment was performed.
- **Limits/positive controls:** one independently confirmed task establishes these three mistakes; broader frequency is not yet measured. A generic currency note on another page could raise a different ambiguity, so this does not prove the entire agreement is unambiguous. The later B2C answer adds a business-travel-only restriction not explicitly established by the cited clause, while qualifying personal-holiday exclusion as “likely”; treat that as an interpretation hypothesis, not a proven contractual restriction. The review's “reciprocal indemnity” summary omits a cross-page guest-loss qualification that its full tool results contained. Add qualifier-preservation cases to H-017/H-025. The bounded OCR/render recovery itself succeeds and is a positive control for H-049.



- **H-003/H-006/H-017, bounded web recovery with misleading success envelopes:** task 808 (da9cf545-12a5-4fd3-bb17-30d17368e4d9, October 5) completes a travel-safety comparison in about 79 seconds. Three scrape calls are recorded as completed even though their payloads contain success=false and ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED; two sequential failures consume about 26.2 seconds, followed by a fast proxy retry. Search remains usable and supplies official advisory snippets, so this is a recovered task, not a task-level outage. Preserve that bounded alternative-source recovery, but expose transport failure as a typed tool outcome and report retrievability separately from execution status. Group same-origin network failures to avoid paying repeated browser setup delays once the route is demonstrably failing; key any short circuit by tenant and actual route, with a short expiry and a fallback rather than blanket network disabling. Current transport-cause/route parity is unverified; do not treat this historical trace as proof the current MCP connection is broken.
- **Answer scope from the same task:** the final answer turns advisory language into “banned/recommended against,” narrows crime risks to southern regions and adds insurance/weather assurances beyond the retrieved snippets. The broad country-advisory comparison has supporting official search results; these narrower assurances were not verified in the captured calls. Treat these as evidence-scope gaps, not independently adjudicated current travel advice. Extend H-017 fixtures to distinguish an advisory from a legal ban, nationwide precautions from regional restrictions, and source-backed facts from seasonal generalizations. Measure recovered-fetch latency and unsupported claims separately; no per-answer mandatory second model is proposed.



- **H-037/H-017/H-025, search-query damage leads to an unsupported equipment definition:** task 1056 (b1acc3da-bee2-49d8-ba40-271da1371a77, October 8) has two completed runs. The “ecology unit in HVAC” follow-up spends about 63.9 seconds and makes five successful searches. Four exact-phrase queries lose the entire quoted subject in the returned query; one becomes only “HVAC OR OR,” another “chiller OR OR brand.” The final answer then says an ecology unit most likely means an ERV/HRV or a translation error, with citations that define ERVs but do not establish that equivalence. Manufacturer evidence identifies established kitchen-exhaust treatment equipment under this name, a meaning the answer omits. [DRI Ecology Unit brochure](https://www.drirotors.com/wp-content/uploads/pdfs/EcoLogyUniit.pdf), [Halton kitchen-ventilation service scope](https://www.halton.com/commercial-kitchen-ventilation-service-and-maintenance/).
- **Confidence/status/fix/test:** the subject disappearing from the production search result is confirmed; matching local BerryCrawl operator parsing is already documented in H-037, while the remote BerryCrawl revision remains unverified. The model's initial energy-recovery-biased query also contributes, so do not assign all answer error to the search service. Preserve the subject and supported operators, report a query transformation explicitly and stop treating irrelevant results as evidence that a technical term is nonexistent. Add this real case to the H-037 operator-preservation tests and H-025 equipment-disambiguation eval; confirm a manufacturer's actual product definition before declaring a phrase a translation artifact. Existing H-037 effort remains 1–2 days after remote verification. Measure query-subject retention, relevant-source retrieval and correction rate; this one task is additional evidence, not a prevalence estimate or proof the user's unspecified equipment is a particular manufacturer's unit.



- **H-014/H-008/H-024/H-040, project recall versus live-file access:** task 1200 (fe392398-9f7f-4d70-b485-e762e3fa241b, October 12) fails after three model attempts; the durable provider diagnostic identifies HTTP 429/rate_limit, while the displayed message only says “Model request failed after 3 attempts.” Continue preserves the earlier searches and produces an answer in about 48.7 seconds. Preserve this successful continuation and expose the actual retryable rate-limit category, next action and retained work in the recovery UI; this is not an attachment-verification or sandbox-destruction failure.
- The same task spends calls probing an empty current workspace, a missing current-workspace inputs directory, the filesystem root and the unrelated template workspace. Both frozen attachment manifests are empty. Read-only SSM inspection of its frozen grounding confirms that the discipline counts, tracker mappings and spreadsheet defects in the answer are present in retrieved project facts/personal memory. **Do not label those numbers fabricated because no binary file was mounted.** The original spreadsheet accuracy/recency remains unchecked, and expanding an abbreviation into a new meaning is a separate source-fidelity question. For requests to review “all project data,” expose a scoped project-file inventory/retrieval route or a clear current capability note, and label recalled summaries with their source/version rather than implying a fresh inspection of all files. Stage only authorized needed files; do not auto-copy an entire project into every run. These are additional H-008/H-024/H-040 fixtures, with source-accuracy uncertainty retained.



- **H-012/H-019/H-014, an installed schedule is not a verified working automation:** task 63 (0d3ccde3-1c08-4506-a2e0-63007092f20b, September 13) delivers a PowerShell copier and batch installers. The user later supplies a screenshot showing both SUCCESS and FAILED; the answer assures them the copy is “set up and working,” attributes FAILED to a redundant second step/old attempt and says the daily run will happen. The immutable delivered installer contains only one task-creation command, and the copier still contains the missing exact-filename placeholder.
