Independent SSM downloads/hash checks of both scripts and the screenshot confirm the contradiction and the incomplete configuration. The screenshot establishes registration, not successful copying.
- **Likely script cause and further verified limit:** the installer echoes unescaped parentheses inside a parenthesized conditional, consistent with the screenshot's missing closing parenthesis and both branch messages. Microsoft documents parentheses as CMD special characters requiring escaping. Native Windows replay was not performed, so record this as a high-confidence parser cause, not a reproduced Windows execution. [CMD syntax](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/cmd). The copy routine's 60-iteration same-second loop has no final uniqueness check: a pure in-memory frozen-clock fixture reaches an already-existing destination on the fourth rapid run. The actual PowerShell copy was not executed and no user overwrite was observed. [Copy-Item behavior](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.management/copy-item?view=powershell-7.5). Its earlier Python simulation exercised three benign cases and could not validate CMD parsing, scheduled-task configuration or the untested collision case.
- **Recommendation/status/acceptance:** this is a historical generated-artifact defect plus an overconfident recovery explanation; it does not establish the same script is generated on today's deployment. Add these cases to H-012/H-019 target-runtime validation and completion-state evals: distinguish generated, configured, registered and test-executed; inspect the delivered script before inventing an error cause; require an exact source file or clearly blocked configuration; preserve copies through an atomic unique-name strategy with counter/random suffix and explicit failure on exhaustion. Offer actionable local verification with the scheduler query and the copier's output/log rather than an assurance. The suggested Win+R “schtasks” step also lists tasks in a console, rather than opening the described Task Scheduler Library. [Microsoft scheduler query](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/schtasks-query). Estimate 1–2 days for scoped regression fixtures plus a supported Windows validation lane if these artifacts recur. Test four same-second runs, concurrent launches, filenames containing spaces/parentheses, unavailable mapped drive, placeholder input, installer branch output and an actual dated-copy result. Do not automatically run user-facing installers or make scheduling claims from a Linux simulation.



- **H-025/H-017/H-002, an earlier discussed design becomes an “accepted” precedent:** task 427 (8cbe10df-9461-451f-b095-50432bf26787, September 22) reads the full supplied discussion and two manufacturer search-result sets, then categorically says no cavity-face breather membrane is required. It changes the user's earlier build-up “discussed with SFS” into a build-up “already accepted,” while also acknowledging that the exact insulation product's rainscreen exposure and the project-specific assessment still need confirmation. The retained calls do not establish the universal no-membrane conclusion, fire/cavity-barrier assurances or acceptance status. This is confirmed evidence/qualification drift, not a finding that a membrane is actually required for this unspecified assembly. No project-specific engineering assessment was supplied or replayed.
- Add this pre-September-24 case to the existing source-fidelity/faithful-reply regressions: “discussed” must not become “accepted,” a prior detail is not automatically an assessed equivalent in a different assembly, and unresolved manufacturer/application checks must remain inside copy-ready advice. Its roughly 143.8-second run includes a 94.5-second gap between finishing the source read and the first search; the tool calls themselves are fast. Diagnose model/provider versus user-visible progress before recommending a timeout change. Current generation adherence is not established by this historical example.



- **H-012/H-017/H-025, a fetched technical table is contradicted by the final answer:** task 930 (522c9425-a36e-42b7-bd4f-10a7634aa688, October 6) completes two insulation-property answers without tool failures. The fetched page identifies itself as Nevada Mechanical Code 2018 and its below-40°F cooling row gives 0.5-inch insulation for the 1-to-less-than-1.5-inch pipe column. The answer calls this an SBC 601/ASHRAE adoption check and says that same size range needs 1 inch; it also equates its quoted 0.5-inch minimum with R≈3.3, inconsistent with its own conductivity/R-value table. This is a confirmed source-table/unit consistency error within the retained evidence, not a determination of the actual applicable Saudi code edition or project minimum.
- The follow-up uses a specialist ArmaFlex LTD search snippet to support broad standard-grade density and KSA/GCC acceptance wording without opening the product specification or identifying the governing project standard. Treat the unverified density ranges/acceptance assertion separately from the confirmed prior table error. Extend existing numerical/source-fidelity evals with column boundaries, units, jurisdiction/edition and product-grade applicability. For code comparisons, name the document actually retrieved and retain uncertainty when the applicable project source has not been inspected. Add a deterministic arithmetic check to this fixture (0.0127/0.034 m²·K/W converted to IP is about 2.12, not 3.3), not a mandatory second large model call on every technical question. Post-September-24 source-adherence remains unresolved.



- **H-049/H-019/H-017/H-014, correct core term answer with unchecked fee assurance:** task 569 (f87aa959-289f-477c-8d59-fc21743e8376, September 28) completes a scanned 23-page agreement review in about 234.9 seconds. Clause 7.1 is actually returned and supports the answer that the agreement remains in force until terminated. However, the answer also assures the user that cancellation after two years no longer triggers fees, despite not acquiring the referenced Article 7.2/7.3 text or the full termination provisions. OCR processing of 23 pages is not the same as reading/validating all 23 OCR results. Treat the fee assurance as unsupported by the captured evidence, not yet as a proved wrong interpretation of the original agreement.
- The OCR process succeeds after about 130 seconds with only the final page count returned; no intermediate OCR-page progress or assistant update is recorded during that operation. Existing E2B execution already supports stdout/stderr events, so do not propose adding a nonexistent streaming foundation or raising all timeouts. A shared OCR helper can emit page-completion progress, retain a page coverage register and return complete relevant clauses including their references in one structured retrieval, reducing the many tiny grep/sed follow-ups. Validate that fees/notice/termination cross-references are resolved before a copy-ready “no penalties” conclusion. This is an additional H-049/H-014/H-019 case; visible spinner/timer behavior was not independently replayed. Source-artifact inspection remains separate from the complete transcript review.


### Verified source and image follow-up — review checkpoint 451/1,229

- **H-049/H-017/H-019, contract task 569 source verification:** the immutable 11,969,342-byte original PDF was retrieved through read-only SSM using canonical blob resolution and bounded S3 ranges; SHA-256 \`fd8a3f4239202dd6e464cbcf69f05055a0e3b2795c06c65141552aff508b5ba2\` matches both canonical and logical records. Pages 9, 10, 11, 12 and 18 were visually checked. The supplied adjacent pages jump from Clause 7.1 to 7.4; an independent English OCR search over all 23 pages finds only references to 7.2/7.3, not their operative text. Page 18 confirms the February 18, 2025 execution date. Article 8 still includes accrued charges, possible discount charge-backs and equipment-removal obligations. The exact fee outcome remains undetermined; the confirmed defect is the unqualified post-two-year penalty assurance without the necessary clauses. Recommend checking cross-references, exposing missing source sections and qualifying any fee conclusion while still answering the supported term question. All-page OCR processing does not establish all-page human review. The audit's initial raw file-location lookup and large SSM-output transport failures were audit-helper limitations; canonical retrieval succeeded and they are excluded from platform failure counts.
- **H-041/H-029, task 388 (September 21–22), preservation and dimensions:** the first rendering request produces a square image; Berry detects and replaces that draft, which is a positive recovery. The replacement is labelled 9:16 although its reported and locally inspected dimensions are 1024×1536, or 2:3. The 634×1283 original is narrower still. The final claim of a 9:16 master and a merely slight aspect change is therefore inaccurate. The later comparison properly includes both source and output, but reports wider foreground and altered tower proportions; the final “no distortion” assurance is stronger than that check supports. Recommend deriving ratio labels from decoded dimensions, selecting preservation-aware image operations, and carrying comparison qualifications into the final response. No independent pixel comparison or actual logo-shape mismatch is claimed for this task.

### Context attribution checkpoint — 452/1,229 tasks

- **H-040/H-017/H-026, task 824 (October 5):** read-only SSM retrieval of the frozen run context proves that the cooling-weather, glazing, roof and 350 L/s values were actually supplied by stored BHAD project facts; they were not invented from an empty context. The uploaded image and user request do not identify BHAD or Abu Dhabi. Berry applies those values first and asks at the end whether this is that project. A later frozen context includes an inferred profile record that incorporates Abu Dhabi/design weather while citing the original user message, whose text contains neither. That record is marked non-explicit with 0.75 confidence, which is useful metadata but insufficient if downstream prose turns it into a confirmed project link. Exact wrong-project application is not proven; source attribution and assumption persistence are the confirmed risk. Recommend project-scoped retrieval, visible source/assumption distinctions, and preserving uncertainty when storing and recalling inferred associations. Evaluate on new images in the same workspace from different projects; unrelated stored design parameters must not become asserted properties of the new image. The IESVE help portion correctly retrieves official documentation and gives a supported column-recovery workflow. The benefits question receives generic guidance after an unrelated seven-second input-folder listing; no actual HR policy access failure is established.

### Verified technical-answer checkpoint — 453/1,229 tasks

- **H-039/H-012/H-017/H-025, task 428 (September 22), incorrect values in a successful answer:** the original 37,491-byte HVAC schedule image was retrieved through read-only SSM and visually checked; SHA-256 \`f5c94dedfc5d26174136ced17ddb2af254ecc3e658623474b920eb2afefd8e05\` matches the immutable source. It shows model **RXQ22ANRYFK**, T1 COP **3.53/3.56**, T3 EER **8.89/9.47**, and CSPF **18.80/16.85**. Berry instead “corrects” the user to RXQ22ANYFK and reports the adjacent power-input values **10.40/9.40** as COP, with the remaining efficiency columns shifted. Three vision observations conflict; the final chooses the repeated wrong extraction and presents it confidently. Resizing the image, then copying it into the permitted workspace, does not help because the next vision call still uses the original path. One /tmp vision attempt is correctly rejected by the existing path boundary. The run completes after about 195.1 seconds with incorrect numeric guidance; completion status is not answer correctness.
- The follow-up standard answer also creates an incorrect selection table. Berry's 4–5-storey row switches to systems 7/8 above 25,000 ft² and its ≤3-storey row omits the upper-area boundary. The official table retains systems 5/6 for buildings up to five floors in the 25,000–150,000 ft² band, with systems 7/8 above 150,000 ft² or above five floors. This is confirmed against [ASHRAE's published 90.1-2007 addendum, Table G3.1.1A](https://www.ashrae.org/file%20library/technical%20resources/standards%20and%20guidelines/standards%20addenda/90_1_2007_r.pdf). Berry's claims that baseline envelope performance is identical to the proposed design and that Appendix G compliance savings are simply raw energy savings also need correction: [ASHRAE's explanation](https://buildingeq.ashrae.org/user/help) distinguishes prescribed baseline envelope performance and annual energy cost. The specific project's actual system selection is not independently adjudicated.
- **H-037 contributes:** the exact quoted model numbers are removed from a search, returning \`OR Daikin\`; the quoted standard edition and table identifier are removed from the later search too. This is a confirmed captured query mutation; the previously inspected local BerryCrawl sanitizer explains it, but current remote BerryCrawl revision remains unverified.
- **Recommendation:** use question-aware regional crops at native useful resolution, structured header-to-value mappings and ambiguity checks; resolve conflicts through source evidence rather than number of repeated observations. Preserve exact search terms and edition identifiers. Require direct primary-table extraction before publishing a code-selection table. Add this actual screenshot as a licensed internal regression fixture, with the model string and all three efficiency columns checked; add boundary cases at 25,000/150,000 ft² and 3/5/6 floors with the applicable edition fixed. Existing IDs cover these defects; no new issue is needed. Estimate 2–4 days for extraction/validation and evaluation work, apart from the separately scoped search-query fix. Expected benefit: fewer confidently wrong technical answers and fewer repeated OCR calls. Preserve path restrictions and avoid generative changes to source evidence.

### Finalization and recovery follow-up — 454/1,229 tasks

- **H-009/H-014, task 777 current-code trace:** the October 2 run delivered a sensible request for the missing source document, then failed immediately after its last successful model response with a sandbox-not-found error. The captured sequence contains no artifact-generation operation. Current deployed `sandbox-continuity.js:1091` still ensures a sandbox and lists outputs during finalization; missing output directories already return an empty list, while sandbox availability errors propagate. Deployed `turn-runner.js:1971` finalizes before committing completion; its outer failure path can therefore turn a delivered text answer into a failed task. These paths support finalization as the likely cause (high confidence), but no retained internal stack identifies the exact failing provider method. The already established stale-handle reconnect gap remains relevant; increasing timeouts alone does not address this state transition. Recommend tracking output-writing intent, bypassing unnecessary output scans for proven text-only/no-output work, and presenting answer delivery separately from artifact publication/cleanup. Actual artifact publication failures must remain visible and retryable, with idempotent receipts; do not hide them as success. Test a missing sandbox immediately after a completed text answer and after a generated-but-unpublished artifact. Estimate 1–2 days for phase/state and recovery behaviour, coordinated with H-009.
- **H-009 positive recovery, task 829 (October 5):** resource activation encounters sandbox-not-found after a user-answer wait, then the identical activation succeeds about six seconds later. The task produces a structurally validated memo, reads its rendered page through the native-vision path and publishes the DOCX. The about 225.7-second wall time includes human response time and must not be labelled wholly compute latency. This is additional evidence for transparent lifecycle recovery after waiting, not a renewed memo-formatting recommendation; memo fixes remain excluded as requested.

- **H-017/H-025, task 768 (October 2), location-specific source binding:** the Dubai venue recommendation imports group-room and charging rules from Lucky Voice's UK-domain FAQs, where the search results explicitly quote sterling prices, then applies them to Lucky Voice Dubai. Dubai package estimates are labelled estimates, which is helpful, but the stated group capacity/free-room-splitting rule is not established by a Dubai source in the captured history. This is confirmed provenance mismatch; current Dubai booking terms and prices were not independently adjudicated. Recommend binding price, capacity and eligibility claims to the requested branch/location, preserving currency and date, and stating quote-needed when only another branch's policy is available. Use this trace alongside jurisdiction/product-edition tests under H-017. A single 404 scrape is handled without a repair loop, a positive fallback; its misleading “blocked” classification remains an H-003 taxonomy example.

### H-051 — Continue anchors workspace recovery before the work being continued

- **Classification and priority:** confirmed current implementation defect, P1. Independent of H-010's unproved snapshot-timeout race, and distinct from H-009's cached-handle reconnect gap. H-046 describes one downstream failure.
- **Concrete example:** task 682 (\`17ba6355-300a-4c6e-8bca-37ac0cf3d2c0\`, September 30) writes the edited graphic and preview at 08:34:50 UTC, is recorded cancelled at 08:34:54.151, and has a complete checkpoint at 08:34:54.267. Continue starts on another model at 08:34:55.462 with the original user-message ID reused. Its new sandbox lacks the preview. The read tool reports success without checking the file, and the next model preparation fails with not-found. A third model reconstructs the edit and publishes it. Cancellation reason is not inferred; the subsequent missing-file failure is a distinct event.
- **Cause and current parity:** deployed API \`durable-turn.service.js:206\` reuses \`continuableRequestMessageId\`; admission also sets the session leaf to that original message (\`:336\`). Deployed worker \`sandbox-continuity.js:1822\` walks only the original request's ancestors to determine compatible earlier runs and snapshots. For this continuation, the actual retained branch consists solely of the original user entry, with no run ID or parent. Read-only replay of the **exact deployed SQL** returns no prior sandbox and no snapshot, despite the complete checkpoint preceding Continue. Worker module SHA-256 \`0978f3bb5e56366b4fbde40a3211a76ac241b74a94d8c9bae72729c3a7e80759\`; SQL SHA-256 \`e49d04c806f07e063a68c36deb03e12aafc732b969b1da6d416d19ca864c93b2\`. **High confidence.** This is checkpoint ineligibility, not evidence that its archive was late, corrupt or deleted. The implementation remains present on the inspected deployment.
- **Frequency:** the exact monthly window contains **129 explicit Continue runs, 81 tasks, 47 users** with empty input and an earlier failed/cancelled run sharing the request anchor; all 129 flags were independently verified as \`continueInterruptedTurn=true\`. Applying deployed branch-eligibility predicates to retained journal rows finds **53 runs / 42 tasks / 33 users** with neither a prior sandbox nor a snapshot eligible. **37 runs / 29 tasks / 22 users** also have a checkpoint from an earlier run of the same request already complete before admission. The 53 runs comprise 19 completed, 21 failed, 12 cancelled and 1 waiting. **These are lookup conditions, not 53 proved file-loss incidents or failures caused by this defect.** One full source/workspace trace establishes its concrete consequence here; broader consequences still need per-task review.
- **Proposed fix:** carry an explicit source run and intended continuation/checkpoint head into admission; select and restore workspace state against that head while retaining the original request ID for input identity. Keep conversation and filesystem continuation on the same branch. Do not fall back to an unscoped latest checkpoint: deliberate edits/rewinds, tenant boundaries and ownership must still exclude incompatible descendant work. Resolve legacy continuation records through a bounded same-request/same-branch recovery policy, with a clear recoverable state when no valid checkpoint exists. Preserve uncertain external-operation receipts and never rerun such mutations automatically.
- **Expected benefit, tradeoffs and effort:** preserve generated files and staged resources, avoid regeneration and stale preview failures, and make Continue behave as users expect. Adds explicit continuation metadata and migration/compatibility handling; checkpoint integrity and branch isolation take precedence over reuse rate. Estimate **2–3 days plus a day of end-to-end regression work**, coordinated with H-009/H-046.
- **Acceptance:** create a file and preview, interrupt after the completed write, then Continue on a different model and worker; both files must remain available with the same hashes. Cover failed and cancelled runs, an already-paused sandbox, valid/corrupt/missing archives, and concurrent tabs. Editing an earlier message must not inherit incompatible descendant outputs; cross-tenant access must remain impossible. Test that text-only continuation avoids unnecessary sandbox preparation and that a missing preview produces a typed per-file recovery result instead of aborting the whole model request. Measure continuation file-survival rate, restoration versus regeneration, and p50/p95 resumed time-to-useful-output.

**Task 682 validation and bounded repair:** the ultimately delivered PNG has the same 1478×716 dimensions and RGBA mode as the immutable original. Independent SHA-verified comparison finds 3,022 changed pixels, all inside the requested legend patch, with **zero changed pixels elsewhere**; the new DOO/DOF text was visually checked. This is a positive deterministic-preservation example. PNG DPI/sRGB/gamma metadata was not retained, so this proof covers decoded pixels, not every file property or print workflow. The earlier Pillow \`ImageDraw.getbbox\` typo is corrected in one retry; it is not evidence Python execution is unavailable. Font probing and two silent download failures consume avoidable preparation time; an installed-font capability map would help H-007/H-013 without requiring another global font installation.

Private evidence: \`task682-snapshot-timeline.json\`, \`task682-request-branch-anchors.json\`, \`task682-deployed-continuity-query-proof.json\`, \`monthly-continuation-branch-eligibility.json\`, its summary and explicit-flag verification, and \`task682-exact-preservation-proof.json\`. No production state, sandbox or application code was changed.

**Implementation (14 October, H-051/H-072):** Continue admission now records the source run and a verified journal head; the worker anchors workspace recovery at that head for continuation runs only. A real-PostgreSQL regression (`scripts/test-continue-lineage.mjs`) fails on the old lookup and passes with the fix; edits and unrelated branches remain excluded. Not deployed.

### H-052 — Requested skill is missed despite being enabled in the admitted catalog

- **Evidence:** task 1164 (\`2b30b3cf-f060-4cfb-b577-b902173a5cb1\`, October 12) asks to use “BSBG” on an attached CV. Berry searches the workspace and unrelated/protected skill directories, never calls \`activate_skill\`, and asks the user what to do because no BSBG skill is installed. Read-only SSM inspection of the frozen runtime request proves that **\`bsbg-cv-converter\` was enabled and model-invocable**, with a BSBG CV description, a valid personal-skill path and 9,862 characters of instructions. The admitted built-ins include \`activate_skill\`. The request later ends cancelled after clarification; no generated deliverable is established. Do not classify that cancellation as a platform failure.
- **Classification, frequency and confidence:** confirmed incorrect capability claim and discovery friction, **P1**, one fully reviewed catalog-verified task. The broader incidence remains unmeasured. The current organizational catalog's lack of BSBG is irrelevant to this admitted personal skill.
- **Cause and current implementation:** a requested short brand name is not resolved to the canonical skill name, and filesystem absence is used as proof of catalog absence. Deferred resources intentionally need not exist before activation. Current \`apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:5516\` already includes skill metadata in the system prompt; \`:5780\` already enumerates canonical skill names in the activation schema, and \`:5364\` performs exact canonical-name lookup. The corresponding deployed paths are present, including \`turn-runner.js:4377\`. **No missing catalog, disabled skill, missing activation tool or need to reinstall this skill is proven.** Exact physical provider request bytes are not retained; the frozen catalog and compiled prompt/tool construction are the implementation evidence. Confidence is high for the false absence claim, moderate for which model-attention/prompt factor caused it.
- **Proposed fix:** amend guidance to resolve explicitly requested skills against names, configured aliases and descriptions before probing any filesystem. Supply a concise canonical-name hint for an unambiguous configured alias such as BSBG; keep that hint in dynamic context after the stable cache prefix. If multiple skills match, ask a specific choice. Use the existing activation tool and deferred-resource contract, with actionable canonical choices on invalid-name errors. Never activate a merely similar unrelated skill or bypass its scope/permissions.
- **Benefit, tradeoffs and effort:** fewer incorrect “not installed” responses, unnecessary searches and user restarts. Requires maintained alias metadata and ambiguity handling; existing metadata/enum features should be reused. Estimate **1–2 days** for guidance, resolution hints and evaluations, with catalog revisions included in any cached lookup.
- **Acceptance:** with this frozen catalog and request, the first skill action must select \`bsbg-cv-converter\` before workspace/protected-directory searches or an unavailable-skill question. Include canonical names, casing, unambiguous short names, two conflicting BSBG skills, disabled/non-invocable skills, unassigned personal skills and deferred resources. Track requested-skill activation success, false-absence claims and preparation calls/time. This extends H-007/H-026; it does not reopen a skill installation or memo fix.

Private evidence: \`task1164-admitted-skill-catalog.json\` and its fully reviewed task transcript.

- **H-012/H-017, task 1038 (October 8), aggregation and confidence fixture:** the software-identification answer correctly finds named STAAD bracket captions and EasyFix4 version headers, and it renders selected image pages rather than relying on sparse PDF text alone. Its optional numeric summary labels the combined concrete-utilisation values 43.6%/69.4% as maxima even though the captured individual tensile checks reach 56.5%/77.3%. Distinguish individual, combined and governing checks before calling a value “maximum”; omit unrequested calculations when they add an unvalidated conclusion. The glass software identification is based on visual style and should carry that inference qualifier. Underlying structural adequacy and original source pixels were not independently adjudicated. Add this to the numeric-summary/source-confidence evaluation set; no new execution or extraction outage is established.

### Certificate and continuation verification — 458/1,229 tasks

- **H-039/H-049/H-007, task 1131 (October 9):** eight image-inspection calls across the cancelled and continued runs include missing files, empty observations and repeated output-length exhaustion at a 1,024-token allowance. Berry initially assures the user that Sarah Fayez is absent, then reverses that conclusion. The immutable original single-page certificate was independently retrieved and visually checked; its 471,506 bytes have SHA-256 `b37c2d20dfd95ad03b6a9f86fa5e7e759b261624b44e4d2023d5f16fb321f864`. **The final main answer is correct:** Sarah’s name appears in the Arabic opening paragraph; Mayar’s name appears elsewhere. This is a useful recovery, with avoidable time and an incorrect intermediate assurance. Two OCR languages, English and orientation detection, are available in the captured sandbox; Arabic is absent. Recommend bounded language-aware OCR capabilities and question-aware crops, reserving enough answer budget to return the requested observation. Expose uncertain/conflicting intermediate readings instead of announcing verification prematurely. Test this bilingual source for the names and locations, not merely successful tool exit. Scope installed language packs and their maintenance deliberately; do not automatically download arbitrary dependencies during every task.
- **H-051, second concrete continuation trace:** run `7956af73-41c7-4b20-ad6f-d35304ecc5bd` has no prior sandbox or snapshot eligible under the current branch predicates, even though a same-request checkpoint is complete before admission. Its first image check cannot find a previously created crop, and the next shell call confirms the entire temporary directory is absent; Berry renders the original again. The original attachment remains accessible. This is additional observed regeneration associated with the confirmed continuation lookup gap, not proof that the saved archive itself was corrupt. Cancellation intent is not established. The 53-run eligibility cohort remains a condition count, not a count of confirmed task failures.
- **H-001/H-014:** the resumed task takes about 414 seconds, delivers its final answer, then is marked failed by `session_entries_run_id_fkey`. **H-016/H-026:** the unsuccessful Arabic-data download returns curl exit 35; calling that “no network” is broader than the evidence. Typed TLS/connection results would guide recovery without repeating capability probes. These examples extend existing issues; they do not establish a new general network outage or Python regression.

- **H-004/H-007/H-008/H-019/H-050, task 539 (September 25):** two ordered four-DOCX conversions publish 58-page PDFs successfully. The first merge requires three failed shell calls: invalid qpdf syntax, an identical retry attributed incorrectly to quoting, and then a missing output directory. The subsequent batch uses the learned syntax and completes more smoothly. A tested conversion/merge helper with output-directory creation and explicit page-count/order receipts would remove these repeated preparation errors; reuse existing Office tooling rather than installing another stack. The first visual observation ends at the output limit before its layout verdict, yet Berry says all checks pass; acceptance should distinguish a truncated description from a completed layout check. In the second run vision is not admitted; Berry correctly states that limitation and preserves differing source project headers. This extends the measured optional-capability cohort; the admission deadline as its individual cause is unproven. No independent all-page source/output fidelity comparison has been made.

- **H-009/H-011/H-029/H-041/H-042, task 1054 (October 8):** the approved logo files are copied and published unchanged, a useful deterministic fast path. A shell call after a user-answer wait hits a stale sandbox and later succeeds. A vision observation describes the white logo as multicoloured; the subsequent pixel probe proves its opaque pixels are white. Transparency-aware inspection must therefore use a suitable composited preview and preserve the original alpha-bearing deliverable. The image-enhancement run receives HTTP 413 when comparing two original-size PNGs, spends two shell calls making smaller previews, and succeeds with roughly 262/270 KB JPEGs. Recommend provider-specific encoded-byte and dimension limits before dispatch, selecting bounded previews automatically while preserving originals; request-size/gateway-limit details are not retained, so the exact limit is unknown. The generated dimensions maintain the source’s actual 3:2 shape despite 16:9 metadata. Source/output comparison is a positive check, but its explicit uncertainty about fine vehicle positions disappears from the final no-objects-moved assurance. Carry those limits into the answer and prefer deterministic restoration where exact preservation is requested. Test alpha visibility, large multi-image input, truthful decoded ratio labels and preserved comparison qualifications.

- **H-022/H-012/H-019, task 1052 (October 8):** the user requests Dubai, **London**, and Riyadh icons; Berry instead plans, generates and publishes Dubai, **Abu Dhabi**, and Riyadh. The 305-second run is marked completed, and the user must send “london” to obtain the omitted deliverable in a further 130-second run. This is a confirmed instruction/deliverable mismatch after the rewriting prompt amendment; no memory or compaction cause is established. Recommend deriving a concise expected-output list from explicit user constraints and checking generated/published items against it before claiming completion. Test the exact three-city request and require London, no substituted city, without an unnecessary clarification. Measure omission/substitution corrections and avoidable generation time, not only completion status. Estimate 1–2 days for guidance and validation/evaluation integration.
- **H-016/H-019/H-029:** a filename mismatch raises Python FileNotFoundError but the shell returns zero because it ends with an unrelated listing. The task notices and repairs it, so this is a validation-status problem rather than Python unavailability. Its first colour check counts every transparent PNG pixel as exact orange without filtering alpha, while the white-background outputs contain zero exact target-colour pixels; a later normalization repairs the colour. Tests should count visible line pixels, check alpha occupancy and anti-aliased edges, and use checked filename manifests. Single-output visual reads and colour counts do not prove the final pixel-for-pixel geometry assurances. The subsequent London run successfully reuses the corrected algorithm, a positive recovery.

- **H-017/H-037, task 1207 (October 12):** five searches and one unsuccessful page extraction provide no captured equivalent UAE finishing-rate quotations, yet Berry publishes specific 2026 AED/m² ranges, premium percentages and a façade-package uplift. The final does label them indicative and requests supplier quotations, a useful caveat, but calls the figures typical local market rates without supporting source evidence. Their actual accuracy is unverified; the confirmed issue is unsupported market precision, not proof that every number is wrong. The exact quoted supplier name is also removed from a query, weakening entity matching. Recommend distinguishing retrieved quotations, calculated differentials and explicit illustrative assumptions; retain finish specification, unit, geography, date and scope on each estimate. If comparable rates are unavailable, state that and provide a clearly assumed comparison or quotation checklist. Test this captured no-rate-evidence case: no invented market-authority label, compatible surface-area units and traceable rate inputs. The initial project-name clarification is sensible, although empty-workspace probes add no useful task context.

- **H-037/H-012/H-017, task 350 (September 21):** seven of nine company searches lose their quoted identifying terms. A full company name becomes just GOC; an exact Australian company name becomes generic Pty Ltd terms or OR design-studio searches. The returned unrelated entities cannot establish that the requested companies are absent. The final appropriately flags two uncertain matches, but its category summary counts two Other entries even though its own eight-row table contains three; the summary totals seven. Preserve quoted search operands and carry entity-match evidence or an explicit unresolved status for each row. Validate category totals from the actual rows before publishing. No independent conclusion about the disputed companies’ real classifications or corporate affiliations is asserted here.

- **H-041/H-013, task 218 (September 16):** both airport-image runs publish 1536×1024 outputs while calling them 16:9. The follow-up correctly compares source and generated images and discloses the different aircraft silhouette; this is a normal requested refinement, not evidence of a failed first task. The first generation follows three resource-activation calls and two reference reads, about 64 seconds before image dispatch. Grouping the exact required resources and retaining revision-aware prepared state could reduce serial preparation; the follow-up already reuses the staged resources successfully. Preserve that working behaviour and the brand policy, while deriving ratio claims from decoded dimensions. No independent assessment of every generated detail is claimed.

### Independent schematic check — 465/1,229 tasks

- **H-019/H-007/H-039, task 874 (October 6):** the published 1920×1080 PNG was retrieved through read-only SSM; its 151,837 bytes match SHA-256 `bc0511d5bd6090b862fecb8df20cea87c556a12f5b5ba81bf0542692232c6358`. Independent full-image inspection confirms that Zone 5’s wrapping is repaired, but the queue label in Zone 1, both long equipment labels in Zone 2, and the loader label in Zone 3 still extend beyond their white boxes; the loader also crosses the zone boundary. The last vision check incorrectly says every label fits and reports four two-line bottom labels when the generated code and delivered pixels show six. **Confirmed delivered layout defect, high confidence**, extending H-019; source pixels have now been checked, not merely inferred from model descriptions. Recommend measured text bounds and wrapping for every label, then visual QA on the final version. Include long labels, ampersands, angle brackets and Unicode in a shared diagram helper’s acceptance fixtures; check all zones after a regional repair. A completed/truncated vision description is insufficient proof of containment.
- The same run first emits invalid SVG because unescaped ampersands enter text, then its fallback tries a missing executable even though CairoSVG is installed. Berry repairs both and publishes successfully. Treat malformed input and unavailable renderer as distinct recovery cases, use XML construction/escaping and advertised renderer capabilities, and retain this successful recovery as a regression fixture. The first visible response arrives roughly 138 seconds after admission; that is observed user feedback delay, not measured first-token latency. Better phase feedback and a tested diagram primitive should reduce silent drafting and repeated repair without reopening already fixed timeouts.

- **H-017/H-040/H-022, task 949 (October 7):** Berry fills a 12-row independence-declaration form from two blank screenshots. Many cells and the final checklist use [Confirm], which is helpful. Other cells assert that the delivery team has been verified and that independence is confirmed, although no such check occurs. Read-only SSM inspection of the admitted input and complete frozen grounding finds no evidence of the company’s CRA participation, methodology access, personnel history or conflict status. The 12 project facts mostly concern older classifications, secondment and spreadsheet/Word guidance; 11 profile memories repeat the user’s work and writing preferences. This confirms unsupported attestations and irrelevant preparation context, **not** that AESG actually has a conflict or made a false submission. The explicit user profile already says not to invent technical/project information.
- Recommend a per-cell evidence state for declaration/compliance forms: supported, conditional draft, or pending confirmation. Unknown factual cells and their dependent overall conclusion must remain visibly pending; a generic draft footer and selective [Confirm] tags are insufficient when other cells say verification is complete. Provide useful conditional wording or a short fact checklist without inventing a negative answer. Test the two blank screenshots with this frozen context: no unqualified no-involvement or verified/confirmed conclusion, and pending status survives into both DOCX and final summary. This extends source-fidelity and relevance work; do not infer a new compaction or memory-access failure. The successful repeated-table-header repair should remain an artifact regression fixture; creating the output directory before generation would avoid its initial save failure.

- **H-029/H-041/H-002, task 145 (September 15–16):** two “high resolution” requests use generative recreation and output-only descriptions to support source-faithfulness claims. Both generated images are 1536×1024, but the final calls them 16:9; one says it is wider than the source’s 3:2 ratio even though its actual output ratio is 3:2. Selecting preservation intent and actual dimensions before dispatch would prevent this inconsistent explanation. Original source dimensions and exact geometry/material differences have not been independently checked for this task, so those remain unresolved checks. Its earlier polish strengthens “commonly used” to “the standard”; this predates the September 24 prompt amendment and is retained as a regression fixture, **not** evidence that the same editing defect persists today. The last pasted congratulations message is interpreted as a heads-up; its intended rewrite action is ambiguous and is not counted as a proved rewriting failure.


### Verified PBIX and UTF-16 recovery defect — review checkpoint 468/1,229

- **H-011/H-014, task 1222 (`9525a175-0c61-4c99-bb03-6295097c38e3`, October 12):** the user's first uploaded Power BI template read ends the run after 15.5 seconds with “unsupported Unicode escape sequence.” After the user retries with a screenshot, Berry successfully lists the PBIX archive, extracts its report and parses its UTF-16 layout into 11 pages. Reading an extracted, valid BOM-less UTF-16LE `.tmdl` model script then ends the second run after 77.4 seconds with the same error. Neither failed run delivers the requested template analysis. This is two failures within one task, already within the monthly Unicode-error cohort; do not add it again to the aggregate total.
- **Confirmed cause/current status, high confidence:** the immutable 2,126,348-byte PBIX matches SHA-256 `b868a1e5fe83411fb7e1b1a6c9091b034d6d133e0fbfc7607b99ffcd033f35b1`. An offline fixture executing the exact deployed reader (`sandbox-continuity.js` SHA-256 `0978f3bb5e56366b4fbde40a3211a76ac241b74a94d8c9bae72729c3a7e80759`) returns successful text results containing 151 NUL characters from the ZIP/PBIX and 2,786 NUL characters from the 5,620-byte UTF-16LE script. The current reader classifies formats largely by extension, omits `.pbix`/`.tmdl`, and applies UTF-8 decoding without binary/encoding validation. `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:6154` serializes tool output and `:6179` casts it to JSONB. A **read-only production `SELECT $1::jsonb` diagnostic**, deliberately containing a NUL escape, reproduces the same PostgreSQL rejection. This diagnostic is not a production task failure or a write. The exact historical exception stack was not retained, but the file bytes, deployed algorithm and storage constraint establish this causal path independently.
- **Proposal, benefit and tradeoff:** use MIME, magic bytes and bounded encoding inspection before treating an unknown file as text; handle PBIX as a bounded archive/metadata inventory and decode credible UTF-16LE/BE text, including BOM-less scripts. Return a typed unsupported-format result for genuine binary data. Validate recursive tool/journal payloads at the storage boundary so invalid Unicode cannot abort the whole run; preserve an explicit escaped preview, encoding/byte-count/hash and the unchanged source rather than silently dropping NUL bytes and presenting corrupted text as valid. Keep existing workspace/tenant boundaries and archive-size/path safeguards. Allow recovery from the preserved template and extracted files, with an actionable file-format explanation. Estimate 2–3 engineer-days for decoder, persistence and integration safeguards; permissive encoding detection risks false positives, so uncertain files must remain explicitly unsupported.
- **Acceptance/measurement:** replay the hash-verified PBIX and UTF-16 script through the reader and actual PostgreSQL tool-result persistence; include BOM UTF-16LE/BE, Arabic/emoji UTF-8, corrupt text, genuine NUL-bearing binary, large/unsafe archives and forbidden paths. Require correct decoded content or a recoverable typed result, unchanged source hashes, no raw invalid JSONB payload and continued model execution. Track whole-run Unicode failures, successful format recovery and time to a useful template analysis. Python parsing and ZIP access already worked here; another Python installation or timeout increase would not fix this defect.
- **Other evidence/limits:** an earlier Drive download was explicitly denied by the task's network policy and the run was cancelled; cancellation intent and alternate connector availability are not established. A prior search calls a public template a downloadable Microsoft sample without verifying its author or PBIX download route. Retain that as an H-017 source/availability gap, not a proved incorrect author claim or a new general network outage.


- **H-008/H-013/H-014/H-019, task 1136 (`98324166-41d1-4eb8-99e7-f9c3d1a712d8`, October 9):** a shape crop of a 634×655 portrait completes in **943.7 seconds**, after repeated boundary/circle fitting and grid-cleaning passes. A NumPy sliding-window median is killed with exit 137. Its 64-bit 9×9×3 window materialization alone is approximately **787 MB**, before median workspace/copies. The later bounded Pillow median succeeds. Memory pressure is a strong explanation, but there is no retained historical OOM-kill receipt; exit 137 alone does not prove it. A scan identifies seven killed/137 tool steps across four monthly tasks; only this conversation establishes the full context here, so this is not seven confirmed memory defects.
- **Current gap/proposal:** the bash path at `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:1291` preserves exit code/output but turns all nonzero exits into a generic error; it does not report actual memory pressure or a resource-specific next step. Add bounded resource receipts (configured limit, peak use and OOM counter only where available) and a typed killed/resource-pressure result that does not guess a cause. Supply tested memory-bounded image/filter and Office-render entry points, reuse workspace results, and discourage whole-image sliding-window materialization when a bounded library operation suffices. Prefer a changed algorithm or scoped retry; indiscriminate larger sandboxes and timeout increases are not justified. Existing Python/tool repair works; this is a resource/avoidance improvement, not a blanket Python regression. Estimate 1–2 days for diagnostics and one shared operation, with additional workload helpers separately sized.
- **Independent deliverable check/acceptance:** immutable original and delivered PNG hashes match canonical production records. Both complete images were visually checked; the requested panel crop is substantially delivered and the grid largely removed. Aligned comparison measures 4,830 changed RGB pixels; the final's approximate 4,800 cleanup count is consistent. The exact silhouette and preservation of every tie pixel remain unchecked, so do not claim an independently proven pixel-exact result. Retain this useful successful recovery as a fixture: bounded operations must finish within the actual sandbox limit, preserve source bytes and explicitly requested regions, publish transparent 541×527 output and distinguish an unverified cause from a measured OOM kill. Measure total preparation/model-round-trip time and repair count; image quality must not be sacrificed to meet a latency target.

### H-053 — Repeat guard blocks a renderer after its source file changes (P1)

**Confirmed production defect and exact deployed-code reproduction; high confidence.** Task 718 (`a48ea045-fa66-46ad-9999-bdfb01e2ab30`, October 1), run `f789b3e5-fc00-4acc-b91a-8617dce8d74a`, edits its diagram script between successful renderer calls. Each successful run returns the same stdout, “saved [output path],” although the script/diagram has changed. On the next edit, Berry suppresses the identical shell command. Adding a directory listing bypasses that fingerprint once; after another successful edit, the original command causes `duplicate_tool_call_loop` and terminal failure. The requested supporting image is never published. The preceding drawing run was cancelled; its intent is unestablished and is not automatically counted as a failure.

**Cause/current status:** `toolRepeatSignature` hashes tool name and canonical arguments, while `assessRepeatedToolCall` compares prior result fingerprints and does not consider intervening file edits/dependency versions. Once a signature is suppressed, its next occurrence terminates even after new file edits. The exact retained deployed functions from `turn-runner.js` SHA-256 `c030d2cab85b11ee48d41c589e9963890f4fd429bcf4b9b7cc9d291740ecf050` reproduce allow → warning → suppress → cosmetic-command allow → terminate against the actual recorded steps. Private proof: `task718-repeat-guard-proof.json`; fixture SHA-256 `64e4aa0388f1c3b2a284d110cfe36246d295218ee3b402f89becd2e417e5ccda`. Current matching source: [repeat signature/assessment](apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:4791), [suppression message](apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:2332). The existing changing-result test allows a second response but does not cover a third renderer execution after source mutation with identical stdout.

**Frequency boundary:** the fixed monthly window contains **64 repeat-guard steps / 40 runs / 37 tasks / 27 users**: 58 suppressions and six terminal-loop steps. These are not all false positives. A narrow metadata scan finds **10 steps in nine runs/tasks** with a completed edit/write of a filename referenced by the blocked shell command since its last matching successful execution. This is a condition cohort; only task 718 is fully traced/reproduced here. Eight other candidate conversations remain unchecked for this cause. It is distinct from H-030's legitimate asynchronous polling but should be implemented within one coherent repeat-policy design with H-004/H-028.

**Proposed fix/benefit/tradeoff/effort:** distinguish an operation's visible acknowledgement from its actual inputs/results. For local artifact generators, include declared source/dependency revisions and resulting file receipts; reset the relevant duplicate assessment when an authorized, meaningful dependency edit occurs. A tested run-script entry point with explicit dependencies is more reliable than arbitrary shell parsing. Permit bounded rerendering of the changed script without teaching the model to add meaningless command text. Keep no-change loop limits and external-operation/idempotency protection; do not exempt all bash calls or invalidate external-write deduplication after unrelated file edits. About **2–3 days** for the focused revision/receipt contract and integration fixtures; broader arbitrary-command dependency support needs separate scoping.

**Acceptance/measurement:** replay render → edit → render → edit → render with unchanged stdout but changed artifact hashes; all required generations and the final valid artifact must complete. An unchanged source/result remains bounded, an unrelated edit must not automatically justify repeating an external mutation, and repeated publication/delivery remains exactly once. Test pause/restore, cancellation, source revert, multiple inputs and edits after suppression. Measure false suppression/terminal rate, repair calls and time to a verified deliverable, with resource/latency caps retained. Error UX must explain whether work exists and what remains unverified, preserving the last prepared version without silently publishing a cancelled/unverified file.

**Implementation (14 October):** for observation tools, identical calls made before the latest write/edit of a file the call references no longer count toward suppression or termination; external writes and publication keep their dedup. The task-718 sequence now completes; unrelated edits still leave the loop bound in force. Not deployed.

- **Related task 718 observations, H-017/H-025/H-019/H-039/H-008/H-013:** initial advice treats an eave soffit as part of the tested envelope, then later categorically excludes it and any U-value, without a supplied assembly/section or primary retrieval. It also invents a specification/testing commitment in the design-stage draft and supplies unsupported material U-value ranges. The real project's classification and applicable 90.1-2010 provisions remain unadjudicated. Require assembly location, adjacent space condition and applicable code edition before a project-specific compliance assertion; proposed wording must leave unconfirmed design/testing facts conditional. PNNL's primary review guidance distinguishes exterior/semi-exterior boundaries by the spaces they separate, supporting the need for that input rather than blanket classification from a component name. [PNNL envelope review guidance](https://pnnl.github.io/90.1-PRM_ECB-ReviewManual/content/6_5_building-envelope.html).
- The first drawing spends roughly 137 seconds before writing, then repairs `z` versus `zorder` separately in Circle and FancyArrowPatch. Matplotlib/Pillow are installed; this is API usage/preparation overhead, not missing Python. Three later vision checks truncate at the fixed focused budget while reporting more layout defects; Berry does try to repair them. Reuse measured diagram primitives and complete regional QA to avoid a series of speculative coordinate changes. Final diagram pixels/layout have not been independently verified, and the failed/cancelled image work does not establish a delivered bad figure.

Several replies still add unrequested variants, long change summaries, subjects and courtesy closings; test a default faithful polish with optional alternatives rather than classify every added courtesy as a material defect. Berry supplies a representative list and warns the user to check the current approved offering, a useful qualification, but that qualification is outside the copyable email. - **H-017/H-025/H-037, task 132 (`cf0ec81f-b638-4d6b-91cd-ce214bcc0617`, September 14):** after a brief cavity-barrier answer, the user points out a missing closed-state/external-material detail. Berry spends 168 seconds on six searches and a document-index scrape, then presents a broad combustible/profiled/rainscreen intumescent-strip rule as current regulator/certification practice. ### H-054 — Vision retry guard ignores the cause and state of a previous failure (P1)

**Confirmed current harness defect; high confidence.** Task 843 (`c6482323-1e7f-4c17-924c-beaf5313c3c6`, October 5), run `8ee9c493-772d-4cd1-b0a0-487b6b665bc2`, receives a provider HTTP 500 while inspecting an extracted headshot. Berry attempts one identical retry after checking the file, but the guard rejects it locally in 0.01 seconds, with no provider attempt. It then relies on dimensions/embedded-image inventory and claims a real headshot without a successful semantic inspection. The task subsequently waits for user section choices; this is **not a terminal failure**. Fully reviewed task 647 also encounters a 500 followed by the guard and successfully obtains text through Tesseract; its later cancellations have unknown intent.

**Cause/current status:** [vision executor](apps/worker/src/vision-tools.ts:191) invokes [latestUnresolvedVisionFailure](apps/worker/src/vision-tools.ts:469) before image preparation/cache lookup. Any prior failed call with the same normalized mode/question/paths blocks the request, regardless of whether it failed from a transient server error, missing input, malformed request, credential issue or exhausted empty-content attempts. A later step exposing any `visionPath` clears the condition without comparing image bytes, and a cosmetic question change avoids the matching fingerprint. A completed inspection alone does not clear the older matching failure. The guard is scoped to matching arguments, **not all vision calls in the turn**; earlier shorthand suggesting a blanket prohibition is corrected here. The adapter retries empty final content internally but immediately rethrows its first provider exception. Fresh read-only SSM at **2026-10-13T14:24:02.132Z** confirms current deployed `/app/apps/worker/dist/vision-tools.js` SHA-256 `da5e6502d35491b8d1d7843b7d1462bb48e9bb941eaba3a237cf10445ba0de54`, identical to the replayed module. Existing tests cover empty observations and corrected paths, without a transient-error recovery contract.

**Reproduction/frequency:** exact deployed-function replay reproduces the task's block and shows unchanged-image reread/cosmetic-question bypass, failure-type indifference and older-failure retention after a simulated completed inspection. Fixture SHA-256 `0be4e0a3c2b7892015c5f12e86f5f1fc10acf86ff556f86bed7d1b4108812f1e`; private proof `task843-vision-repeat-guard-proof.json`. This tests guard policy offline; it does not assert the provider would have recovered on retry. The fixed month contains **nine guard steps / nine runs / nine tasks / eight users**: four completed, three failed, one cancelled, one waiting. Prior messages identify **four HTTP 500, one HTTP 504, two fetch errors and two HTTP 413**. These are exact guard-event frequencies, not nine causal terminal failures or nine wrongly retried calls. Only two conversations in this cohort are fully reviewed at this point; seven remain unchecked for full outcome/cause.

**Proposed fix/benefit/tradeoff/effort:** classify the failure and keep a bounded recovery state per semantic operation/image version. Permit one controlled retry/backoff for retryable transport/5xx failures within the existing turn deadline and cancellation budget, honouring provider retry information; preserve the no-change limit for deterministic input problems and exhausted empty observations. HTTP 413 needs a supported bounded preview; authentication/billing errors need their corresponding recovery, without repeated identical requests. A successful inspection should resolve that operation's failure; new bytes or a corrected path should be tested explicitly, and rereading unchanged bytes or rewording the question must not reset the retry budget. Provide a typed result explaining retry, input repair, administrator action or unavailable inspection. About **1–2 days**, coordinated with H-004/H-039/H-046/H-053 rather than removing loop protection. Benefit: recover transient vision outages and avoid misleading unverified-photo claims. Tradeoff: one additional bounded provider call/latency when recovery is warranted; no unlimited retries or identity proof from metadata.

**Acceptance/measurement:** replay 500 → same valid image → one success, with exactly two provider attempts and retained attempt/usage receipts. Persistent 500 stays bounded; 413 normalizes once, unchanged invalid input does not loop, and 401/402 expose the correct action. Test completion clearing a matching failure, same-question changed image, corrected path, stale/missing file, unchanged-image reads, cosmetic question changes, parallel selections and abort during backoff. Measure recovery rate by error class, local false-block rate, repair calls, time to a verified result and misleading unverified-success claims. A dimension check can establish a valid image file, not that it is the requested person's headshot.

**Implementation (14 October):** one identical retry is now allowed after a single transient provider/network failure; repeated or deterministic failures still block, and a later successful inspection or new image resolves earlier failures. Not deployed.

A trailing directory listing masks the second Python failure with exit code zero. Fresh read-only production package verification still finds both unconditional imports in the published 3,793-byte extractor, SHA-256 `8e3afd4f77fedf76b5ad6e38048068a06487bf0321e74de9f03161f23b8412d7`, and the old instructions lack the complete interactive staging manifest. ### H-055 — Multi-output helpers leave partial files that block recovery

- Priority: **P2**. Classification: **confirmed production recovery defect + current code reproduction**, high confidence. One fully reviewed causal example; a candidate cohort is separate.
- Example: task 694 (`dc3a31d9-1332-4b73-aac7-1b2c3a1f038a`), run `d85e64c3-ccf5-478d-9127-e76865eb3a77`, September 30. Photo preparation writes the square JPEG, then rejects a 1024×1536 portrait as incompatible with its required 7:9 crop. A corrected portrait retry fails because the existing square file triggers the no-overwrite guard. Berry deletes/recreates its temporary photos directory to recover. The final four CV files do publish; this is unnecessary repair work, not final task failure.
- Frequency: the exact helper error signatures appear in **38 monthly tool steps, 18 tasks and three users**: 25 ratio failures and 13 overwrite failures. Nine candidate task histories contain both signatures. Other histories are not yet all reviewed end to end, and an overwrite after an already successful helper (task 1214) is legitimate protection rather than this partial-write defect. Do not equate these counts with 18 confirmed partial-write incidents.
- Current status: still present in the personal `cv-creator-new` package, `scripts/prepare_profile_photos.py` SHA-256 `bc01c2d485aacd868f849c0719cca77961cbbd13fcce56a57c79c865b5ecdbdf`, freshly read through SSM on October 13. `main()` checks destination existence once, then calls `prepare()` serially; `prepare()` writes immediately. The square succeeds before a portrait exception, leaving an incomplete pair. A synthetic offline CLI reproduction using these exact bytes returns exit 1 with only the square written, then exit 2 on a retry with a valid portrait. No production files are changed.
- Fix: preflight both inputs, build a complete pair in a private temporary/versioned directory, and expose it only after successful validation. Return an output manifest with source hashes, sizes, transformation version and completeness. Permit safe resume of a matching incomplete attempt; preserve the no-overwrite protection for unrelated existing/user files. Preflight actual provider image dimensions before photo preparation rather than trusting aspect-ratio metadata.
- Benefit: fewer retries/probes and manual `rm -rf` repairs; clearer preserved-work status. Tradeoffs: small temporary disk overhead and an explicit commit/resume contract; do not silently replace approved photos or relax crop/identity safeguards. Effort: about **one day** for this helper and fixture; shared multi-file transaction semantics require broader design before reuse.
- Acceptance: bad second input leaves no visible final pair; corrected retry completes without deleting unrelated files; interrupted writes are identifiable and safely resumable; existing complete outputs remain protected; EXIF rotation, correct crop bounds, actual dimensions and source/version identity are checked. Measure photo-helper failure→recovery calls and preparation latency separately from generation/model latency.
- Private evidence: `task694-current-personal-helpers.json`, `task694-photo-atomic-fixture.json`, `photo-helper-failure-candidates.json`.

Preserve these positive source/parity results. Actual generation takes 16.32 seconds; repeated preparation, image generation, native-image/model gaps and several masked shell failures consume most of the run. The portrait's declared 3:4 ratio is actually 1024×1536, and the crop helper rejects it for the intended 7:9 target. The initial size failure and ensuing partial-file overwrite rejection are the H-055 cause above; a manual central crop bypasses the safe-crop rejection, so this is not proof the crop or likeness is visually correct.
- A separate roughly 157-second diagnostic loop follows the source-coverage rejection: one footer exclusion is not a body line. The generator initially hides exit 1 behind `tail`, the next command echoes a failing exit but itself completes, and a direct verifier call uses `--spec` instead of `--structured`; its `tail` also masks failure and prevents the fallback. Berry ultimately locates the footer XML, removes the inapplicable exclusion and passes the gate. The currently published verifier still reads only `word/document.xml` and reports the count of unused exclusions without identifying the item/source part. Return a typed, part-aware diagnostic containing the unmatched exclusion, expected CLI contract and body/header/footer coverage, retaining strict exclusion bounds rather than bypassing them. Clarify that body-line coverage and six output parity units are different guarantees. Source extraction includes footer text; its documented boilerplate omission is not an unexplained lost project. Four repeated portrait-verification assurances add little progress information. Exact delivered page/slide pixels and original/final identity are independently unchecked.

### H-056 — Duplicate JSON keys silently omit substantive document content

- **Confirmed defect; P1; high confidence; current production parser verified.** Task 837 (`7e5d1b78-c51d-4b63-914f-a101ddd7d680`, October 5), run `dab156f1-035b-4908-ac6c-70db8cc07126`, is marked completed after 239 seconds. The request is a client scope-acceptance statement. Its JSON section repeats `paragraphs`: three opening/acceptance paragraphs are overwritten by two closing paragraphs. Python accepts the duplicate key. The published DOCX omits the project/proposal reference, the actual “AESG confirms … accepts” statement and the documents-accepted lead-in. The final explicitly claims the opening and confirmation are present. This is independently checked in canonical published body XML, not inferred from an incomplete vision description.
- Published file `46ca14a7-3748-4b48-90d0-d6314832eb95`: 1,051,417 bytes, SHA-256 `1a6fe37c575bfc116bbd7f24a24b5f415aa95975a95189575f8b6f62bd7e9ae4`; DB/blob/immutable bytes match. Branding validator returns ok and focused visual QA reports a tidy page containing only the two closing paragraphs; neither tests whether the requested acceptance is present. Source/spec/output content completeness must be separate from attractive rendering. Independent production pixels are not checked.
- **Frequency:** a bounded monthly scan finds 3 duplicate-key JSON write calls across 3 tasks/users among 561 syntactically valid `tool.write` calls to `.json` files. Candidates are tasks 837, 1047 and 1093. Only 837 is currently causally verified against a published artifact. Heredocs, edits and script-generated specs are outside that scan; these counts are neither all document specifications nor three independently proved final omissions. Other full histories remain governed by the coverage ledger.
- **Current cause:** a fresh read-only SSM query confirms the active organization DOCX helper still has `spec = json.loads(args.spec.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))` at line 1874, with no duplicate-key hook. Its 72,381 bytes/hash `a496e98ad11da9c72285cc27c39b723f2897130fbb5c9144f1adda1e19f1da87` match local [create_aesg_docx.py](deploy/skills/docx/scripts/create_aesg_docx.py:1874); organization capability updated September 24. Offline parsing of the exact recorded spec produces two paragraphs; strict pair parsing rejects `paragraphs` before any generation. This establishes the remaining parser mechanism, not a live production re-run.
- **Proposed fix:** strict duplicate-key parsing at artifact-spec entry points, versioned schema validation, actionable key/path diagnostics and no output overwrite on invalid specs. Use ordered content blocks/sections where paragraphs and bullets interleave; do not rely on repeated dictionary keys to express order. Include a source/spec/output content receipt and ground the final inventory in that receipt. A system-prompt admonition alone cannot protect a permissive parser. Do not reject repeated keys in distinct valid objects or ordinary user attachments indiscriminately.
- **Benefit/tradeoffs/effort:** prevent silent loss before files are made; surface a short correctable specification error. Strictness may expose previously tolerated bad specs and needs a migration path for existing saved inputs. Estimate 0.5–1 day for strict parsing/diagnostics and focused regression; 2–3 days for ordered-block coverage receipts across document routes, with scope refined during implementation.
- **Acceptance:** the exact task837 fixture must fail before publication/output mutation with the duplicate section/key identified; its corrected ordered specification must retain all five paragraphs and accepted-document lines. Test equal-valued duplicate keys, nested duplicates, valid same-named keys in separate sections, schema omissions and final-inventory consistency. Track duplicate-spec prevention, source-content omission rate and first-pass artifact success, separately from visual/branding checks. Private evidence: `task837-duplicate-json-proof.json`, `task837-current-parser-proof.json` and canonical file receipt.
- Related existing findings: two converter import failures are masked by `| tail ... && ls`; PYTHONPATH change cannot create the deferred file. Exact staging repairs rendering in 9.16 seconds. Repeated helper inventories/validation precede a 1.18-second generator. These extend H-007/H-016/H-020, without a new generic Python defect. The final offers unrelated project examples from context; relevant grounding versus source facts should remain explicit under H-040.

### H-057 — Malformed question batches silently lose questions and choices

**Priority:** P2. **Classification:** confirmed production behaviour and current implementation defect. **Confidence:** high for normalization loss; user-time impact is observed, but not all the delay is caused by this defect.

- **Example:** task 1105 (`4ac42dee-7c0c-43df-ae31-4aa7169bcd1a`), run `070d8c13-fed6-4a28-8f68-a51da981e448`, question step `bcf7d580-f1dc-4167-bada-d7b0dbeb3f7f`, October 9. The model supplies three items with `label` instead of required `question`. Each has meaningful format/style/event choices, plus a top-level legacy question. Berry silently discards all three and displays one legacy open-text question with **zero choices**. The user repeats the already supplied Halloween event.
- **Frequency:** a bounded structural scan of recorded monthly question events finds **317 steps, 305 with a questions array; one malformed batch, one task and one user**. Event time is step start, otherwise completion, otherwise run creation; this is separate from monthly task correctness and does not certify the relevance of all other questions. This batch is read in full and causally replayed, not merely a scanner candidate.
- **Cause/current status:** `durableQuestionItems` drops invalid children and falls back to the root question; its schema advertises required `question`/no extra properties, but execution does not enforce that contract. Local source `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:5321` and the captured compiled function reproduce the exact one-question/no-options result offline. Fresh SSM at **2026-10-13T16:15:21Z** confirms deployed runner SHA-256 `c030d2cab85b11ee48d41c589e9963890f4fd429bcf4b9b7cc9d291740ecf050`. No fix has been applied.
- **Proposed fix/benefit:** validate the complete built-in tool argument schema before committing a waiting question. Return a bounded, field-specific repair error for a malformed batch instead of silently dropping entries; use legacy normalization only when no batch was supplied. Preserve valid legacy clients and canonical answer IDs. Avoid asking the user to repair model schema mistakes. This also supports a shared validation boundary for other built-in tools.
- **Tradeoffs/effort:** strict validation may expose formerly tolerated malformed callers; retain explicit legacy compatibility. Avoid guessing that an arbitrary label is question text. About **0.5–1 day** for validation and diagnostics, with another day if shared tool schema plumbing must be introduced. No observed missing options should be reconstructed as an answered choice.
- **Verification:** replay the exact label-only payload and mixed valid/invalid children; assert no waiting state or partial UI is committed until repaired. Verify valid multi-question options/multi-select, root-only legacy questions, blank/oversized inputs, preserved answers and bounded repair failure in a focused UI/worker integration test. Measure malformed-to-valid repair rate, silently dropped question count (target zero), repeated user information and avoidable human waits. Private proof: `task1105-question-schema-proof.json`; deployed extracted-function hash `76ffc4ae73aa1171ff37997be435d272708095ee300ec8fd327be77f26629be1`.
- **Implementation (14 October):** ask_user_question arguments are validated against the declared schema before anything is shown; a malformed batch fails the step with a field-specific repair message (bounded by the repair budget) instead of a reduced question, and legacy single questions are unchanged. Not deployed.

- **H-032 cache investigation, full captured monthly usage cohort:** offline reconciliation now separates **33,269 successful main-model usage events / 3,691 run-model groups**, drawn from the already captured 36,990 usage events. This is a complete metrics cohort, **not additional completed conversational review**. Metric is **sum(provider-reported cache-read input tokens) / sum(provider-reported input tokens)**, separately by model; an any-hit call rate is not this metric. First observed call in a run is not necessarily cold upstream.

| Routed model | First observed call in run | Later calls in same run | Later calls with all tracked prior messages retained, stable tools/system/first-message hashes and usage-event gap ≤60s |
|---|---:|---:|---:|

| Qwen 3.8 Flash Next | 0.23% (338) | 16.32% (3,824) | 21.43% (2,075) |
| MiniMax M3 main model | 0.79% (156) | 43.04% (1,075) | 46.16% (636) |
| Kimi K2.6 | 10.47% (139) | 56.34% (696) | 61.63% (454) |
| Gemini 3.7 Flash | 1.36% (159) | **89.56% (780)** | **90.66% (589)** |

It therefore excludes tracked history deletion/rewrite and changing tools, but cannot prove upstream tokenization, backend identity, affinity or cache eligibility. Gaps are between usage events and include model/tool/human time; **≤60s is not a measured provider TTL**. DeepSeek has 371 main events where cache reporting is false; absence is unknown, not a certified miss. Do not infer equal provider/model cache semantics or causation from these observational groups.
- **Confirmed recorded instability:** within that stable-history cohort, **3,729 transitions across 940 runs / 577 tasks** drop from prior cached/input ≥80% to current <50% **and fewer absolute cache-read tokens**, with both events reporting cache data. That absolute condition excludes a ratio decline caused only by newly appended input. Counts by model: DeepSeek 3,194; Qwen 377; MiniMax 134; Kimi 9; Gemini 15. **302 transitions occur from October 10 onward; latest is October 13 at 04:58:49Z**, so this is not only an early-deployment observation. Example task1229 (`e5abfe44-8716-4edf-b9bd-c7ec7f81d65d`), run `825f41f2-0c7d-40e5-99c8-401dbf08e891`: 21,504/22,136 cached tokens → 10,240/23,175 in 5.974 seconds while tracked prior messages/tools are retained. These are telemetry transitions, not 577 failed deliverables or proof of a gateway defect.
- **Cause confidence:** high for measured usage/hash behaviour; **unknown for the remaining provider/gateway cause**. Prefix stabilization is still useful across turns, but cannot alone explain these within-run gaps. Deployed `prompt-cache.js` hashes `serializeMessage`, and local `packages/router-client/src/index.ts:1104` uses the same serializer for outbound messages. Local `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:4023` sets `cacheEligible = planned eligibility OR observed cache hit`, so this field is not an independent eligibility denominator. Existing manifests omit physical upstream routing/cache residency; avoid declaring expiry merely from an app-side retention label.

Those direct-provider rules **do not establish Canopy Wave/gateway behaviour**. The reviewed [Canopy Wave compatibility documentation](https://canopywave.com/docs/get-started/openai-compatible) documents API compatibility, but does not establish a cache affinity/TTL contract. Do not reuse stale fixed-minimum/expiry assumptions without the actual routed model's contract.
- **Proposed next work after approval:** capture redacted wire-prefix/component hashes, real upstream/backend/cache identity, eligibility separately from hits, cache hit/miss token fields, request/response IDs and true time-to-first-token. Design a bounded synthetic append-only benchmark per actual route with stable tools, controlled prompt lengths/delays and a no-cache baseline; reconcile provider counters with invoice/usage data and test one changed component at a time. Confirm supported session/tenant affinity with the gateway before setting any field. Use that evidence to choose routing, serialization or prefix changes. Target ≥85–90% input tokens served from cache for demonstrated reusable/warm cohorts; report all-traffic/cold traffic separately and expected cold ceiling. Gemini already meets the warm target in this cohort. No traffic switch, paid replay, cache padding, keepalive or cross-tenant response reuse is performed/recommended to inflate the metric. Estimate **1–2 days** for diagnostics and a bounded benchmark, then provider-dependent work; tradeoffs are extra telemetry and trace retention, so redact content and preserve tenant isolation.
