It also requires narrative stages to be priced before the pricing gate, while fee placeholders flow forward to that later gate. The latter can be resolved through provisional workload pricing, but that state needs an explicit contract. **Fresh read-only production data confirms the original installed ID remains enabled and unchanged since September 14**, package hash `f6545928855f8274c515d236cddf1db2def9af0dd37d2c3d05420f0b363afffe`, main-content SHA-256 `b8c215c589b6f58dca4d51322a8e21ebddb1b17e3323a23c8d01afb0592c7975`, reference SHA-256 `beee0071e188f7ad58e496a33218ad30bc3054d238b4d1c27ca33745ed723476`. This is an unresolved **instruction/validation risk**, not proof of an incorrectly priced delivered proposal. Four recorded activation steps occur in two other task histories; those activations alone do not establish successful full-pipeline execution.
- Local `apps/worker/src/personal-skills/tools.ts:109` parses skill metadata and persists the package; it does not reconcile workflow defaults or cross-skill commercial invariants. Existing save validation should add declared dependency/resource checks and useful diagnostics, plus a bounded mock/synthetic handoff test for complex orchestrators. Keep a single explicit allowance object shared by workload, narrative and pricing; distinguish planned estimates, reviewed fees and final approved numbers. Estimate **1–2 days** for dependency/reference validation and **2–3 days** for a reusable handoff fixture, with domain-owner review of professional defaults. Semantic linting should warn on ambiguity instead of claiming proof of correctness or blocking every simple prose skill. Verification should catch the one-versus-two review fixture, resolve in-scope/unpriced transitions, preserve approved asset versions and avoid sending a bid until the already defined user/professional gate. No skill, professional policy or production configuration is changed. Evidence: `task69-current-skill-proof.json` and recorded package writes; downstream proposal files are not independently adjudicated here.

### H-058 — Placeholder checks reject legitimate user content and trigger repair loops

**Confirmed defect, P2; current production package verified.** Task 366 (`7ffdc623-f744-4a6b-9ac0-db1921617417`, September 21; run `2a3d7fc8-0a2e-4e82-b80e-cfd25991a350`) requests a ten-field security-consultancy task card including the exact field **END CLIENT NAME**. The validator rejects Task Card!A4 as placeholder text. Berry inspects the workbook and validator, then renames the requested field to END CLIENT and rebuilds. This is avoidable preparation and an unrequested content change; the final workbook does publish. The trace separately repairs an unstaged converter and two font gates, so the whole 363-second run must not be attributed to this regex alone.

**Cause and confidence:** [PLACEHOLDER_RE](deploy/skills/aesg-branding/scripts/validate_artifact.py:17) includes an unanchored, case-insensitive word-boundary match for “client name”; [validate_xlsx](deploy/skills/aesg-branding/scripts/validate_artifact.py:553) applies it to every string cell without differentiating template slots, labels, quoted instructions and filled values. The same regex scans DOCX, PPTX and PDF text. Fresh AWS SSM database-byte verification at 2026-10-13T16:50:47Z confirms the published 30,427-byte validator still matches local SHA256 `d142096704673409fa881d5e2abd8aa975a4ff5959fb3160b1926bc32728bdba`. An offline execution of this exact implementation rejects both END CLIENT NAME and “Client name as stated in the RFP”, accepts END CLIENT, and still rejects lorem ipsum and Insert title. High confidence in this task's cause and present code behavior; no generic claim that every placeholder diagnostic is a defect.

**Frequency and limits:** the complete monthly step scan finds **72 bash stdout/error observations containing actual placeholder diagnostics across 50 tasks and 22 users**. Source-code/grep echoes were excluded. These are diagnostic observations, including masked-success commands; they are neither 72 independent failures nor 50 confirmed false positives. Only task 366 is independently reproduced as causal here; broader outcomes remain subject to the end-to-end ledger and source checks.

**Proposed fix / benefit / effort:** use template-owned slot IDs or explicit markers for hard blockers; preserve supplied field labels and substantive text. Turn ambiguous legacy keyword matches into a located warning with the matched text and an intentional-content acknowledgement, rather than making the model alter user wording. Add a content-aware validation receipt before rebuilding. Approximately 1–2 days for the first validator/receipt change and focused regressions; additional template migration can follow. Tradeoff: weaker generic heuristics can miss a forgotten placeholder, so retain known template-slot checks and genuine unresolved-marker tests rather than removing validation.

**Acceptance:** END CLIENT NAME, a normal “client name” sentence and a quoted instruction preserve exact text and pass; an unresolved template slot, lorem ipsum and actual specimen text still block. Cover XLSX labels and values plus DOCX/PPTX/PDF text. Re-run the task-card fixture with zero label changes and no keyword-driven repair calls. Measure false-positive warnings, repair count and preparation time separately from domain/content correctness.

- **H-007/H-043/H-019/H-020/H-017, task 366:** all retained messages/tool payloads/source PDF extracted text and final publication receipt are read. The source's unnamed end client, absent project size and missing deadline are correctly surfaced, and no unsupported owner is supplied as fact. “Active tender” is inferred from the provided scope/offer request rather than independently verified current procurement status. Native workbook rendering/content was not independently inspected. The two font gates are caused by the arrow glyph absent from the installed Verdana cmap; repeated cache rebuilding does not fix that glyph coverage. Berry eventually replaces the arrow, obtains a Verdana-only PDF and publishes. Recommend glyph-aware permitted fallback diagnostics or deterministic symbol substitution before conversion, while preserving accessibility and required scripts; this is a known-glyph/font-policy case distinct from missing fonts and inherited theme furniture.

- **H-027 follow-up:** an offline fixture runs the exact current local composer `filesFromDataTransfer` function on direct and fallback directory-like zero-byte File payloads, a legitimate empty text file and a normal PDF. Both directory-like cases pass through unchanged; the directory-entry inspection function is never called. Local API initiation/completion accept nonnegative zero-byte sizes and intentionally identify zero bytes as application/octet-stream; the captured deployed API initiation guard also accepts zero. This narrows the missing directory/empty-input UX contract, but does not establish the historical browser's directory representation or current deployed web parity. Preserve legitimate empty files, distinguish unsupported directory drops with ZIP/import guidance, and prevent empty inputs from becoming substantive review findings. Browser-specific end-to-end reproduction is still pending.

- **H-029/H-039/H-020/H-008/H-016, task 200 (`9f10d05f-64b3-4175-81e9-d090bb58fae6`, September 16):** the complete retained trace shows a simple “remove extra curves around step 6” request becoming a 15.3-minute run with no final accepted cleanup. The first healthy image generation takes 59 seconds and publishes a provisional image automatically; recorded decoding changes the original 1062×680 RGBA canvas to 1024×1024 RGB. The model notices broad drift and conflicting visual observations, then spends the remaining work on increasingly elaborate crops, masks and ASCII/topology analysis without producing the promised local edit. Five image inspections consume about 155.6 seconds; three finish at their token limit, and the final mask response contains only 286 content characters. Statements that geometry is now precise are followed by more preparation. This supports precision-edit routing, bounded investigation and complete vision receipts; it does not prove every generated-image pixel comparison is valid (the logged comparison resizes and drops alpha). Canonical source/final pixels were not independently inspected in this task.
- **Task 200 cancellation/dependencies:** the run/model call records cancellation at 06:26:09Z, with no terminal error or final message. The cancellation actor/reason is not established by this transcript; do not count it as a model failure or label it a confirmed intentional user abandonment. Missing cv2 is initially masked by a subsequent successful shell command, then scipy fails normally; a capability probe confirms Pillow/NumPy present and cv2/skimage absent. These are optional-library preparation choices, not proof that Python execution is broken. Prefer a cheap runtime capability receipt and supported bounded image-operation helper; add needed heavy dependencies only after workload/size benchmarking. A precision-edit acceptance test should preserve canvas, alpha and all pixels outside the approved mask, and measure time-to-usable-output rather than provisional generation alone.

### H-059 — XLSX generator fixes row height despite wrapped long text (confirmed current defect, P2)

- **Evidence and impact:** task 529 (`ca5858cd-3e7e-44de-b194-50a7720ac103`, September 25; run `3eba629e-c673-4ab2-8b29-a8fa216fa792`, 307.76 seconds) converts an electrical SD comments DOCX into two sheets. All 29 messages and captured payloads are read. The native 17,308-byte published XLSX is retrieved via read-only SSM (SHA256 `ed112870e1c3fb70c5b2bcfb6f46b51996043b29a3e2bfd3bc6aab35aa133556`), along with the 25,516-byte source DOCX (`102650de3e10463a3ef1ddf0672c63449460e4e704f45fa60351b86435dac977`). All 153 nonempty source paragraphs (26 section headings plus 127 items) match final cells after explicitly allowing whitespace/list-label normalization. Literal Unicode escapes are absent: the apparent transcript escape issue is disproved for this artifact. The workbook stores the content, but every data row is 19.5 points with wrap enabled; the requested 65-character comment column is silently clamped to width 45. Long comments therefore do not fit their cell geometry. An independently rendered private copy visibly clips multiple comments. The audit's local LibreOffice/font environment produces four pages versus production's recorded three, so this is a reproduction of the layout failure, not an assertion of identical production pixels or native Excel display certification.
- **Cause/current status/confidence:** `deploy/skills/xlsx/scripts/create_aesg_xlsx.py:132` clamps widths to 45 and line 149 assigns 19.5 points to every data row regardless of wrapped lines. The current production script is rechecked via SSM and matches local SHA256 `cff9548f455fb9225568ab1248f945ca84dfcff96d7c75299656544facee0ca7` (9,845 bytes, unchanged since August 27). High confidence in current geometry defect. The current XLSX validator checks placeholders, declared font, author, sheets and dimensions; it does not validate text fit. The production overview ends with `finishReason=length` and warns of apparent strikethrough/poor legibility. Berry checks `font.strike == 0` and dismisses the warning as banding, which does not establish that wrapped text fits. This also extends H-019/H-039.
- **Frequency:** one independently source/artifact-verified failing layout example so far. A monthly command-exposure scan finds 351 bash steps across 137 tasks containing `create_aesg_xlsx.py`, `--spec` and `--output` (313 outer completed, 38 failed). Those are exposure/review candidates, not 137 proven clipping failures, successful generator invocations or fully reviewed histories. Private receipts: `task529-native-workbook-proof.json`, `task529-current-generator-proof.json`, `xlsx-generator-monthly-exposure.json`.
- **Proposed fix/benefit/tradeoffs/effort:** support explicit row heights and a measured wrapped-text layout mode; respect validated user/spec widths or return a clear effective-width warning. Size rows using font/column metrics or leave heights for supported viewer auto-fit, then validate the actual export. This makes comments readable without editing row heights manually. Expect larger page counts for long registers; never delete/shorten source text to force a fit. About 1–2 days for generator/contract changes, plus 1–2 days for Excel/LibreOffice export fixtures and a final-version QA gate.
- **Meaningful validation:** use one-line, 200+ character, nested-parent, multiline, wide/narrow, formula and RTL/CJK cells. Compare cell text to source, verify requested/effective width, and inspect the resulting Excel/LibreOffice/PDF layout for clipping. Measure first usable artifact latency and source items readable in the delivered export; token presence and zero strikethrough flags alone are insufficient.
- **Implementation (14 October):** the generator keeps requested widths up to 100 (reporting any adjustment in a `warnings` list) and sizes header/data rows to their wrapped lines with the old heights as minimums; LibreOffice renders confirm the clipped rows now show all their text. Not published; the organization xlsx package needs a sync.

- **H-007/H-018/H-019/H-020, task 529 additional evidence:** the XLSX package contains no embedded logo images (confirm in the native receipt), although the final claims a logo. The generator's `add_logo` silently skips a missing logo path, and this run stages a template but not the separate logo the generator reads. A generated workbook is the documented privacy-safe route; do not solve this by copying the old template's hidden employee sheet. Declare the logo as a required dependency for logo-bearing output, stage only that asset, report whether it was applied and validate its presence when the task/format requires it; allow an explicitly requested unbranded mode. About 0.5–1 day within dependency-closure work, with missing/valid/invalid-logo and no-hidden-employee-data fixtures. A missing `office_converter` fails behind `tail` with outer exit zero; staging the dependency repairs it. Preparation and model intervals dominate the roughly five-minute task while the workbook generation takes 2.1 seconds. Current dependency staging and error normalization need direct tests; the existence of a bundled converter is already established.

For a 218,789-byte attached document-number list, Berry first reads a 50KB/1,220-line preview, then runs a strict regex that omits one of the nine document-number fields. `grep -cE` returns zero and exit one, stopping the `&&` sequence. A CRLF-normalizing field extraction then succeeds over the full file, yielding eight unique codes and 4,800 document rows; the displayed counts sum correctly and the header is excluded. This is recovered model-written shell logic, not a Python/network outage or lost-input failure.
- **Task 320 proposal/positive outcome:** for an explicit deterministic extraction/count request, provide a small file-analysis recipe that validates the requested field/pattern and distinguishes no matches from execution failure, returns counts/exclusions/source coverage and avoids feeding a large preview into the model when filesystem analysis suffices. About 0.5–1 day as a shared optional recipe, with malformed lines, CRLF, blank headers and zero-match fixtures; measure model-facing input tokens, repair calls and exact source-derived counts. Keep the short answer short: “one unique value after deduplication” is clearer than “no true duplicates,” which can imply that the source has no repeats. The screenshot explanation and subsequent Arabic response remain on the requested subject, with speculative motives labelled; no confirmed context/translation defect is established. The attached source file and screenshot pixels are not independently audited here, and this task is a positive end-to-end recovery example rather than evidence of unresolved runtime failure.


### H-060 — Re-reading an older image does not make it eligible for the next inspection (P2)

- **Evidence and impact:** task 219 (`a47ed4fa-ce6b-46ed-8e4a-1da099b5f246`, run `0a3c4acf-b1d7-439e-9045-accd83bfafc9`, September 16) reads six photo candidates together. Berry then says Emara's first candidate was not shown, rereads it successfully, still cannot verify it, receives `duplicate_tool_call_suppressed`, and copies the unchanged image to `headshot-check.jpg` before proceeding. This approximately 60-second sequence ends in recovery. Current deployed compiled `sandbox-continuity.js` (captured SHA256 `0978f3bb5e56366b4fbde40a3211a76ac241b74a94d8c9bae72729c3a7e80759`) and local `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:2057` deduplicate image paths by **first** occurrence and then keep the last five. Executing that captured function on this task's retained steps excludes the specifically reread first image; the new filename becomes eligible. The read receipt promises attachment to the next request without confirming actual inclusion.
- **Frequency and confidence:** the fixed monthly dataset has **60 completed repeated-image-read candidate steps / 23 runs / 20 tasks / 11 users**, latest October 13 at 02:50:33Z. Fresh SSM checks confirm all 23 admitted requests have `modelAcceptsImages=true` and no auxiliary vision configuration. These counts measure selector exposure, **not 60 proven failed inspections**; other histories remain subject to their coverage status. Code behavior is confirmed and the task 219 loop is observed; the exact historical provider wire body is unavailable, so the causal contribution remains likely, not absolute. Task 168 also has two reread source pages excluded by this selector, qualifying its repeated visual-review assertions rather than proving every final slide defective.
- **Status/cause:** unresolved in the captured current deployed selector; historical run-version identity and actual wire-image bytes are not retained. `new Set(paths)` retains first insertion order, so rereading an existing path never updates recency. The five-image cap itself is a valid bound; the defect is failing to prioritize the explicit renewed inspection request and failing to explain exclusion. H-036/H-046 cover related history/image outcomes, but this is a separately reproduced selection mechanism.
- **Proposed fix, tradeoff and effort:** prioritize explicit requested images and deduplicate historical paths by their most recent occurrence. Give each preparation/read an inclusion receipt or a precise instruction to use the existing explicit-path `inspect_images` route. A safe cached read can retain/reuse its observation without requiring a filename copy; do not claim a suppressed read has inspected pixels. Keep image count/encoded-byte/tenant constraints, file validation and supported-model rules. Approximately 1–2 days including selection/receipt integration. Better recency can displace an older reference, so keep intentional source-versus-output comparisons explicit and report omitted paths. This should reduce cosmetic file copies, repeated reads and false visual-verification claims; saved time is a hypothesis until measured.
- **Meaningful verification:** exercise the actual next provider request after six distinct reads and an explicit reread of the first; verify its image identity/hash is included, total image budget remains bounded and no file rename is needed. Cover repeated paths in a concurrent batch, missing/corrupt files, attachments competing for the same budget, text-only models and duplicate-read recovery. Measure requested-image inclusion, avoidable reads/copies and time to verified image. Prefer self-recovery; a user selection control is appropriate only when several genuinely ambiguous photos require a human choice. Private receipts: `task219-image-recency-fixture.json`, `image-recency-selector-monthly-candidates.json`, `image-recency-candidate-admitted-image-policy.json`.

- **Task 219 additional H-018/H-020 evidence:** all 50 messages, one completed Qwen run and 68,337 transcript characters are read. Generation first rejects string-valued `source_quality_flags`; after two code searches and edits to both specifications, the same command succeeds approximately 96 seconds later, generating all eight CV files in 21.35 seconds. The current local guide only shows empty `source_quality_flags`/`warnings` arrays, while the preparer requires objects for quality flags and converts warning objects into Python representation strings. Document element types and provide a machine-readable preflight schema plus structured field-specific errors (about 0.5–1 day within H-018), preserving the distinction between flags and warning strings. Fresh SSM lookup by the actual owner/name confirms the enabled current personal skill is `skill_f1bb67ac-9695-4f16-8d22-6131f3f1b706` (metadata last updated September 23). Its guide (SHA256 `2a333ce63c66417f2c9626456988e5203ef7afd229888b471ab64ecf3e421a27`) explicitly documents object flags and warning strings, and its preparer (SHA256 `4f7570f10d74d8402708b9a98603cc037180de04c272070845916e88b40480dd`) normalizes plain-text flags and emits field-specific errors. **The observed quality-flag mismatch is fixed in this current personal route; do not recommend implementing that normalizer again.** The legacy local/org contract still differs and requires route-specific parity before extending the recommendation. ### H-061 — Image inspection accepts a question that is silently discarded (P2 harness contract)

- **Confirmed current code behavior:** `inspect_images` defines optional `mode` (default overview) and an optional `question`, but the deployed executor sends that question to the vision provider only for focused mode. Omitted mode and explicit overview instead send the generic reusable-observation prompt. The schema does not explain that the question is ignored. The main-model instructions already ask for focused mode for specific questions; another general system-prompt reminder alone will not enforce this contract.
- **Monthly exposure, not incorrect-answer frequency:** all 3,403 retained monthly inspection steps are scanned. **703 completed calls in 447 runs / 296 tasks / 100 users** supplied a nonempty question while returning overview: 685 explicit overview and 18 omitted mode. Another 100 failed and three cancelled calls have a non-focused question argument; their errors/cancellations are not attributed to question discard. Some questions request a broad description and the generic digest may suffice. Latest exposure is October 12, 18:30:12 UTC. This scan does not mark the 296 conversations reviewed or establish 703 wrong observations.
- **Concrete fully reviewed example:** task 142 (`026c00bb-775a-418b-90ce-d0da9824ffed`, September 15–16) supplies four questions about exact text, section headings and figure numbering, always omitting mode; all four outputs report overview. The answers are mostly usable, so this establishes silently disregarded arguments and unnecessarily broad observations, not four failed captions. Source and crop QA questions in the wider scan require separate conversation/source checks before assigning outcome impact.
- **Cause, status and confidence:** `apps/worker/src/vision-tools.ts:201` defaults everything except focused to overview, and line 245 chooses `DEFAULT_OVERVIEW_QUESTION`. The definition at `turn-runner.ts:6096` accepts both fields independently. Read-only SSM at **2026-10-13T18:38:41.941Z** verifies the current worker's compiled vision module SHA256 `da5e6502d35491b8d1d7843b7d1462bb48e9bb941eaba3a237cf10445ba0de54` and its matching branch. An offline replay of the actual compiled executor with provider/cache seams mocked proves the question is absent for omitted/overview cases and present for focused. High confidence in current contract behavior; medium confidence in avoidable cost/quality impact, which must be measured. No production model inference or mutation is performed.
- **Related cache correctness:** the compiled first focused inspection uses turn input rather than the actual question in its cache identity. Two distinct first questions with identical turn input/images produce the same cache key in the fixture. This is a confirmed key-collision condition, **not an observed wrong production cache answer**; the baseline has no focused observation-cache hits. Include the effective question and any required context in focused identity. Preserve tenant-scoped cache access; no cross-tenant disclosure is demonstrated.
- **Proposal / benefit / tradeoffs / effort:** normalize omitted mode plus a nonempty question to focused. For explicit overview with a question, define whether the question is honored in a bounded digest or return a precise argument diagnostic; update the tool schema and effective-mode receipt accordingly. Preserve the questionless reusable overview path and existing image/output limits. Approximately **1–2 days** for the contract, cache identity and regression fixtures. Expected benefit is predictable visual queries and fewer broad digests/repair calls. Narrow questions can reduce generic-cache reuse and focused output currently has a smaller cap, so coordinate with H-039 rather than blindly converting every broad query or increasing tokens.
- **Acceptance:** instrument the actual provider request and cache lookup for omitted, explicit overview, focused, no-question and invalid-mode cases. No supplied question may be silently discarded; any rejection must explain the exact field correction. Different focused questions must not reuse an unrelated observation. Check exact figure/date/table queries, length-truncated outputs and meaningful cache reuse while measuring provider tokens, latency, repeat inspections and answer accuracy. User-facing modals are unnecessary for an argument problem the harness can normalize.
- **Private evidence:** `monthly-vision-question-discard-exposure.json`, `vision-question-contract-fixture.json`, `vision-question-current-deployment-proof.json`. The counts are static-window exposures, separate from the full conversation ledger.
- **Implementation (14 October):** a question with no mode now runs as focused; explicit overview keeps the reusable digest but returns `questionSent: false` with an instruction to use focused mode if needed; the tool schema describes both fields. The focused cache identity is unchanged (see H-039). Not deployed.

All **35 messages / 12 completed DeepSeek runs / six successful tool calls / 69,095 captured transcript characters** in task `026c00bb-775a-418b-90ce-d0da9824ffed` are read end to end, including the complete raw email read and four image observations. ### Fully reviewed task 653 — CV recovery loops and source-check diagnostics

**H-041 / H-055 / H-016 / H-019 / H-020:** task `e9370047-122e-4cd6-96b4-4a2c7372a5ee`, September 29, run `e19b46b5-f833-4ce0-8335-49a5f354828f`, is read completely: **65 messages / 69,341 captured characters / 38 tools / 26 model iterations**, ending completed after **749.62 seconds**. Four tool failures are recorded: portrait-ratio rejection, an image-provider 400 for dimensions not divisible by 16, and two overwrite refusals. Four CV files are eventually generated and published, with successful structural/content parity. This is successful recovery with excessive preparation, not an unrecovered Python/network/MCP fault.

The 3:4 image request is coupled with 1024×1536 (2:3) size, which the CV photo helper correctly rejects for its 7:9 target. The first helper writes the square output before failing on portrait; the corrected retry then refuses to overwrite that partial file. Removing only the private temporary output directory recovers it, but Berry later repeats the already completed preparation and gets a second refusal. This corroborates H-055's existing partial-output issue. Current `sandbox-continuity.ts:3206` maps both 3:4 and 9:16 to 1024×1536 and passes arbitrary explicit sizes through at line 1154; the provider's supported-dimension error is useful input feedback, not an upstream outage. Prefer provider-specific dimension validation and an actual-ratio receipt, plus photo-helper preflight/atomic outputs and a reusable successful preparation receipt. Retain safe crop rejection rather than forcing distortion. This example does not establish that every image provider requires the same size grid.

Two source-coverage failures are hidden behind a successful shell status: a generator pipeline ends in `tail`, and a direct verifier followed by `echo EXIT=$?` reports the echo's exit status. Berry notices the content failures, so there is no falsely published incomplete CV from those attempts. The first gate reports only the number of unused exclusions; resolving it requires another source/script investigation. A subsequent gate incorrectly treats a column-merged “Work Experience” heading plus an already-preserved qualification fragment as missing content. An exact, explained exclusion repairs it without dropping the qualification. Add located unmatched-exclusion diagnostics and column/heading-aware evidence within the existing H-019 work; retain fail-closed coverage rather than weakening it to suppress all warnings. Measure first successful generation time, duplicate preparation calls, specific gate diagnoses and preserved source facts.

Task `09714c5a-5dd4-4235-ac8e-0deba0a87296`, September 29, run `8ce9164a-2271-41b5-8815-b6c9e9820827`, is fully read: **46 messages / 63,032 captured characters / 28 tools / 17 model iterations / 461.89 seconds**, ending completed with a 5,284,483-byte Word file. Berry's draft applies a fixed control-circuit test voltage without identifying the actual panel or those component restrictions. This supports a missing device-specific verification requirement, not a finding that Schneider is the actual supplier or that this unidentified equipment was damaged. Other individual electrical clauses and standard applicability remain unadjudicated.

**Recommendation, confidence and acceptance:** extend existing H-017/H-025/H-040 work with an equipment-scoped provenance fixture and a draft-status/required-input receipt. A short request may produce a useful draft, while clearly separating supplied facts, proposed generic steps, source-required limits and unverified equipment fields. Do not present two cosmetic placeholders as the only items needing confirmation when supplier, technical criteria, issue date and promised appendices also remain unresolved. Keep publication possible for a draft; offer an actionable review list instead of an automatic modal on every technical document. Approximately **1–2 days** for the fixture, completeness manifest and scope/assumption contract within the existing grounding work; domain validation and licensed source integration may require additional specialist time. Tradeoffs are extra source retrieval and occasional input questions. Acceptance must prevent DX-supplier carryover, distinguish index versus included sheets, retain qualified/source-bound technical values, and preserve useful generic drafting without asserting approved operational instructions. Measure unsupported entity transfers, missing promised sections and time to a usable draft, separately from successful generation.

### H-062 — Failed indexing leaves files permanently “processing” and conceals recovery (P2)

**Confirmed current state defect; high confidence.** A fresh SSM read at **2026-10-13 19:11:21 UTC** finds **249 of 6,744 currently undeleted files created in the audit month** still marked `files.status='processing'` while an active knowledge source is already `index_status='failed'`. They belong to **39 users** and are associated with **73 tasks in the monthly manifest**; those associations are not 73 demonstrated task failures or additional complete conversation reviews. There are 246 uploads and three connector imports. By detected media type: 117 PNGs, 62 JPEGs, 35 octet-stream files, 29 PDFs, three ZIPs and three WebPs. The newest affected file was created **October 13, 04:44:24 UTC**, so this is not solely old backlog.

**Concrete end-to-end example:** task `d304dc53-e3f3-410e-929d-2bd18fd941dc` (ordinal 60), run `569a765b-a434-49d6-8d63-9b77358930cc`, successfully extracts participants from a screenshot using the vision tool. Original file `02ce5556-67ef-42e1-ae99-f04eeb2d99e6` remains processing after its source `1bdea7cf-c3e5-4b22-a167-dacf3362ecea` failed at **September 13, 07:53:15 UTC** with “Document extraction produced no text.” The original bytes are verified and usable; this is failed background search preparation, not lost upload content or a failed participant-extraction run.

**Cause and deployment evidence:** upload completion sets global file state to processing when indexing is scheduled (`apps/api/src/files/file-platform.service.ts:473–488`). `KnowledgeProcessor.extract` throws on empty extracted text. `SqlKnowledgeRepository.markFailed` (`apps/worker/src/knowledge/repository.ts:348`) updates the source and workspace-file failure state but leaves the global file status unchanged; the success path sets it available at line 534. The deployed compiled handler is inspected through SSM at **19:10:35 UTC**, SHA256 `311c9d0d777ac476b8307c14672552455d74c118047ac83c54330ef5fb3227da`, and contains the same two writes. The current API can return workspace `indexStatus` and `indexFailureReason` and already supports targeted retry at line 631. The inspected current Library and task-file-dialog components show file metadata/preview/download without indexing state or that recovery control. **No live browser screenshot confirms a spinner**; do not invent one.

The population's stored failure reasons are: **188 empty-text results, 32 Tika HTTP 422 responses, 27 Tika timeouts, one fetch failure and one extraction-output size limit**. Those reasons describe retained failures; they do not establish that a timeout/network repair remains broken or that every empty image requires OCR. A decorative image may have no useful searchable text. Direct vision extraction succeeds for the concrete screenshot.

**Recommendation:** separate durable storage readiness from each workspace's search-readiness state. A verified stored file stays downloadable while indexing is pending, unsupported or failed. Show a compact “Stored; not searchable” state with a clear reason and appropriate next action: targeted retry for transient failures, searchable/OCR input for textless scans when search is needed, different inputs for limits/encryption/parser incompatibility, and administrator diagnostics for service failures. Expose the existing retry API through the relevant file surface with an idempotent job receipt. Reserve a modal for an action that cannot proceed without that file's searchable content; do not block native image use or downloads. Keep source failure details scoped to the authorized workspace/tenant.

**Expected benefit and effort:** approximately **1–2 days** for coherent state/DTO/UI recovery plus a separate evaluation of selective OCR/parser support. The benefit is accurate readiness, less blind retrying, preserved direct use and diagnosable missing project knowledge. Tradeoffs include handling one physical file in multiple workspaces and distinguishing textless-but-valid images from failed extraction. OCR may add cost/latency and should be governed separately; do not automatically reprocess all production files in this investigation phase.

**Acceptance:** empty-image, transient failure, 422, output-limit, deletion and stale-revision fixtures must end in an accurate index state while verified bytes remain accessible. Include the same file in two workspaces, retry-versus-delete races, an already successful text-only index with degraded embedding, and a native vision task that works while search preparation fails. The mismatch count should fall to zero for newly processed files; measure terminal-index latency, actionable error coverage and first successful targeted recovery. Report storage completion and search completion as separate denominators. Private evidence: `task60-index-state-proof.json`, monthly state baseline/cases/summary and the deployed handler proof. **Unresolved on current deployment; no fix applied.**

- **Implementation (14 October):** a failed index now moves the file from `processing` to `available` (current revision only; quarantine untouched), matching the success path; verified on PostgreSQL. Not deployed. Existing rows need a separately reviewed reconciliation; the UI state and retry control remain.

### Monthly audit: Berry's connector setup guidance needs its actual product contract — task549

### H-063 — Streaming spreadsheet random access wastes minutes and ends without an answer

**P1; confirmed inefficient execution pattern and observed user failure; high confidence in the algorithmic cause, historical CPU attribution unmeasured.** Fully reviewed task **836**, `e6406a29-64eb-4d0d-bed2-37afc5b303ca` (October 5), asks for timelines using existing per-person project assignments at 25/25/15 per day. Its single run `bab71993-de22-40f3-b197-e921c6f64fc9` fails after **33m54s** with no calculated timeline. Two completed bash calls take **1,415.79s** and **500.12s**; the next is rejected by the cumulative tool-time budget. This is not an intentional cancellation, provider outage or lost workbook. Current source at `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:1429` correctly enforces the tool budget; increasing that budget is not the proposed fix.

**Cause:** generated Python opens the workbook with `read_only=True`, then loops through each sheet's `max_row` using repeated `ws.cell(row=r, column=...)`. In openpyxl 3.1.5, each `ReadOnlyWorksheet._get_cell` starts a new streaming worksheet parser, reading through the requested row. Repeating this over growing row numbers creates quadratic work. Source dimensions include 1,000 rows per many sheets even though only 915 entries are listed across 13 sheets. A second script repeats the same access pattern for four sheets and samples. Both scripts finish normally; successful tool status hides severe wasted work until the total budget is exhausted. No unsupported claim is made that all slow Python calls have this cause.

**Independent measurement:** production file `4cc9c11b-6317-4276-934a-3d1cd2a0cc74` is available and its verified immutable blob SHA256 matches the already retrieved 7,132,821-byte source `0722221279657c0891cb0262c3c9d8ac807700d4b834c86de585e2fcf74ea06c`. On private copies, the same first 100 Acoustics rows and three columns give byte-equivalent values: random cell access opens the worksheet **300 times**, versus **once** for `iter_rows(values_only=True)`. Local scan times are about **0.544s versus 0.00335s**; a single-pass count of all 13 sheets including loading takes **0.332s** and reproduces the inclusive 915 count. These are local measurements, not guaranteed production speedups or a historical cloud CPU profile.

**Frequency:** an explicitly limited monthly co-occurrence scan finds **9 candidate tool inputs / 3 tasks / 3 runs / 3 users**, including the budget-blocked next call. It detects `read_only=True`, a range loop and `.cell()` together; header-only loops and unwitnessed script execution are candidates, not confirmed slow incidents. Task836 establishes one causal user failure with two lengthy executed scans. The other histories still require end-to-end adjudication. Latest candidate: October 5. These counts do not replace the full-task coverage ledger.

**Current status:** fresh SSM at **October 13 19:43–19:44 UTC** confirms compiled runner SHA256 `c030d2cab85b11ee48d41c589e9963890f4fd429bcf4b9b7cc9d291740ecf050` retains the budget checks; the published XLSX guide content hash `0afb59ca6cad0d776c4c06a48226c188a2cafe61f914ca366eb7eb6c23d63f0b`, unchanged since August 27, offers generation/branding instructions but no efficient analysis route or streaming-access guidance. No spreadsheet skill is activated in this historical task. No current production replay demonstrates recovery from this access pattern; the proposed harness/helper guidance remains unresolved. The separate already-fixed Python invocation/network issues should not be reopened.

**Proposed fix:** provide an analysis-first workbook inventory/aggregation helper with bounded headers, named/populated row counts, exceptional blank/marked entries and one-pass row iteration. Make its small usage contract available when inspecting XLSX inputs; reserve artifact-generation/branding preparation for requested files. Add a targeted performance hint for large streaming-sheet scans using repeated random access, with progress/elapsed-budget feedback and safe strategy changes. Preserve formulas/styles/source bytes and tenant boundaries; do not blanket-block legitimate header-cell reads, silently cap rows or infer that blank QA/QC always means unfinished work. About **0.5–1 day** for guidance/helper and **1–2 days** for measured executor feedback. Expected benefit: fewer loads, much lower processing time and fewer budget failures without weakening timeout safeguards.

**Acceptance:** replay this exact tracker request in an isolated evaluation; reproduce all source assignments/exceptions and calculate each person's workload timeline with stated treatment of unassigned/finished rows. Assert single-pass worksheet access, identical values and no omitted populated rows; measure elapsed CPU/tool time, source opens, model turns and completion before the budget. Include a sparse formatted sheet, style/strike-dependent counts, legitimate small random reads and a genuinely long calculation. Recovery UI should preserve the extracted counts and offer retry using the efficient path, instead of asking the user to repeat the whole investigation. Do not publish a generic timeline when the source's assignment/completion policy is ambiguous.

Private evidence: `task836-stream-cell-performance-proof.json`, `xlsx-stream-random-cell-monthly-candidates.json`, `xlsx-stream-random-cell-monthly-summary.json`, `task836-current-skill-and-file-proof.json`, `task836-current-xlsx-guide-proof.json`, `task836-current-budget-runtime-proof.json`. This is related to H-020 preparation and H-014 recovery, with a distinct measured algorithmic failure. No production change.

### H-064 — Deferred skill staging omits required entry-point dependencies

**P1; confirmed avoidable repair loop; high confidence in the staging/guide gap, candidate frequency not a causal defect count.** Task **821**, `f1938ce4-55c8-46ae-93a9-26abb84acd1b` (October 5), asks to format a supplied job-description DOCX in AESG branding. All **44 messages, one run and 72,339 transcript characters** were read. Run `e3de11c2-aad2-4f64-8c2f-1505c781e1a9` lasts **5m38s** and ends with a model request failure before publication, despite generating and rendering an 11-page document.

**Observed cause:** activation stages `create_aesg_docx.py` and selected branding assets/checkers, but omits the generator's branding imports. The first invocation raises **FileNotFoundError for `scripts/report_structure.py`**. Berry stages that helper and retries; the second raises **ModuleNotFoundError for `normalise_office_templates`**. Only after another activation stages the normalizer and converter does generation succeed in **9.83s**. The first failure to successful third invocation spans roughly 36 seconds, including model/activation/generation time; do not assign the entire run's duration to missing dependencies. Before generation, the model also reads several hundred lines of helper source over about 100 seconds to infer the contract. This is preparation and resource discovery, not an unavailable Python runtime or a need to install existing helpers.

**Implementation/current status:** `apps/worker/src/personal-skills/tools.ts:244` passes exactly the requested resource paths into `stageSkillPackage`; `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:770` selects that set, adds SKILL.md and loads only missing selected files. It verifies content hashes and preserves sandbox/path isolation but has no entry-point or cross-skill dependency expansion. `deploy/skills/docx/scripts/create_aesg_docx.py:1679` requires `report_structure.py`; line 1703 imports the normalizer, and TOC refresh uses the office converter. The canonical guide describes the command and branding directory without specifying this import closure. Fresh read-only SSM at **October 13 20:33:46 UTC** verifies deployed staging SHA256 `10d0e048…` and continuity `0978f3bb…` retain that exact selection behavior. Current DOCX/branding guides, updated September24, have hashes `5700c3ec…` / `8363b241…` and still omit the dependency closure. **Unresolved current contract/staging gap; no current end-to-end replay is claimed.** Merely activating a guide does not guarantee deferred resources exist in the sandbox.

**Frequency:** an anchored monthly actual-exception scan finds **391 bash-step candidates across 261 tasks, 271 runs and 84 users**: 258 mention `office_converter`, 83 `normalise_office_templates`, 51 `report_structure`; one step matches two names. Latest candidate **October13 04:47:33 UTC**. Of these steps, 171 have completed status and 220 failed status. Only 34 associated task histories were fully reviewed at this supplementary scan;821 is now reviewed separately. These counts identify missing-import symptoms, not 391 confirmed dependency-staging defects: wrong paths/cwd and independent uses of a converter require separate adjudication. The exact two successive imports in821 establish the causal case. Other already-reviewed converter incidents remain under H-007 rather than being automatically relabelled.

**Fix and benefit:** give authorized skill entry points a small versioned dependency manifest, including cross-skill imports/assets and conditional render/TOC prerequisites. On selecting an entry point, resolve and stage that declared closure with existing hash/size/path/tenant checks; return a concise ready-to-run command and missing-prerequisite explanation. Validate manifests during package publication and run an isolated minimal-resource smoke fixture. About **1–2 days** for this Office closure/validation, **2–4 days** for a general cross-skill resolver and version-aware receipts. Expected benefit: fewer activation/model turns, shorter preparation and fewer predictable execution errors. Tradeoffs: manifest upkeep and a little extra staging for conditional dependencies. Preserve lazy loading; do not stage every large asset, dump full code into context, install packages to disguise missing bundled files or share private packages between tenants.

**Acceptance:** activate only the canonical DOCX entry point for this task in a fresh sandbox, generate/validate/render with zero missing-import repairs, then repeat on a reused and replaced sandbox. Record bytes staged, activation calls, first useful output, task wall time and input tokens; require unchanged content and declared dependency hashes. Include direct letters, report TOC disabled/enabled, PDF conversion, unknown/missing cross-skill references, package updates, revoked access and hash mismatch. A missing dependency should report the exact recovery action before running the producer, rather than forcing the model to inspect hundreds of lines.

**Related user experience evidence:** both failed commands pipe to `tail`, so the recorded tool status is completed/exit0 although the error text is visible and repaired (H-016). A later focused vision result reports possible divider footer overlap; native pixels are not independently retrieved, so this is an unverified layout observation (H-019). The final model step exhausts three wrapper retries, containing six recorded physical adapter attempts with five server/5xx categories and one rate-limit/4xx category; physical ordinal resets belong to H-033. The task has no final answer or persisted artifact receipt. H-014 should preserve the generated work/check results and offer an explicit resume/review path; recovery must not silently publish an unchecked file. Compact branding without an unnecessary control/TOC/divider package is an additional UX hypothesis for a short job description, not a proved user complaint. Generated issue/review/approval metadata follows guide defaults but is not source-attested (H-017).

### H-065 — Image generation publishes unfinished bases as downloadable task outputs

**P2; confirmed current tool/skill contract mismatch; user confusion and storage savings remain hypotheses.** In fully reviewed task **715**, `1ccaf548-d9f1-4bb5-9652-b27332ca0693` (October1;55 messages, three completed runs,72,593 transcript characters), the user requests a square titled commissioning thumbnail, adds scope text, then explicitly changes style to photorealistic. Berry creates **three image versions** and successfully publishes each finished title/logo composition. Each base generation is also independently persisted and projected as an image before that work, leaving **six published images for three final versions**. The bases intentionally lack the requested title and exact composited logo. No observed lost file, wrong user download or dissatisfaction about the extra bases is claimed; the photorealistic follow-up is an authorized style change, not proof that the original flat-style preference was ignored.

**Cause/current evidence:** `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:1176` always writes generated bytes under outputs, uploads to object storage, calls `persistOutput` and returns a downloadable image/artifact. `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:6900` inserts an image message part for every `create_image` result. The declared tool and system prompt explicitly say generation already publishes. Meanwhile the current AESG image completion gate requires compositing/inspection and **publishing only the final image**. The model cannot fulfill that workflow literally with the current tool contract. Fresh read-only SSM at **October13 20:38:21 UTC** confirms the unconditional publication/projection in deployed continuity `0978f3bb…` and runner `c030d2ca…`. **Unresolved contract gap**, high confidence; live browser grouping/download visibility is not replayed here.

**Frequency:** a monthly co-occurrence scan finds **63 runs /21 tasks /12 users**, containing78 completed generations and81 completed image-extension persistence calls. It detects generation plus image persistence in the same run, not all intermediate files or all intentional variants; separate-image requests and unrelated attachments can match. Task715 provides three directly traced base-to-composite examples. Do not label all63 runs as duplicate-publication defects, and do not add the same generated file's bytes twice to cost/savings estimates.

**Proposed fix:** add an explicit generation purpose/publication contract: immediately published final image for simple requests, preserved draft/base output for declared post-processing, and final artifact receipt linking source/draft/version. The UI can label working images and emphasize the finished download, with previous versions available on demand. Keep private durable recovery storage rather than deleting bases; align skill instructions with the actual tool, and avoid making cleanup remove model-visible references (H-046). About **2–3 days** for draft/final metadata, tool schema and rendering, followed by workflow regressions. Benefit: clearer output choice, fewer unfinished downloads and a usable failure-recovery trail. Tradeoffs: extra lifecycle/version handling and backwards compatibility; durable draft storage still consumes bytes. Do not silently hide a user-requested comparison or change permissions for source images.

**Acceptance/measurement:** run the exact three-version workflow, verify three clear final downloads with titles/logos and preserved links to working versions. A plain image request should still publish immediately; failure after generation should expose an actionable resume/draft-review path rather than lose bytes or pretend completion. Test editing, cancellation, continuation on another worker, missing sandbox files, already-published references and tenant ACLs. Measure unfinished outputs presented as final, wrong-file selection in usability tests, repeated persistence calls and recoverable bytes; native visual quality and subjective preference require their own evaluation.

**Related H-020 / H-019 / H-017 / H-029:** first-run wall time is6m45s, including about108 seconds of user-answer wait; exclude that wait from AI latency. Base generation takes33–36 seconds, while font/package preparation and two vision passes add substantial surrounding work. All seven image observations finish normally; none is another H-039 truncation. Reuse of the selected square/title style across the second request is correct. A later vision response limits fine-detail verification at thumbnail scale, but the final says verified at full size with credible anatomy/no artifacts. Carry that qualification into final claims and report actual inspection dimensions; exact native anatomy/layout is not independently certified here. Deterministic title/logo provenance is established by the commands, rather than by vision alone. No production/source changes.

Private evidence: `task715-current-generated-image-publication-proof.json`, `generated-image-postprocess-publication-monthly-candidates.json`, `generated-image-postprocess-publication-monthly-summary.json`, full task715 transcript and six publication receipts.


### Monthly audit: task1032 — native logo overlap passes a claimed visual check

Task **1032** (`ee38c7fc-e962-4417-b6be-06f98e6485db`), October8: all **74 messages, two completed runs and75,089 transcript characters** read. The first request converts ten Word specifications at exact paper size/format; all ten PDFs publish in~2m. The user then expressly asks that **Qiddiya/RSP logos remain visible and not overlap**. The second~5m57s run studies VML/header XML, reconverts, renders all first-page headers plus one continuation crop, reads the images and publishes ten more PDFs. No tool/provider/cancellation/queue fault prevents either batch.

**H-019 / H-012, confirmed incorrect verification:** independent read-only retrieval verifies the 109,304-byte source and both450,273-byte published Administration PDFs (`89f7f81a…`, `0941c265…`). The source's RSP artwork is intact. Native first-page inspection at150 and600DPI shows the **left stem of RSP's R obscured**. PDF operators explain it: RSP's visible clip is x478.25–514.25pt; Qiddiya's opaque image box is x424.05–485.00pt and is drawn afterward. Their horizontal intersection is **6.75pt over RSP's full13.349pt height**. The white edge of Qiddiya's image covers the letter despite apparent space between the coloured marks. Both versions have identical first-page pixels and all14 decoded page-content streams, so the logo-focused rerun does not repair this document. Berry's final claim that both logos are fully visible/no overlap is disproved. **High confidence** in this specific two-version defect; the other nine PDFs and Word-native positioning are not independently certified. Historical main-model image exposure is not reconstructed from read receipts alone.

**Cause/proposal:** a raw geometric/image-box overlap can be missed by semantic vision, especially where a white image border covers another glyph. Repeated regex/XML probes first target DrawingML, then accidentally capture a nested stroke style before finding the VML shape data. Extend H-019's tested inspection/repair helper to resolve DrawingML/VML anchors, crop fractions, z-order, opaque image rectangles and visible-content bounds. Validate branded header regions against the actual source logos with an appropriately bounded crop; repair only a derivative's placement when authorized, preserving source bytes, sizes and content. About **1–2 days** for this narrow helper/fixture inside the broader artifact QA work. Test this exact two-logo header and variant offsets, white/transparent image borders, deliberate overlapping layouts and multiline title tables. Require the complete RSP glyph and no image-box occlusion, not a generic successful vision call. Measure detected/escaped overlap defects, repair iterations and time to verified output. False positives from intentional layouts require scoped rules, not universal rejection of overlapping shapes.

**H-043 / H-017 / H-029:** captured font inventory genuinely lacks Arial/Times New Roman, and native Administration PDFs embed LiberationSans. Berry discloses substitution but claims identical wraps/tables/pagination without a Microsoft Word reference render; metric compatibility does not prove complete layout identity. Some retained fonte names are inactive theme choices, so do not demand every theme font. A conversion preflight should resolve used fonts/features, disclose the actual limitation and offer an actionable choice for exact-font/exact-layout work, with source-to-output page/content checks. Existing approved font/template capability work applies; no arbitrary font installation or current template change is recommended without license/runtime evidence. Current production replay on these files is pending; recorded historical substitution is confirmed, its present availability state is not newly measured here.

**H-020:** the logo rerun reconverts and republishes all files rather than supplying a scoped validation receipt; two input batches also make a broad inputs glob enumerate20 files during metadata inspection. A deterministic batch manifest can select the ten current inputs, reuse unchanged conversions/checks by tenant+source hash+renderer/font version and verify only changed/header-variant regions. A4 geometry and qpdf structural checks pass, but neither establishes logo correctness or all-page visual parity. Both sets remain downloadable; no file loss is observed. Private evidence: `task1032-native-conversion-proof.json`, three immutable `source-…-proof.json` receipts and private600DPI/header/source-logo images. Existing issue IDs, no implementation/deployment.

Berry reads all four attachments, follows the14-page RFQ pagination and clearly separates the two lots, monitoring triggers, exclusions, duplicated-price risks and clarification requirements. No queue/provider/tool/cancellation failure occurs. A roughly114-second full review and~17-second follow-up summary complete successfully; subsequent shortening runs take~36s/~24.5s.

**H-012 / H-002 / H-020, confirmed misleading measurement:** on October13, Berry says a rewrite is **“roughly40% tighter”** while retaining references. Comparing the exact uploaded text, excluding its “Paragraph2” label, with the actual rewritten body gives **329→308 whitespace words (6.38% shorter)** and **2,478→2,300 characters (7.18% shorter)**. Neither supports40%. The user then requests the specific offered second-paragraph reduction. The first paragraph is byte-identical in that follow-up, and omitted detailed references are explicitly disclosed; that authorized compression is a positive fixture, not meaning loss to count as a failure.

Extend existing constrained-edit evaluations with deterministic word/character receipts and realistic brevity targets. Do not claim percentages without calculating them or satisfy “shorter” by retaining almost the entire source with a confident reduction label. About **0.5–1 day** for this fixture/helper within H-002's existing1–2-day constraint/evaluation work. Define the tokenizer, remove framing from the metric and preserve requested numbers/names/qualifiers. Acceptance: this exact source receives an accurately labelled reduction, explicit named-item preservation where requested, and the follow-up changes only the requested paragraph. Tradeoff: true compression may require marking omitted details or asking about indispensable items; the task does not establish a need to remove all numerical requirements. **Post-prompt-hotfix behavior**, high confidence; the exact current-model prompt replay remains pending.

**Positive controls / boundaries:** the initial review acknowledges that150,000m² is the source's **built-up area** wording rather than silently asserting a separately certified GFA. It flags unnamed owner/award status and start dates instead of inventing area/building totals, and keeps Lot2 instruction/appointment conditions. Some boilerplate still says no licence conditions are stated despite valid-trade-licence/registration qualification requirements: distinguish missing contractual T&Cs from actually specified bidder credentials. Underlying regulatory applicability and native source drawings are not independently certified, and the final narrative should not promote a likely main-contract award into independently verified appointment. The invitation to confirm receipt/bid is a proposed next action; no message is sent. Private evidence: `task1140-rewrite-measurement-proof.json`. Existing issue IDs, documentation only.

### H-066 — Operator recovery reopens a turn with an already settled budget (P1)

**Confirmed current defect, high confidence.** A month-wide exact `session.note` signature finds **five runs /five tasks /four users**, September30–October12, saying **“The budget reservation for this turn is no longer active.”** Every signature follows an explicitly recorded operator tool retry. Tasks **559,983,1158,1171,1187** are the concrete examples. This signature cohort does not mean five permanently failed deliverables: tasks1187,1158 and983 are reviewed end to end here and delivered no requested answer/document; task1171 loses the affected run's answer but recovers in a later fresh turn. All five whole histories are reviewed; task559 and1171 recover in later fresh turns. All five reservations are independently confirmed reconciled before operator retry. All five runs record completed state after that stop. Do not merge this with provider402 or the already fixed September28 budget initialization issue.

**Exact cause and current status:** worker terminal settlement includes `recovery_required` and reconciles the reservation. API `DurableTurnService.recover` reopens the same run/step/finalization and emits an operator-retry event, but never creates/reactivates a budget reservation. Worker `reserveNextModelCall` then correctly refuses a reconciled reservation; `finishForBudget` incorrectly treats this lifecycle refusal as a need to increase allowance and sets task/run completed. Source: `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:3654,3158,2770,7261`; `apps/api/src/runtime/durable-turn.service.ts:1837,1979,2050`. Fresh read-only SSM at **October13 21:10–21:13UTC** verifies worker hash `c030d2ca…`, continuity `0978f3bb…` and API durable-recovery hash `865befad…`; the mismatch remains deployed. This is harness/accounting work, not a system-prompt fix.

For task1187, reservation `d8c855ea-420b-4a2a-ad96-e70934e06826` changes from reserved to **reconciled at08:16:51**, retaining77,557 reserved micros and13,072 actual micros. The org/user ledgers each show the same reservation/reconciliation for their separate scopes. The operator retries at08:20:23 and08:25:41; no reactivation exists. The final denial occurs before checking allowance amounts, so raising allowance alone does not repair the inactive reservation. These are recorded accounting amounts, not an independently reconciled provider invoice.

**Proposed fix:** make authorized recovery acquire a valid, generation-specific admission/reservation **before** reopening executable work. Preserve previously consumed cost; reserve only the next bounded increment under the existing tenant/scope locks. Alternatively create a clearly linked continuation operation with a fresh identity and preserved context. Do not simply clear usage, flip a settled reservation to reserved, disable budget guards or duplicate its ledger entries. Retry and operator-confirmed completion both need this lifecycle; Cancel must remain terminal. Give inactive-reservation, exhausted-allowance and provider-payment refusals distinct error codes and next actions. An unresolved stop must not be counted as completed user work.

**Benefit/tradeoffs/effort:** makes the advertised recovery controls able to reach a subsequent model call without misleading financial instructions. Budget idempotency, concurrent tabs, finalization races and prior-charge preservation make this approximately **2–3 engineer-days**, including integration tests. The five signatures measure observed denial, not the number of all affected recovery attempts or a guaranteed completion gain.

**Acceptance/measurement:** real-PostgreSQL sequence reserve→usage→recovery checkpoint→Retry/Mark complete→next model call→terminal settlement, including duplicate/concurrent recovery, true hard limits, cancellation and crash between re-admission and enqueue. Assert one incremental reservation, no double billing/refund, immutable consumed history, tenant/department/user isolation and successful model continuation when funds allow. Retain fail-closed behavior for stale unrelated reservations. Track authorized-recovery model-continuation rate, inactive-reservation refusals, completion-without-deliverable stops and exact incremental costs. Evidence: `recovery-inactive-reservations-month-proof.json`, `task1187-current-api-recovery-proof.json`, `task1187-current-runtime-proof.json`. Recommendation only; no production state changed.

**Implementation (14 October):** reproduced with real PostgreSQL (the run ends `completed` with the inactive-reservation allowance message). Retry/Mark complete now reserve a new generation (`<request>:recovery:<n>`) through normal budget admission before reopening; settlement charges only post-baseline usage to it, so earlier usage is neither re-reserved nor billed twice. Inactive/missing reservations now fail with an accurate message instead of completing with allowance advice. Not deployed; API and worker must ship together.

### Monthly audit: task1187 — expensive PDF search, truthful retry provenance, no answer

Task **1187** (`924a855f-4cc8-40a1-9ea4-adf70d3376bc`), October12: all **16 messages, one run and78,030 captured transcript characters** reviewed, with additional SSM recovery events/reservation evidence. Wall time is **906.47s (15m6.47s)**, including **224.52s operator wait**. The last shell step's apparent829.99s spans multiple attempts and wait; it is **not one829-second uninterrupted computation**. Exact starts/ends show two~300/305-second attempts and an immediate third failure. Both retries are explicitly operator-authorized, not automatic duplicate mutations. Current final state completed does not mean the close-out response was delivered: it ends with the inactive-reservation message and no requested answer (**H-066/H-014**).

**H-020 / H-049, bounded document work:** the116.9MB PDF has358 pages. Captured latest C04 cover still lists RE-R2 **and RE-3**; a reply claims the cover was corrected, so the retained pages already support a qualified discrepancy response. Berry reads the complete captured first-ten-page extraction plus a partially returned11–30 range, then uses pypdf to re-extract every page for a handful of keywords. It prints nothing until the whole loop ends. Two five-minute attempts fail; a later sandbox-not-found failure ends recovery. PDF watermark/layout whitespace also consumes most of the returned byte budget before useful later pages. Native all358 pages/regulatory close-out correctness are not independently certified; this is an observed ineffective strategy and missing answer, not an assertion all source pages were reviewed.

**H-009 / H-010 / H-014:** all arbitrary bash commands are conservatively non-idempotent; the read-only search therefore receives the generic **external mutation uncertain/operator review** message. Retain conservative handling for opaque shell/external writes; do not trust a model's claim that arbitrary code is read-only. Add a typed, bounded document-search operation with page/progress receipts, cached extraction keyed by tenant/source hash/engine version, proven process abort/settlement and safe restart after sandbox loss. Prefer a focused latest-revision response with explicit limits when the relevant evidence is already present. Supply local-analysis recovery wording and “continue with a smaller search” controls; leave external-action outcome review intact. About **1–2 days** for a bounded search workflow within existing H-020/H-049 work; complete abort-aware lifecycle remains H-010's broader proposal. Current deadlines already exist, and this example does not justify reopening the fixed late-receipt timeout patch. The stale-sandbox/reconnect fix remains separately undeployed; exact first timeout/sandbox-expiry causation is not proved here.

**Test/positive evidence:** compare this input's focused cover/reply lookup with the all-page pypdf loop under a controlled cap; require a cited partial-close-out response, bounded extraction, retained input/work and progress before the cap, then simulate sandbox loss and change strategy without losing context. Four provider calls succeed, so this is not a provider/API failure. Cache diagnostics show21,504 cached tokens of27,822 input, followed by0 of30,163 on the next call despite stable captured prefix metadata; this is another **H-032** observation, not proof of a gateway TTL or a cache-disabled setting. Preserve operator wait separately from model/tool latency and report per-attempt duration. Private timeline: `task1187-execution-recovery-proof.json`. No deployment.
