### Monthly audit: task983 — self-matching edit loop, lost scratch input, unusable Retry

Task **983** (`1aba1c13-2df6-4d64-a647-90647539a29e`), October7: all **47 messages, one run and221,406 captured transcript characters** reviewed. The user requests spelling/text corrections to an11.2MB Arabic DOCX. Berry reads the source through six paginated Office read receipts and performs targeted XML probes. It never publishes a corrected document. The eventual final message is the inactive-budget allowance instruction, with state completed: the third fully reviewed no-deliverable example for **H-066**. Native source pages, technical interpretation and all inherited fields are not independently certified.

**Confirmed script defect / H-008,H-020:** the generated edit helper uses `while old in joined`; one rule replaces `رقم الوثيق` with `رقم الوثيقة`. The replacement retains the search prefix, so repeated passes append the final character indefinitely. Testing the exact AST-extracted function on one in-memory XML paragraph times out at one second; a finite single replacement completes in0.019s. This demonstrates nontermination for a captured source match, not an installed Python/lxml dependency defect. Historical script SHA256 `71d203826e47a27b…`; private `task983-prefix-loop-proof.json`. The original process's CPU trace is unavailable, so do not label its entire elapsed span as measured busy computation.

**Exact timing / H-010,H-014:** run wall6744.211s (112m24.21s). Original script invocation10:39:19.783→11:34:45.231 lasts3325.448s (55m25.45s) before the recovery checkpoint. The operator then waits2817.253s (46m57.25s), explicitly retries at12:21:42.484, and the replay fails after4.320s because `/tmp/uvu_docx/extracted/word/document.xml` is absent. Its script had been saved under the durable workspace after the write tool correctly rejected a /tmp path, while the working XML remained in /tmp. No restored-working-copy prerequisite runs before replay. Current continuity declares sandbox operations non-abort-aware, and current runner deliberately waits for settlement to avoid overlapping side effects. A bash timeout omitted by the model passes0 to the provider. This supports the existing need for bounded, abort-settled local operations; it does not justify removing conservative external-action handling or discarding valid late receipts from the already fixed timeout path.

**Proposed improvement:** add a tested DOCX patch helper that enumerates source matches once, applies spans without reprocessing replacement text, preserves fields/run boundaries and produces an exact changed-span receipt. Exercise prefix-containing replacements, split runs, nested textboxes, Arabic, fields and SmartArt. Keep working copies under the snapshotted workspace and restore/revalidate dependencies before authorized replay. Prompt guidance can prefer finite replacements and a small fixture before whole-document editing, but does not substitute for the helper. About **1–2 days** for a scoped replacement helper and fixtures; broader preservation guarantees depend on supported Word structures. Measure edit completion, retained-context recovery and model-authored script time with operator wait excluded.

**H-066 corroboration:** fresh read-only accounting records for all five signature runs show reconciled reservations and reserve/reconcile ledger entries preceding operator retries. Task983's343,065 reserved micros/234,541 actual micros reconcile at11:34:45; task1158's181,659/74,578 at15:21:39. These are per-reservation records, not provider invoice verification. Evidence: `recovery-inactive-all-five-ledger-proof.json`; task559 and1171 are fully reviewed and recover in later fresh turns. No production data, source code or prompts changed.

### H-067 — Office pagination repeats the whole extraction (P2)

**Confirmed current inefficiency; benefit is a measurement hypothesis.** Task **983** makes six successful reads of one unmodified11.2MB DOCX at successive line offsets. Each takes20.46–20.94s; total **124.016s**, with **103.218s** spent on reads after the first. The full history shows no edit to that input before these reads. These durations include preparation, binary transfer and extraction, so103.218s is an opportunity ceiling for those repeated calls, not a measured cache saving.

**Month-wide candidate frequency:** **661** completed Office reads of source-designated /inputs/UUID paths form561 task/path groups. **62 repeated groups /50 tasks /31 users** contain100 reads after their first read. The cohort includes DOCX/XLSX/PPTX and supported macro/template variants in runs created inside the fixed month window. Counts do not establish100 byte-identical cache misses: a sandbox copy could have been edited, and per-call byte versions are not recorded. These50 whole histories are not all adjudicated by this query. No global cache-hit rate is currently instrumented for this layer.

**Cause/current status, high confidence:** `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:907` reads all Office bytes and calls documentTextExtractor **before** applying the offset/limit. `apps/worker/src/knowledge/services.ts:104` issues a new Tika PUT on every extract; `apps/worker/src/main.ts:103` directly wires that extractor. There is no Berry extraction-result lookup or concurrent-request coalescing in this path. Fresh SSM **October13 21:27:30UTC** confirms continuity `0978f3bb…`, services `a58b7fa2…`, main `f29cfcd1…`; worker `c030d2ca…` remains unchanged. Internal Tika/backend caching is not measured, and knowledge chunks should not be assumed to preserve complete document text/page boundaries.

**Fix/benefit/tradeoffs/effort:** retain full bounded extracted text plus source mapping under a tenant-scoped **verified byte hash**, extractor/normalization/OCR configuration and version key; slice it for each offset. Deduplicate concurrent extraction of the same verified input. Reuse immutable stored inputs only under an enforced identity/integrity contract; changed sandbox files must get a fresh hash/result. Recheck current access on every hit, invalidate revoked/deleted sources, respect input/output limits, and avoid caching transient failures. Preserve pagination and extraction-quality warnings. A shared durable cache can survive worker/sandbox recovery, at the cost of storage, invalidation and privacy controls. Approximately **2–3 engineer-days** including failure, concurrent-access and isolation tests; measure transfer-versus-extraction time before selecting its storage path.

**Distinct cache target:** for byte-identical, authorized repeat reads within the retention window, target **≥90% extraction-cache hits** and report requests/bytes/time avoided. This is separate from provider cached input tokens in H-032. With only the measured within-task path repeats and one cold read per source group,100/661≈**15.13% of all observed Office reads** are repeat candidates; an85–90% global Office hit target is unsupported by this month's cold workload. Identical content across different task/path groups and mutable versions remain unmeasured.

**Acceptance:** the six-offset reproduction makes one Tika request with identical text and cursor/line-limit receipts; changed bytes/MIME/engine/OCR configuration miss, duplicate concurrent readers extract once, timeout/error responses are not cached, and deleted/revoked or cross-tenant sources cannot be served. Compare warm-read p50/p95, whole-file transfers, Tika request volume and exact output hashes under these scenarios. Fixtures must preserve useful text/quality flags rather than treating a faster empty extraction as success. Evidence: `office-repeat-immutable-source-month-proof.json`, `office-repeat-current-runtime-proof.json`, task983's complete control flow. Recommendations only; no cache implemented.


### Monthly audit: task1171 — unusable Retry, later recovery, costly OCR and unsupported review claims

Task **1171** (`23868049-a2ec-4cd0-9634-3f56c0715df3`), October12: all **134 messages, eight runs and375,522 captured transcript characters** reviewed. The user asks for SRI guidance, then detailed construction-stage reviews of five material packages, plus a quick summary. There is no intentional cancellation. The rockwool review's first run stops without an answer, but the user returns with their own assessment and reuploads the source; a fresh run produces the review. Subsequent EPS, access-panel, gate and handrail reviews also complete. This task therefore demonstrates a failed **run and recovery control**, not permanent loss of the entire task's deliverable.

**H-066 / H-009 / H-014, fourth whole-history corroboration:** run `0f0853c1-c3fd-4bbb-8f4b-9ec090ae6a47` lasts **1233.472s (20m33.47s)**. Its blocking OCR poll runs **900.015s**, then records an uncertain-mutation recovery checkpoint at06:49:52.689. The operator waits **100.753s**, explicitly retries at06:51:33.441, and the replay fails after **0.175s** with sandbox-not-running. The next model call is prevented by the already reconciled reservation, and the run is marked completed with only the allowance instruction. Native accounting corroborates100,219 reserved/31,247 actual micros reconciled at the checkpoint, before retry. These are reservation records, not independently verified provider invoices. A new user run at06:58:40 completes in **1006.258s (16m46.26s)** with the answer. All five exact-signature histories are now reviewed: three have no requested deliverable and559/1171 recover in later fresh turns. Evidence: `task1171-execution-recovery-proof.json`, `recovery-inactive-all-five-ledger-proof.json`.

**H-020 / H-049 / H-010, avoidable preparation and weak progress contract:** the91.6MB/249-page mixed PDF is rendered wholesale. The first background command takes118.21s; shell backgrounding changes the effective directory for its follow-up probe, which incorrectly reports no img directory despite249 rendered images being found by an absolute-path probe. No OCR text is present in the later continuation. Berry attributes this to a sandbox restart, but the captured process/exit records do not prove that explanation. Its900-second sleep/poll loop provides no useful intermediate OCR progress before checkpoint. The fresh run's foreground four-worker OCR does succeed in **668.4s**, producing249 files; neither Tesseract, PyMuPDF nor installed Python is shown broken. Later reviews use selective OCR, but text-length-only selection leaves annotation-rich or header-only pages dependent on further ad hoc inspection. Splitting pdftotext at its trailing form-feed also includes empty terminal segments in reported page/OCR totals. Do not equate generated OCR files, keyword search or page snippets with complete visual/technical verification.

Extend the existing typed PDF/OCR proposal with actual PDF page counts, per-page extraction-quality/source receipts, incremental durable OCR checkpoints, bounded batches, progress and safe cancellation, and targeted visual follow-up on figures or annotation-heavy pages. Runtime-enforced local extraction can be replay-safe; do not relabel arbitrary shell commands or unknown external actions as safe based on model assertion. Approximately **2–3 days** for scoped orchestration/checkpointing, with broader document-QA work tracked separately. Test this package through sandbox loss after rendered pages, reuse completed page results, visibly advance remaining-page counts, avoid a900-second opaque poll and recover the next model call under a valid budget. Measure page-quality coverage, OCR/model turns and time excluding operator wait; added durable storage and quality checks are the main tradeoffs.

**H-025 / H-012 / H-017, confirmed unsupported technical guidance:** the first answer gives a linear reflectance expression, vaguely “adjusted for emittance,” as an ASTM E1980 recomputation and supplies an SRI≈106 example without an auditable calculation. SRI is a temperature-based metric using both reflectance and emittance, not that stand-alone expression. The primary [ASTM public scope](https://store.astm.org/standards/e1980) and [Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory definition](https://heatisland.lbl.gov/about-cool-surfaces) support this distinction. In the EPS review, the attempted official DBC download is explicitly denied by policy, and searches yield no verified exposed-floor0.35W/m²K clause. Berry nevertheless introduces that approximate benchmark to assess compliance; its follow-up summary repeats it more firmly. The detailed review also calls CFC-free compliant while noting that its supporting conformity certificate expired before submission. These are evidence/qualification failures; this audit does not certify the correct project-specific target or all regulatory conclusions.

Require retrieved governing clauses or approved project criteria for normative thresholds, a tested calculator/complete inputs for computations, and explicit separation of supplied evidence, inference and unresolved compliance. Summaries must retain unresolved qualifications. Add this multi-turn fixture to H-025/H-017 evaluations (**0.5–1 day** within the existing source-verification plan): after a denied download, withhold a numerical compliance verdict or label the missing criterion; do not promote an expired certificate into unconditional conformity. Useful positive behavior includes distinguishing concealed façade insulation from roof finishes, checking newest revision comments, noting absent separate calculations and identifying actual drawing/product mismatches. Native pixels and complete engineering applicability of all five PDFs remain independently unchecked. No new issue ID or implementation/deployment change.

### H-068 — Newly imported connector files report available before sandbox hydration (P1)

**Confirmed current contract defect; likely verification race, high confidence.** Task559 contains four first-time Drive imports with no sandbox path/artifacts entry: the main Zutari RFP, AppendixC, tender clarification responses and pricing schedule. Three unsuccessful filesystem probes follow the first import. A later repeat call stages each file; in these examples the returned persistence status also changes from temporary task use to Library. A first-time NDA email does stage correctly in the same history. This variability fits readiness timing rather than a universal download failure.

**Frequency, with an explicit denominator:**447 successful Drive-import tool receipts with artifact metadata occur in monthly-created runs.270 are first imports (`reused=false`);217 return no staged artifact/path (**80.37% of first imports;48.55% of all447 receipts**), across49 tasks/14 users.213 are temporary and4 Library imports. The latest is October12 at13:05:23.674Z; five occurrences are on/afterOctober10. These217 are a missing-hydration symptom cohort, not217 proven failed tasks or unauthorized Library writes. Seven retry/recovery chains in task559, task1191, task1172 and task1217, plus two missing-path examples in the fully reviewed task1189, have now been traced. The remaining affected histories are queued for full review.

**Current implementation:** fresh SSM inspection at21:45–21:46Z October13 verifies deployed API file-service SHA256 `db761576d6b90e8572bf0d4ba1c0fceabbc8d4d401ccb348f3c84edc3bbb13df`, MCP wrapper `d189ebdfd7282a2c56f60e4622bcbb00b80015191d8bce0fb75596c20c2e5777` and continuity `0978f3bb5e56366b4fbde40a3211a76ac241b74a94d8c9bae72729c3a7e80759`. API `importConnectorArtifact` inserts an unverified blob, associates it, queues verification and returns a file-level available/processing DTO. Continuity `inputFiles` correctly excludes unverified blobs. `stageAssociatedInputFiles` reads that filtered list once, passes the resulting list with selected-file verification disabled, and may return an empty array. The MCP wrapper then reports a successful download without readiness, path or recovery instructions. Attachment admission has a wait path, but newly imported files do not use it. Task1191 provides timestamp correlation: the first missing-path import completes75ms before its blob is verified. Task1172 confirms a second race: the missing-path receipt completes472ms before verification. Task1189 adds a third correlation: its first missing-path receipt completes approximately73ms before verification. Task1217 adds an approximately351ms post-receipt verification correlation. These timestamps support a verification race in those four examples. Its second missing-path import uses an already verified blob, proving that readiness timing is not the sole explanation for the full cohort. Trace staging selection and hydration separately; do not bypass the verification gate.

Supporting local code: `apps/api/src/files/file-platform.service.ts:808–972`, `apps/worker/src/mcp-tools.ts:165–188`, `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:676–700,1875–1932,2220–2271`. Evidence: `connector-import-staging-month-proof.json` and both `task559-current-connector-*-proof.json` files.

**Proposed fix,1–2 days:** add bounded, progress-visible waiting for the selected file's verified readiness and materialization, or return a typed pending receipt with an explicit hydrate/retry operation that reuses the existing file identity. Return staging completeness separately from download completeness. Preserve the original Library choice across a readiness retry; expose Save to Library as a separate deliberate operation. Keep tenant/session/owner checks, revision identity, verified hashes and revocation checks. Benefit: remove avoidable searches, re-downloads, storage-scope changes and speculation about missing files. Tradeoff: verification time becomes visible in the import call; bounded pending state prevents indefinite waits and avoids reading unverified bytes.

**Acceptance test/measurement:** hold verification at200ms,2s and beyond the wait bound; first imports must either yield a verified sandbox path or a typed pending receipt, never misleading ready text. Resume the same file without a second remote download, Library promotion or duplicate association. Exercise verifier failure, worker restart, deleted/revoked files, simultaneous imports and cross-tenant IDs. Measure verified-path availability for eligible imports, extra physical downloads and time-to-first usable read; target100% correctly classified ready/pending outcomes before setting a latency SLO. This is distinct from fixed upload-transfer timeouts and H-062 search indexing failures.

**Implementation (14 October):** connector imports now wait (bounded, 250 ms polls) for blob verification before staging, and an import still verifying returns an explicit pending receipt instead of a success without a path. Not deployed.

### H-069 — Temporary connector imports are silently added to Library (P1)

**Confirmed current defect, high confidence.** In fully reviewed task1191 (`8fef96dd-d7ad-4f18-98a1-7ef1ce5a1a29`, October12), two first Drive imports return `library=false` and text saying they were not added to Library. Both have active Library entries created at exactly the file-insert timestamp. A later identical call reports `library=true`; it reveals pre-existing persistence rather than causing a promotion. The same misleading change occurs in the reviewed task559 chains. This is a persistence-contract defect, independent of H-068's missing sandbox path; it is not proof of external sharing, unauthorized Drive writes or knowledge indexing.

**Monthly frequency:** of270 first-time successful Drive-import receipts,261 say `library=false`, across54 tasks/17 users. All261 distinct file records remain retained, and all261 have active Library entries created at file creation. Their combined logical size is1,499,883,647 bytes; that is retained source size, not proved incremental storage cost or an amount safe to delete. The latest receipt is October12 at13:40:15.238Z. Nine first-import receipts report Library use and are excluded. The261 affected import contracts are established by receipts, DB records and current trigger logic; only the named whole histories establish the surrounding user experience.

**Cause and current status:** a fresh read-only production catalog query confirms enabled `files_library_after_insert_compat`, executing `berry_file_library_after_insert_compat()` after every eligible owned file insert. It inserts a Library entry for scanning/processing/available files without checking origin or the requested persistence scope. The API's current connector-import path correctly defaults to temporary use and only explicitly calls `reviveLibraryEntry` for `saveToLibrary=true`, but the trigger overrides that choice. The first DTO reports the requested flag; repeat lookup uses the actual Library entry. Local migration44 (`packages/db/src/index.ts:4831`, trigger definition near4867) and the live catalog match. API source: `apps/api/src/files/file-platform.service.ts:837–862,906–948,1500–1521`; connector default: `apps/api/src/connectors/google-tools.ts:298–333`. Private evidence: `connector-library-trigger-current-proof.json`, `connector-temporary-library-month-proof.json`, `task1191-import-file-state-proof.json`.

**Proposed fix,2–3 days:** make Library persistence an explicit operation for modern writers. Narrow or retire the legacy compatibility trigger through a reviewed migration while preserving genuinely legacy upload/output behavior. Return actual persisted scope and treat a disagreement as a contract error. Add a clear Save to Library control for task inputs. Investigate retained temporary imports before any cleanup; later deliberate saves must be preserved. Expected benefit: accurate receipts, less Library clutter and predictable retention. Tradeoff: migrating compatibility behavior requires testing every file writer; historic intent cannot safely be inferred from current Library membership alone.

**Acceptance tests/measurement:** temporary first import, repeat import, concurrent import and worker restart must retain a usable task input with zero Library entries unless explicitly saved. Explicit save must add one entry, preserve idempotency and use the intended knowledge-indexing policy. Test user uploads, generated outputs, legacy writers, deleted entries, revoked access and tenant isolation. Reconcile requested versus persisted scope for100% of connector imports; measure unexpected Library additions separately from indexing and sandbox readiness.

**Implementation (14 October):** reproduced with real PostgreSQL. New temporary connector imports now remove, in the same transaction, the Library row the legacy trigger creates for them; explicit saves still add one entry. No schema change; historical entries are untouched. Not deployed.

### Monthly audit: task1191 — recent import race, recoverable sandbox failure and lengthy template repair

All114 messages/four runs and223,396 rendered characters were read through the final answer. The October12 task asks about a NOBU C04 submittal and then requests a StatusB review sheet. It ultimately publishes the requested DOCX, so this is not a permanently failed task.

The next turn fails in0.473s during sandbox preparation with a raw missing-sandbox message, before a usable model response. A later continuation successfully stages the user-uploaded PDF and completes in15m22.8s. Work survives; no blanket claim that sandbox recovery always fails is justified. The personal timber-review skill has a broad NOBU material-review description, prompting activation for BTU meters; specialization/routing should be checked before calling that a broken skill.

The histories show the same116,883,006-byte named PDF and identical captured paragraphs; independent whole-byte equality between the two uploads is not established. Document production repairs a wrong relative path, an lxml Paragraph-versus-element type error, a paragraph-index mistake that temporarily removes the reviewer line, and two empty searches before discovering the outcome controls in a nested table. All are repaired before publication. A structure-aware DOCX editing helper that addresses cells/paragraphs/nested tables by validated content could reduce model repair work (H-020/H-064). It should preserve fields, signatures and untouched paragraphs, with before/after invariants; a prompt alone cannot enforce those invariants.

The published43,717-byte DOCX (`e0374008-6891-4d5e-aec2-a51bfa12629d`, SHA256 `654c7fdc3fa4ee69e0f6ec2df04f0736f34d3c840e347ebd8cae7bf3566800da`) was independently downloaded, hash-checked and read through all XML text/nested outcomes. The reviewer and all seven comments remain, and onlyB is selected. Both pages of a separate local render were visually inspected: the second page contains continued boilerplate and distribution controls with substantial empty space. That local layout is a portability/QA example, not proof of the production renderer's pagination; the original task explicitly discloses two pages. It performs PDF text checks but never inspects every rendered output page. Avoid declaring universal Asite acceptance or complete visual verification without those checks (H-019/H-029). ### Monthly audit: task1172 — six-minute import repair, skipped PDF text and partial vision accepted as complete

All152 messages/three October12 runs were read through the final answer (600,459 rendered characters). Two long source reviews complete in11m43.2s and14m42.0s; a41.9s follow-up repeats the assessment. Completed state does not establish correctness.

The41,359,824-byte GI duct import completes at06:25:56.640Z without a sandbox path. Its blob is verified at06:25:57.112Z,472ms later; its Library entry already exists at file creation. This is H-068's sixth whole-history causal chain and second timestamp-correlated verification race, plus H-069's scope defect. Before retrying at06:32:00.089Z, Berry makes32 intervening calls, mostly empty filesystem searches. Reimport stages the same file in13.316s. The gap is6m03.449s from the first receipt to retry start, not six minutes of downloading. Several messages promise a retry while searches continue. Current code's empty staging result supplies no typed pending/readiness recovery, and the error guard does not stop successful empty searches. Prioritize the import contract repair; separately count repeated no-progress discovery and guide a bounded change of approach (H-004/H-020). Do not confuse intentional user changes with a repair loop.

The GI letters explicitly give recycled pre/post content as **N/A**; the answer converts that into **0%**. It combines a user-provided Nobu3Pearl questionnaire with the ECAE PDF's MC2§14 requirements, then presents the combined mapping as the governing criteria. Zinc coating certificates and a sample test become broad confirmation of SMACNA/DW144 compliance, without checking full fabrication requirements or sample traceability. These are confirmed fidelity/claim-boundary problems in the retained response (H-017/H-025), not an independent engineering rejection of the product. Preserve unknown/not-applicable values, separate governing sources, and state what each certificate actually establishes. The subsequent PI follow-up similarly treats0% post-consumer and unspecified pre-consumer content as effectively zero total recycled contribution. Keep missing evidence distinct from a proved zero.

The147-page PI review changes page ranges while carrying over line offsets. It later rereads pages29–30, but the full tails of pages16/120 are not demonstrated. A continuation should bind its file revision and page range to its cursor and return the next valid call, instead of requiring the model to reconstruct pagination. Benefits are fewer omitted portions and fewer repair reads; tradeoff is cursor invalidation after a source revision. Test a50KB truncation through multiple ranges and assert that every source line is returned exactly once or explicitly reread (H-020/H-049).

Nine image-inspection calls include one rejected /tmp path and one empty-observation failure; both are recoverable. Of seven completed observations, **six end with finishReason=length**. One two-attempt observation contains only a document heading and is still marked completed. The assistant eventually obtains a complete single-image ISO observation, but other requested image details remain truncated while the final answer claims full-package review. This is another whole-history example of H-039's partial-observation contract, not nine unrecoverable image failures. Require per-image coverage/completeness metadata; reduce batch size or allocate sufficient observation budget on truncation, and prohibit asserting unseen certificate absence or universal visual review. Test multi-image certificates with long reasoning and require either all requested fields or explicit unknown/incomplete outcomes. Measure completed observations lacking all requested image coverage separately from provider failures. Native image-only pages and project-specific engineering criteria remain unverified in this audit; no deliverable was generated.

Audit presentation note: this transcript uses additional references for long, byte-identical line blocks repeated **inside the same tool field**. Each reference points backward to fully displayed content, includes its SHA256/raw line span, and retains a private receipt. Expansion is asserted byte-equal to the original field; every changed number, new argument, message and result remains literal. The renderer's synthetic round-trip fixture also passes. This is lossless audit reading, not an aggregate scan or summary substituted for task review. It does not change raw production archives or any Berry code.

### H-070 — Large MCP results are silently cut and pagination cursors disappear (P1)

**Confirmed current contract defect.** Berry cuts joined MCP text at 120,000 UTF-16 code units without a truncation receipt. The durable model projection then uses only `output.content`, so complete structured metadata is not available to the model. A next-page token can remain in metadata while disappearing from the usable tool result. Confidence is high for the contract and reproduced cursor loss; confidence is moderate about its contribution to the downstream model mistake, which also involves document-identity drift.

**Whole-history example:** task1189 (`3bdadbe3-b5c5-4752-8ce4-01d215750ba5`), October12 run `d8baef42-8e65-4b0a-9cae-554ec7ab0e6a`. Its first100-file Drive listing, step `765aa6b6-43b1-4805-9844-a603f697396e`, ends mid-JSON at the limit. All100 file records and a444-character opaque next-page token exist in structured metadata; the token is absent from model text. At09:20:05.489Z, Berry supplies an invented token, receives400 Invalid Value, and repeats the first-page listing. The repeat and a later recent-files listing have the same hidden-cursor condition. Berry later locates the correct source through another folder, so this is not proof that truncation alone caused the incomplete final assessment. Two other sources of difficulty are the wrong document number and a known100MB download limit.

**Frequency and boundaries:** all4,137 completed MCP outputs in monthly-created runs have captured payloads and were scanned for this contract. **86 outputs (2.08%)**, across49 tasks/18 users, have exactly120,000 UTF-16 code units of content. **58 begin as JSON and are invalid at the cut (1.40% of completed MCP outputs)**, across26 tasks/13 users. **11 have a nonempty next-page token in structured metadata but none in model text**, across7 tasks/2 users. The latest limit exposure is October12 at13:04:57.989770Z. The86 outputs span Gmail, Drive, Google Docs and AESG pricing, and include DeepSeek70, MiniMax7, Kimi2 and Qwen7. These are symptom/contract exposures, not86 failed tasks. Task1189 and task1217 in the49-task limit cohort have been reviewed end to end at this recording point; the others remain in the coverage queue. Captured binary redaction and upstream limitations remain evidence boundaries.

**Current deployment/code:** read-only SSM at22:18:43–54Z October13 verifies MCP wrapper `d189ebdfd7282a2c56f60e4622bcbb00b80015191d8bce0fb75596c20c2e5777`, runner `c030d2cab85b11ee48d41c589e9963890f4fd429bcf4b9b7cc9d291740ecf050`, Google tools `85c0b86769d1a83a94cad07136f8ec4b33244952dfb1c97ce219051827b25b66` and connector service `aae435095f30b96c307e33ea3172c32a95a7b264123d35a4d82924006ec53243`. Local supporting references: `apps/worker/src/mcp-tools.ts:164`, `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:5690`, `apps/api/src/connectors/google-tools.ts:237` and `apps/api/src/connectors/connectors.service.ts:1192`. Drive returns extensive per-file capabilities/restrictions after eligibility filtering, and `googleToolText` serializes the complete result. The worker slices the serialization. Its journal sanitizer also silently limits strings to120,000, arrays to256 entries and objects to128 keys; nesting/binary omissions do have markers. Consequently “complete result persisted” cannot guarantee a complete original MCP response. Do not weaken source eligibility or ACL checks to make responses smaller.

**Reproduction:** the captured deployed formatter, sanitizer and durable projection plus the exact wrapper slicing expression were replayed offline with100 synthetic metadata-heavy file records. A235,846-character JSON response becomes invalid120,000-character model text, its opaque cursor remains in metadata but is missing from model text, and array/object limits silently remove44 items/12 fields. A proposed compact identity view retains the cursor in6,771 characters. This demonstrates the contract and a possible compact representation, not a deployed fix or provider recovery. Private receipts: `mcp-output-contract-current-proof.json`, `mcp-output-truncation-month-proof.json`, `mcp-output-contract-fixture.mjs`, `mcp-output-contract-fixture-proof.json`. A newline-escaping error in the auditor's first fixture generation was corrected before this successful reproduction; it is not a Berry incident.

**Proposed fix, estimated2–3 days:** provide a valid bounded tool-result envelope with explicit completeness, total/retained item counts, exact opaque cursor and a tenant-authorized handle for omitted text. Use compact per-tool model projections after source permission filtering. Keep original result retrieval and bounded journal retention explicit, including source revision and expiry. Do not fix this merely by raising every limit. Benefits are reliable pagination, fewer invented tokens/re-listings, lower prompt volume and clearer recovery. Tradeoffs are handle lifecycle, cursor invalidation and permission checks on every retrieval; large-result storage and telemetry must remain bounded and tenant isolated.

**Acceptance:** replay metadata-heavy100-file Drive pages through the actual model projection; the exact returned cursor must support the next-page call and all identifiers must remain accessible. Test Unicode, multi-megabyte strings,256+ arrays,128+ keys, empty eligible pages with a cursor, revised/deleted sources, expired handles and revoked permissions across tenants. Require100% explicit truncation/completeness disclosure and preservation of valid pagination state in eligible bounded responses. Measure pagination400s, redundant first-page calls, context tokens and end-to-end retrieval latency before/after. Track incomplete source acquisition separately from intentional user changes, valid empty searches and ordinary model mistakes.

**Implementation (14 October):** oversized MCP results now stay valid JSON (largest list shortened), keep the exact next-page cursor (restored from structured metadata if needed) and carry a truncation receipt; non-JSON cuts are marked with counts. Not deployed.

The modular-support answer labels the page130 QMS certificate as a trade licence despite the captured page heading. ### H-071 — Successful image reads can be silently omitted, and rereading does not reprioritize them (P1)

**Confirmed current contract defect, high confidence; contribution to every model error remains uncertain.** In fully reviewed task1217, four read batches followed by a model call request7,7,7 and8 distinct images. The deployed selector sends at most five unique paths, retaining first insertion order. The first fan batch drops certificate pages233/235. The first jet-fan batch drops schedule pages8/9; later repeated reads do not move those paths to the end. Copying them to new names in another seven-image batch again places both before five other images, so the workaround can still omit them. The garbage-DX batch drops pages126–128. Each successful image-read receipt says its pixels will be attached to the next model request, without disclosing this selection loss. Re-reading can therefore trigger the duplicate guard without making an old image newly eligible for inspection.

**Frequency and boundary:** all captured monthly successful image-read groups before a subsequent model call were scanned:486 groups,13 over-five candidates across9 tasks, with zero missing captured read payloads. Twelve groups across8 tasks use Qwen; one uses Kimi. These are candidate exposures, not13 failed tasks or proof of every provider wire payload. Four delivered over-five batches in task1217 are traced through the complete history. One extra six-image batch has no subsequent model call because the run terminates; it is excluded from the monthly exposure count. Provider bytes were not retained, and smaller batches can also contain previously displaced paths.

**Cause/current status:** read-only SSM at22:39–22:40Z October13 confirms continuity SHA256 `0978f3bb5e56366b4fbde40a3211a76ac241b74a94d8c9bae72729c3a7e80759`, `MAX_MODEL_IMAGES=5`, and `exposedSandboxImagePaths` returning `[...new Set(paths)].slice(-MAX_MODEL_IMAGES)`. `modelContent` selects this bounded set before any receipt of omitted paths. The current local selector has the same insertion-order logic even though local image sizing/omission handling is newer than the deployed module. This cause is distinct from H-061's separate vision overview cap and H-039's provider response ending at length. Local references: `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:550,2060`, `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:5530,4828`.

**Reproduction:** the exact captured deployed selector, with deterministic type helpers and synthetic workspace paths, selects only pages3–7 from seven reads. Repeating page1 leaves it absent; a new path becomes eligible. The fixture passes without provider calls or production changes. Private evidence: `task1217-current-runtime-proof.json`, `task1217-native-image-current-proof.json`, `task1217-image-batch-reconciliation.json`, `native-image-read-batch-month-proof.json`, `native-image-selection-fixture.mjs`, and its proof JSON.

**Proposed fix,1–2days:** make image inspection delivery explicit. Select current requested observations by most recent read/revision, disclose every withheld path and reason, and split larger batches into bounded inspection groups or return a typed pending observation. Retain safe payload limits, original files and permission checks. Have duplicate suppression reuse an existing observation or explain the delivery state; copied filenames should not be required. Distinguish successful filesystem identification from pixels actually supplied to the model. Benefit: fewer retries, omitted schedule/certificate evidence and unsupported visual claims. Tradeoff: extra model turns for large groups and careful revision-aware replay; raising the cap alone does not solve ordering or payload safety.

**Acceptance test/measurement:** request1/5/7/8 images, reread an older displaced path, replace the bytes at the same path, mix old/new paths and attachments, exceed size limits and revoke source access. Every requested path must be delivered or explicitly reported withheld; a fresh focused read must be inspectable without renaming. Verify tenant/revision isolation and do not mark a previously omitted page observed merely because its tool step succeeded. Measure delivered/requested inspection coverage, duplicate read repairs, visual follow-up corrections and task latency.

### H-072 — Continue excludes the interrupted run's preserved sandbox state (P1)

**Confirmed current integration defect, high confidence in the traced case.** Task676 (`79dccad7-baae-4330-9d67-6ea454dbec15`) prepares an amended NDA in run `57474bf6-1453-44e1-b792-20af18c6c43a`, then fails on a model request. Snapshot `1015589f-c2f0-4d13-91da-aa97ff8843cc` completes at07:26:07.020Z and contains that output. At07:27:01.789Z, explicit Continue creates run `c9a3189b-7832-42f6-9684-b184ce4d36df` using the original request message ID. Its ancestry consists only of that user entry, with neither the failed run nor its snapshot leaf. The existing continuity query excludes both. A new sandbox contains the input but no prepared output; publication fails and Berry reconstructs the document. This is a different cause from snapshot capture failure, the E2B cached-handle reconnect gap and H-066's settled budget.

**Frequency with a defined denominator:** a read-only October13 replay of current ancestry predicates covers132 explicit continuations created in the frozen monthly window. For68 continuations across51 tasks, the nearest prior failed/cancelled same-request run has a completed snapshot before admission, but that snapshot is excluded. For35 across29 tasks, there is also no other eligible prior snapshot or sandbox;30 of those have a different new sandbox association. Their current states are13 completed,12 failed,9 cancelled and1 waiting. These are selection-gap exposures, not35 proved lost files or all platform-caused cancellations. Outcomes are current read-time states, and the replay does not recreate historical admission code or provider execution. Only task676 here proves that an excluded archive contains a requested deliverable and that the next run reconstructs it; other affected histories remain queued for full attribution.

**Cause/current deployment:** SSM at22:46–22:47Z October13 confirms deployed continuity SHA256 `0978f3bb5e56366b4fbde40a3211a76ac241b74a94d8c9bae72729c3a7e80759` and API admission SHA256 `865befadc51c89a7f285d52052ef05c98aa3e64ed0fc9302ac3a5db228ee19f1`. API `continuableRequestMessageId` returns only the old user message ID. The worker builds restoration ancestry from that message and its parents, while the completed snapshot leaf lies later in the interrupted attempt. No explicit admitted source-run recovery identity bridges the two. Local references: `apps/api/src/runtime/durable-turn.service.ts:316,2588`; `apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:2121,2173,1830`. A sandbox “ensure” operation alone cannot recover an archive the selector deliberately excludes.

**Proposed fix,2–3days:** persist an authorized source-run and branch identity when admitting Continue, and let continuity restore the matching interrupted attempt's verified snapshot or sandbox. Require the source to belong to the same tenant/session/task and the selected continuation branch; preserve explicit edit/fork semantics and do not restore the latest session snapshot indiscriminately. Present a preserved-work receipt and accurate “restoring work” state, with retry/recreate options if restoration fails. Retain unknown-outcome mutation guards, completed tool receipts and idempotency; do not re-execute external sends or writes merely because files are restored. Benefit: recover prepared outputs without reconstruction, reduce latency and prevent invisible loss of intermediate user work. Tradeoff: explicit branch lineage, snapshot readiness/expiry and multiple simultaneous continuation handling need careful integration.

**Acceptance tests/measurement:** fail the model after creating an unpublished final file, create the snapshot, then Continue with the original user message. Verify the same file hash is restored and publication succeeds without rerunning creation. Repeat with paused/expired sandboxes, in-flight snapshot completion, cancelled tasks, multiple retries, edited prompts, forks, revisions and revoked/cross-tenant access. Deliberately distinguish Continue from an intentional fresh rerun; a cancelled history is not automatically a failure. Record restoration eligibility and actual restored files, zero replay of completed external mutations, unnecessary regeneration count and continuation latency. Private receipts: `continuation-sandbox-branch-month-proof.json`, `task676-current-api-continuation-proof.json` and the snapshot/ancestry proofs above.

### Monthly audit: task865 — skill installation succeeds; model ranking lacks evidence

All32 messages/three runs and78,556 reading-view characters are reviewed. All three runs complete. The October6 user first asks about IDML support, explicitly requests the InDesign skill from SFKislev/Flue, then asks which model to use. The installation run finishes in123.167seconds, with16 tool calls,11 logical model iterations,11.671seconds of tool time and107.964seconds recorded active compute. These counters separate preparation work from model latency; no cancellation or endless repair loop occurs.

**H-003/H-016/H-018, repaired capability discovery:** git cloning fails with a TLS handshake message but the shell pipeline returns exit0. The following codeload/raw requests return clear task-policy blocks naming the host and administrator action; Berry does not disable TLS verification. An available crawl connector retrieves the package through eight narrow scrape calls and one search. The upstream tree result explicitly reports truncation, and Berry narrows to directory listings successfully. This is a healthy completeness/recovery contrast to H-070's silent output cut.

The two-file package is saved successfully into the personal Skills library. Berry removes a foreign machine's hardcoded path, discloses that adaptation, and correctly distinguishes an installed instruction package from a reachable desktop InDesign bridge. It does not install desktop software or claim visual rendering verification. The retained receipts establish historical installation, not current package availability or tested IDML rendering.

A repository-package importer is an **improvement hypothesis**, with one whole-history workflow example rather than an established failure rate: expose read-only, pinned-revision package discovery and a validated save operation, plus environment/bridge prerequisites, so models need not reconstruct directories via generic crawl metadata. Keep the explicit user installation instruction, repository/tenant permissions, content hashes, bounded files and package resource validation. About2–3days for a narrow importer if usage justifies it; tradeoffs are third-party provenance and update policy. Test a declared package containing nested references, blocked network hosts, wrong branch, oversized/truncated source, unsafe paths and absent desktop dependencies. Measure model preparation turns and package equivalence, without broadening network policy automatically.

**H-025, unsupported comparative answer:** the final ranking asserts specific model availability, prices, context sizes and relative XML accuracy without a catalogue lookup, current source or task-specific comparison in that run. This establishes an unsupported confidence claim; it does not independently prove every named model or price is false. Ground model recommendations in the tenant's actual model catalogue and current primary documentation, and mark IDML-quality rankings as hypotheses until representative validation is run. About0.5–1day for this regression fixture within existing source-grounding work. Test unavailable/renamed models, changed prices and a domain without comparative evidence; measure unsupported recommendation claims rather than accepting a completed conversational turn as correctness.

Private evidence: full reading-view SHA256 `54afc763919bbaa1af66e3da5af00afc2fd12dc20d79c526f40a271a35df31b3` and immutable tool receipts. Installed resource bytes, package execution, native IDML and visual layout were not independently verified. No application or production changes were made.


### Monthly audit: task804 — CV creation recovers; PPTX source coverage remains unsupported

All 44 captured messages, two runs, 23 tool steps and 78,862 reading-view characters are reviewed through the four published files. Its generator SHA256 is `418a822aa3ca9c45dceeda35eddbfd7202b199ad0b94d97cf2b42a8f612d7639`; source validator SHA256 is `7ab984b2e12353353cd19d699995225847feb74af37fd9b5d40eac1b21e8ae36`. Test duplicated portrait/landscape slides, split runs, table cells, critical numbers/qualifiers and one deliberately missing project. A missing project must fail source coverage while layout duplicates must not falsely require extra entries. **H-041/H-003, avoidable image rejection:** the portrait request specifies 840×1120 and receives a provider 400 stating that both dimensions must be divisible by 16. Changing it to 832×1120 succeeds. This costs an extra 4.43-second failed call plus a model repair turn before the 20.87-second successful call. The recorded photo preparation produces 1000×1000 and 1400×1800 RGB assets, so the rejected provider input does not establish a wrong final portrait ratio. Actual image identity, provider bytes and final page layout are not independently certified here. **H-029/H-019, bounded recovery and verification:** a focused identity observation fails once with 500, then succeeds on a revised question. No infinite retry occurs. The four requested DOCX/PPTX variants are generated, non-empty and successfully persisted. The final answer accurately reports parity checks and the source-gate limitation. Its verification covers text parity, file existence and prepared image dimensions, without a final pixel-render review. Cleaning duplicated words/spacing is disclosed; the confidential-client and absent-expertise behavior follows the activated skill and is not an inferred content-loss defect.

Reading-view SHA256: `8ee6e8db619ce5caf2abf1747162508821cfca54a894bb9e53cb9095852f078b`. Artifact receipts are retained in the immutable task history. ### Monthly audit: task329 — short-report preparation and cancellation

All 50 captured messages, 29 tool steps and 74,576 reading-view characters are reviewed. The September 21 user requests a short, clear French report from one six-row table image for their manager. The single run is cancelled after 409.703 seconds, with no final answer or persisted deliverable. Cancellation intent is not retained, so it is not counted as a demonstrated platform failure. A 5,262,020-byte DOCX and a three-page rendered preview exist before cancellation; a separate final PDF/publication is not reached.

**H-018/H-064, avoidable preparation and missing imports:** Berry performs six skill activations before its first generator invocation, then numerous reads/greps of the 1,494-line generator to infer the spec. The first DOCX invocation occurs about 312 seconds after admission. It fails with `ModuleNotFoundError: normalise_office_templates`. The historical generator imports through a sibling `aesg-branding/scripts` directory despite receiving the actual hashed branding directory. Four additional resources are staged; an incorrectly relative symlink fails once, then an absolute symlink repairs the path and generation succeeds in 1.07 seconds. This is a bundled dependency/path-contract problem followed by an ordinary model repair mistake, not broken Python or an absent third-party package. The current H-064 SSM checks establish the still-missing declared dependency closure; this old sibling-name path itself is historical and is not asserted to be unchanged in today's generator.

Tool time is 66.664 seconds and active compute 332.588 seconds. Those counters make clear that slow preparation includes many model turns; the missing import alone does not explain the entire six-minute experience. Add this whole-history example to H-064's versioned entry-point dependency proposal and H-018's compact schema/ready command. A supported compact report preset is a UX hypothesis: the user asks for brevity, and the cover plus two content pages may be unnecessary, but no retained complaint proves that layout was rejected. Test this exact six-row French input from fresh minimal skill activation, require zero missing-import/path repairs and measure first useful output, model turns, input tokens and final usable delivery.

**H-039/H-029/H-019, incomplete vision disclosure:** the source overview ends at length. A second focused result stops after a table header; Berry notices and narrows its question, producing a complete observation. That result confidently attributes opportunity IDs to the ambiguous “Assigned for ?” column, without an independently verified native cell mapping. Treat that transcription as unverified. The three-page output overview again ends at length; a focused page-3 verification is interrupted by cancellation. No complete visual QA is established. Preserve the successful narrow-question recovery, while making partial observations explicit rather than implying every requested field/page was checked.

**H-023/H-017, calendar inconsistency in the generated spec:** Berry explicitly reads the system date as September 21, 2026 and dates the report accordingly, yet calls July 23, 2026 the nearest/immediate deadline and says to finalize that response before the September deadlines. All dated entries in the captured observation are already past. The source image does not establish whether the opportunities are still active or completed, so the correct behavior is to identify elapsed dates and request current status where needed, not assume a future pipeline. This demonstrates that supplying a current date alone does not prevent date/status errors; a structured consistency check and evidence-aware status language are needed. No final user-facing report is published, but the erroneous priority is present in the generated specification and preview observation.

### Monthly audit: task966 — sandbox failure after a waiting response; faithful email polish

All 52 captured messages, three runs and 70,383 reading-view characters are reviewed. The October 7 request asks to create a new NDA “same task” from a new commercial-registration certificate; the prior template is not attached to this new task. The first run fails after 231.682 seconds, including 123.660 seconds waiting for answers and 31.029 seconds of tools. The second run, after the user uploads the prior template, completes in 314.702 seconds and publishes the 19,582-byte amended DOCX. A subsequent email-polish run completes in 14.364 seconds.

**H-009/H-014, misleading finalization failure:** all five model steps and the question tool complete. Berry says “I'll wait for the NDA file,” then about 259ms later the run reports a raw sandbox-not-found failure; no `turn.finalize` receipt is present. The next run retains the same sandbox ID and succeeds. This does not prove permanent sandbox disappearance or that the user deleted the files. The current runner invokes tool finalization before committing completion (`apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:2593`), and sandbox finalization ensures the sandbox and lists outputs (`apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:1353`). The sequence is consistent with post-response finalization failing on a stale/unavailable sandbox, but the historical failing stack is not retained, so exact operation attribution has medium confidence. The current cached-handle reconnect gap remains documented in H-009; no fresh replay is claimed here.

Separate “waiting for user input” and response delivery from optional artifact housekeeping. Preserve failures when requested outputs are actually missing, but show the delivered answer and preserved-work state accurately; do not ask a user to modify SDK `timeoutMs` or `.setTimeout`. Retry/reconnect controls should use typed lifecycle evidence. Include this wait → answer → finalization failure → same-ID recovery sequence in H-009/H-014 fixtures, with safe artifact finalization and no external mutation replay. This is one traced status/recovery example, not a claim that every bracketed unavailable message has the same cause.

**H-024/H-020, prior-file continuity and structural discovery:** the new task has only the new certificate. Asking for the missing template is appropriate given that retained scope; no cross-task file-loss defect is established. An explicit recent-file picker or authorized prior-task reference could shorten this workflow without silently importing unrelated files. When the template is uploaded, text extraction succeeds, but generic flattened text does not identify the signature table. The model performs unzip, XML grep, paragraph mapping, a redundant tail check and table/run mapping before editing. The second run records 188.652 seconds active compute and 115.013 seconds tools. A bounded Office block map with body/table/cell/run locations is an improvement hypothesis for precision edits, not a broken parser claim. Estimate 1–2 days for an initial DOCX structure receipt with existing path/tenant limits; test split runs, nested tables, headers/shapes and source revisions, and measure inspection turns/time without losing formatting or content.

**H-017/H-025, preserve identifier meaning:** the source labels 7054083642 “National Number,” and the focused observation explicitly finds no separate CR number. Berry inserts it under “Commercial Registration No.”, noting “National Number” in the final table. This establishes an unverified field equivalence, not proof the identifier is legally invalid. Preserve the source label or verify the equivalence before changing its type. Similarly, the English company rendering is a transliteration, and the final correctly flags it for confirmation. The address and signatory stay visibly pending; skipped question answers are not treated as permission to invent them. Agreement date 05/10/2026 is preserved and disclosed for confirmation.

**H-039/H-019, bounded verification and successful repair:** a 75.75-second focused two-page observation contains two length-ended attempts, the first with only two response characters. Berry identifies the cut and makes a focused right-column check, which succeeds in 14.68 seconds. It also repairs a missing non-breaking space, checks stale names/numbers/address and ZIP/XML integrity, then publishes. These checks do not establish layout preservation “1:1” across all six native pages; only pages 1 and 6 are visually observed. The published file's complete native clause/layout preservation is not independently certified in this task review.

**H-002, healthy rewrite counterexample:** the user's “improve” request supplies an email about signing the NDA, filling yellow blanks and returning a copy. The editable email preserves “I don't have the details,” “for us” and “send us a copy,” and corrects grammar/typos without changing speaker or substantive requests. No sandbox is created and only `compose_message` is used. Keep this positive first-person fixture alongside the documented post-prompt regressions; the complaint is not universal to every rewrite.

### Monthly audit: task312 — native PowerPoint defect and speculative repair

All 59 messages, two completed runs, 28 tool steps and 80,343 reading-view characters are reviewed. The September 19 user asks for an editable PowerPoint version of a supplied flowchart, then reports that the downloaded file cannot be read when opened. Initial creation takes 702.308 seconds and repair another 539.400 seconds; human-wait counters are zero. Tools take only 9.917 and 22.255 seconds, while recorded active compute is 686.368 and 512.549 seconds. This is primarily repeated model preparation/rewrite work, not a slow sandbox operation. Both runs reuse the same sandbox successfully.

**H-019/H-012, confirmed native package defects:** read-only SSM retrieves the original image and both published files, with canonical storage hashes and sizes verified. The first PPTX is a healthy ZIP and parses through python-pptx, yet its slide contains 40 non-integer coordinate/extent attributes, such as `a:off x="1531620.0"` and `a:ext cx="0.0"`. The generated script computes connector centres with ordinary division and passes floats into the serializer. Microsoft documents DrawingML offsets and extents as integer-valued fields: [Point2DType.X](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.drawing.point2dtype.x?view=openxml-3.0.1) and [PositiveSize2DType.Cx](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.drawing.positivesize2dtype.cx?view=openxml-3.0.1). This is a concrete package-validation defect consistent with a strict PowerPoint opening problem; the exact client error and native PowerPoint opening are not independently reproduced.

The first file has 18 connector objects, zero serialized arrowhead elements and zero attached endpoint references. The model assigns `end_arrowhead_width/length`, which are not properties of the inspected python-pptx LineFormat API; the file confirms that the promised arrowheads were not written. Parsing a saved package and counting 33 shapes therefore did not establish arrow presence, reconnectability or application compatibility. The user complaint is supported by independent package evidence rather than being attributed to “connectors commonly corrupt PowerPoint.”

**Repair outcome:** after an unsupported `MSO_SHAPE.LINE` failure, Berry rebuilds with lines and later rectangles/triangles. The final file removes all 40 decimal geometry values and has 50 editable native shapes, including separate arrowhead triangles, but no actual connectors or attached endpoint references. Independent LibreOffice rendering of that final file succeeds. Comparing its single rendered slide with the complete native source image confirms that the NCEC-scoping branch ends in empty space instead of joining NCEC ESIA. The left source connection/bracket also becomes a disconnected solid bar. Text labels mostly persist, but the graph relationship is not preserved. A rebuilt file that converts successfully can still be incorrect. No subsequent user confirmation establishes that the final opens in PowerPoint.

**Current cause/status:** the current publication path checks non-empty bytes, size and media type before storing `persist_artifact` (`apps/worker/src/sandbox-continuity.ts:1230`); it has no automatic Office schema/diagram validation. The published branding validator matches local SHA256 `d142096704673409fa881d5e2abd8aa975a4ff5959fb3160b1926bc32728bdba` in `current-skill-source-parity.json`. Its PPTX checks focus on placeholders, branding and role formatting; `deploy/skills/aesg-branding/scripts/validate_artifact.py:591` does not check integer geometry or graph-edge preservation. This is a current validation gap, not proof the historical producer script remains in a current skill.

Extend H-019 with a format-level Office validator separate from branding, a tested editable-diagram helper and source/output node-edge receipts. Reject invalid geometry before publication with the exact field and recovery action; verify arrow direction, endpoints and intended source relationships, not just shape count. Estimate 2–3 days for bounded package checks/helper integration and 1–2 days for diagram preservation fixtures. Tradeoffs are false positives on supported extensions, compatibility across Office versions and added render cost; report validation levels explicitly rather than requiring expensive render for every trivial text edit. Test float centres, horizontal/vertical/reversed arrows, attached endpoints, graph branches, separate triangle fallbacks and a package that parses/converts but fails the schema subset. Measure opening complaints, invalid published packages and lost graph edges; validate on representative PowerPoint versions before claiming compatibility.

**H-004/H-018/H-020, repair efficiency:** Berry writes four full script versions, repeatedly announces that it now sees the image “clearly/perfectly,” and performs speculative rebuilds before obtaining the actual opening message. One import-case error (`RgbColor` → `RGBColor`) is ordinary generated-code repair, not an unavailable Python package. A grep returning zero matches is treated as a failed command, then investigated again. Provide pinned library API examples and precise producer contracts; prefer a targeted fix for a diagnosed defect, followed by a short preview/progress receipt. Do not turn an uncertain user-side problem into unsupported corruption claims or broad reinstall instructions. The initial activation stages a presentation generator that is never used; avoid unnecessary preparation without forcing a template over the user's supplied diagram.

The PDF preview is generated during repair but never explicitly published in the retained receipts; the final refers to it without a working delivered link. The user asked for PPTX, so this is a misleading optional-preview claim rather than an unfulfilled requested PDF. Track promised secondary files and attach actual receipts.

Private evidence: `task312-native-package-proof.json`, three canonical source receipts and `task312-render/final.png`. Original PPTX SHA256 `4ffedd1dc013a7a20f413fd90304037b98c4126fc09910cfec2722482604765a`; replacement `78618edcbaf544f3a17a8ca43024f7710fc52d938db31d6fac41744fc4c0fb95`; reading view `be1a3651a27d2fe4cd737411d4307386b1ac64b21df70732855b353fed3368db`. The auditor's initial system-Python lxml import failure was corrected using the bundled runtime and is not a Berry incident. No published files or application code were changed.

### Monthly audit: task480 — metadata repair reverses spreadsheet branding

All 61 captured messages, 32 tool steps and 80,533 reading-view characters are reviewed. The September 24 user asks to enhance an 11,144-byte Leavers Tracker with AESG colours. The only run is cancelled after 499.827 seconds, with no final answer or retained publication receipt. Cancellation intent is unknown. It records 20.620 seconds tools, 449.913 seconds active compute and 28 logical model iterations; the long experience is mostly preparation and investigation rather than an expensive workbook save.

**H-012/H-019, confirmed repair-preservation defect:** Berry stages brand references, reads the workbook and generator conventions, then saves a branded 77,637-byte output. The first validator rejects only inherited author metadata. Its repair script copies `tmp/xlsx/Leavers_Tracker_branded.xlsx` over `outputs/Leavers_Tracker.xlsx` before changing metadata. Despite its name, that temporary file was copied directly from the source and never received the branding changes; the producer loaded it but saved changes only to the output. The copy therefore reverses the colours, font normalisation, logo and print setup. The next validator says `ok:true` on the now 6,530-byte file. Subsequent rendering shows original portrait setup and Liberation Sans plus Verdana. The captured producer/repair scripts and receipts establish the overwrite sequence; the unpublished final workbook is not independently retrieved.

The model then wrongly hypothesizes a font cache/profile problem and claims the black-circle glyph is absent from Verdana. The recorded cmap check proves that glyph exists in all four installed Verdana faces, and the full-string check finds no missing characters. It spends roughly three more minutes tracing PDF font streams rather than checking the target workbook's actual styles or noticing the source copy. No evidence establishes a broken installed font runtime in this task.

**Current validation gap:** the current published branding validator SHA256 `d142096704673409fa881d5e2abd8aa975a4ff5959fb3160b1926bc32728bdba` matches the inspected local source. Its `validate_xlsx` (`deploy/skills/aesg-branding/scripts/validate_artifact.py:555`) checks sheet presence, selected forbidden content, literal formula-error strings, any declared Verdana and author metadata, but not the title/header/body font-colour contract, source/output preservation or actual computed formula caches. A mixed Arial/Verdana source can pass after changing authors. This is a current validator coverage gap; no claim is made that the model's exact historical script is still present in a skill.

Add a narrow package-metadata patch helper that operates on the current output revision and preserves all unrelated package parts, with an exact target/hash receipt. Require expected substantive/style changes to survive a repair, and validate the requested styling profile rather than merely finding “Verdana” somewhere. Estimate 1–2 days for the metadata helper/source-output check and 1–2 days for the initial XLSX role/print contract, within H-019's artifact work. Benefit: avoid silent reversions and long false font investigations. Tradeoffs: explicit source/target lineage, merged-cell role handling, custom user styles and unsupported package features; do not rewrite every workbook through a lossy library solely to change metadata. Test source and branded files with similar names, stale intermediates, inherited authors, merged title bands, mixed fonts, drawing/logo parts, formula caches and a metadata-only repair. Require unchanged data/formulas and preserved requested styling, then measure reversion incidents, font-repair calls and usable-delivery latency.

**H-064, distinct dependency repair:** rendering initially raises `ModuleNotFoundError: office_converter` because the selected renderer's companion resource is deferred. Staging the exact helper repairs that import. This is another fully traced dependency-closure example, independent of the overwritten workbook and later fallback. The current staging gap is already confirmed under H-064; do not label all subsequent font findings as another Python/MCP/network failure.

**Scope/healthy counterexample:** the producer keeps the original cell values/formula strings and adjusts date display without converting stored dates. Several source dates are internally unusual relative to last working dates, but their business meaning is not established and the user requested styling. Do not silently repair personnel facts. The copied output removes formula caches through save/reload as a separate known H-019 concern; no wrong final calculated value is asserted from this cancelled history.

Reading-view SHA256: `93efa5c2b6ac8fe7f3eaf9de9f81ffc3de4485e77c711564e5b5cd74f42ce8c4`. Full captured commands, output sizes and font diagnostics support the causal sequence. Native unpublished workbook pixels/parts and cancellation intent remain unverified. No application or production changes were made.

### Monthly audit: task711 — three missing skill dependencies and a repairable TOC

All 61 messages, 31 tool steps, one completed run and 73,070 reading-view characters are reviewed. The October 1 request asks to format the pasted HR & Finance Systems Administrator job description. The run takes 374.602 seconds, with 67.174 seconds of tools and 291.454 seconds active compute across 29 model iterations; no human wait is recorded. A 5,263,549-byte, five-page DOCX is published. All 20 responsibilities, seven qualifications, ten competencies, ten technical-exposure items and seven working relationships are retained in the captured specification/text checks. Native final pages are not independently certified.

**H-064/H-016, confirmed dependency closure and masked producer exits:** the selected generator first fails because `report_structure.py` is deferred. Staging it reveals a second missing import, `normalise_office_templates`. Generation then succeeds, but rendering fails on deferred `office_converter`; staging that helper repairs rendering. The commands pipe each producer through `tail`, so the outer receipt is exit0 even when the producer fails. The model recognizes and repairs the printed errors, so this is a successful recovery with avoidable preparation, not a false final delivery. Three separate missing prerequisites in this full history strengthen the current H-064 dependency-manifest finding. The initial specification is not written until approximately 140 seconds after admission, after repeated generator/validator searches and reads.

**H-064/H-019/H-020, quiet partial generation adds another repair loop:** the generator reports `tocPageNumbers:false`; the first preview has dotted TOC entries without cached page numbers. The model spends roughly two minutes inspecting field flags, DOCX XML, source functions and heading bookmarks, then calls the same paginator after `office_converter` has been staged. It returns true and a new text extraction confirms pages 3–5. Current published `create_aesg_docx.py:1798` imports that helper inside a catch for ImportError/RuntimeError that returns false without an import diagnostic. The observed missing helper and later successful identical paginator make dependency staging the likely cause of the initial fallback (high confidence), rather than a broken Word runtime. An update-on-open fallback is deliberate and preserves usability, but it should not require models to reverse-engineer why it occurred or confidently guarantee every client refreshes it automatically.

Add the TOC renderer as a conditional prerequisite for report generation and return a typed pagination receipt: cached/partial/fallback, reason, exact headings mapped and the supported repair command. Keep fallback available when rendering genuinely cannot run. This belongs in H-064's existing 2–4-day entry-point/dependency work; an initial typed TOC result and fixture is approximately 0.5–1 additional day. Expected benefit: remove three missing-import activations and repeated source/XML exploration while preserving the branded document. Tradeoffs include conditional office startup cost and conservative fallback across clients. Test this exact seven-section JD from minimal activation, plus missing renderer and partial heading mapping. Require zero undeclared import repairs, accurate cached pages or an explicit fallback reason, preserved source facts and measured time/model turns to usable publication.

**Healthy recovery / confidence:** the final validator is clean, all responsibility letters a–t are verified and the final TOC numbers are checked after repair. The five-page output overview ends at length, so its broad layout assurance is partial; subsequent text/TOC checks do not independently prove every page's pixels. A compact JD profile is a UX hypothesis because cover and TOC add two pages, not an observed rejection. Do not recommend removing required branding from this example without a supported document-type profile.

All 56 messages, 32 tool steps, one completed Qwen run and 82,782 reading-view characters are reviewed. **H-018/H-017/H-002, template requirements should not change evidence meaning:** the source supplies 12 project names plus one shared Project Sites scope. The answer does not explicitly select that association. Current published `prepare_cv_input.py` SHA256 `4f7570f10d74d8402708b9a98603cc037180de04c272070845916e88b40480dd` still requires a description for every selected project or a shared-description substitution. Benefit: fewer blocking questions, shorter outputs and less unsupported attribution; tradeoffs include template pagination and preserving genuine project-specific narratives. Test “use as is,” explicit shared-scope authorization, name-only projects, and ambiguous responsibilities spanning jobs. The two generic Overall Responsibilities blocks are mapped to Centena roles without explicit source headers, but Berry flags the uncertainty and says those descriptions are retained only in the data layer. This disclosure is useful; it does not certify that the associations are true or that all original responsibilities appear in the delivered CV. ### Monthly audit: task188 — four prerequisite repairs; source facts changed before successful CV delivery

All 44 messages, 25 tool steps, one completed Kimi run and 74,708 reading-view characters are reviewed. The September 15 CV conversion completes in 186.445 seconds, including 30.831 seconds tools and 140.204 seconds active compute across 18 model iterations. **H-029, healthy headshot correction:** the heuristic selects a large page/background image instead of the 577×572 portrait. Berry reads all three candidates and explicitly copies the real portrait. **H-002/H-012/H-017/H-019, confirmed source-to-spec fidelity errors:** the source's EWMCE employment period is 2011–2017; the specification changes it to 2011–2016, matching a separate project's period. The source explicitly says “+100 projects” and “+50 projects”; the corresponding spec titles drop both counts, while the final claims those portfolios were included. The old personal package is not the current September23 CV contract, so this historical run alone does not prove the same generator remains deployed. Current H-002/H-019 nevertheless requires post-amendment fixtures and source-to-output evidence; token/line coverage can find a year or role elsewhere and still miss its association with a specific job/project. Add exact employment dates, portfolio quantities, publications and source-specified role/client fields to semantic fidelity fixtures. Require requested layout-only conversions to preserve them or disclose a user-approved omission, and distinguish inferred/unknown metadata. **H-007/H-010/H-014/H-066, rendering/recovery mismatch:** despite saying it will render representative slides, Berry runs LibreOffice on the entire274-slide deck. Operator review is required before retry.” The observed command writes only a task-local PDF/log directory and sends nothing to another service. No proof establishes why LibreOffice produced no output: default-profile collision, unsupported slide/package feature and process/resource failure remain possibilities, not confirmed causes.

Current `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:5253` classifies every generic bash call as `non_idempotent_manual`; the timeout path at1738 calls the recovery transition, whose721–738 events/terminal error always describe an external mutation. Preserving ambiguity for arbitrary shell commands is appropriate, but the universal message misdescribes this local conversion and supplies no specific recovery action. A non-abortable operation can also continue past the idle deadline until its promise settles. The terminal counters record219.773 seconds tools and74.511 seconds active compute; they omit the final360.88-second uncertain tool, so they must not be treated as the entire elapsed processing cost. Provide a first-party bounded Office conversion operation with input/output revision receipts, isolated LibreOffice profile, process/job status, verified PDF existence, selected-slide preview support and cancellation acknowledgement. Give this operation an explicitly designed recovery policy; do not reclassify arbitrary bash as safe using model-supplied assertions or string matching. Preserve the generic shell guard for actual external sends/updates. Show “preview conversion stopped,” whether the original/work product is preserved, and a concrete retry/recover action for local work; reserve administrator/operator review for an external outcome that actually needs it. The current exhausted-budget recovery issue under H-066 must also be covered before presenting a working resume control.
