### Monthly audit: task596 — polished compliance letter makes unverified completed-action assertions

All66 messages, one Qwen run, captured tool sequence and84,439 reading-view characters are reviewed. The September28 request is simply “write me a compliance letter as per this RFP,” with a19-page RFQ. The run completes in530.241 seconds, recording482.255 seconds active compute and26.416 seconds tools over30 model iterations with no human wait. It publishes file `16387632-0eef-407c-8d39-e70054af40fd` (1,054,563 bytes), after healthy visual row-split repair and a deferred renderer dependency repair. This review establishes source-to-spec and captured response defects; the final native letter has not been independently downloaded/rendered page by page.

**H-017/H-025, confirmed unsupported assertions in the produced specification and final assurance:** the RFQ lists a separate SOW, welfare policy, minor works contract and ESG workbook to obtain through Tahweel. Only the RFQ is supplied/read. The generated letter nevertheless confirms those attachments and clarifications have been read and fully understood, all scope is compliant, the ESG questionnaire is completed/uploaded, evidence and company profile are attached, both proposals have been submitted, the September16 site visit was attended, certified HSE/QA systems exist, and no conflict of interest exists. Nothing in the captured request/tool evidence establishes these completed acts or organisation-specific statuses. A request for a compliance-letter draft supports drafting intended undertakings; it does not supply evidence that uploads, attendance or verification already happened. This is not proof AESG failed those obligations or that the proposed legal terms are invalid.

The letter also supplies90-day proposal validity even though the retrieved RFQ shows an optional Response Valid Until field without that period. It drops “if applicable” from the retention/LD acceptance wording, while retaining it for the performance guarantee. The final says the QA/HSE rows use ISO9001/45001/14001 wording, but those numbers are absent from the produced specification. Its short “check before you send” note flags attendance/certification but leaves multiple other unsupported completion statements inside a document presented as ready. This is one full-history confirmed drafting example, extending the existing source/modality findings; fleet symptom counts are not automatically a count of proven false declarations.

**H-023, current-date/temporal consistency:** the task is September28, the RFQ closes September23, and the generator sets the letter date to September23 and asserts submission before that deadline. The final advises adjusting it “if you submit earlier,” without identifying the already-passed deadline. No user request to backdate or confirmed extension appears. Preserve the source deadline as historical, use the actual drafting date unless explicitly instructed otherwise, and briefly identify that submission timing requires confirmation.

Strengthen the artifact drafting contract alongside the faithful rewriting prompt: distinguish source requirements, proposed commitments and verified completed actions; retain applicability/conditions; use visible confirmation fields or conditional draft wording for unknown status instead of certifying it. Ask only the few material questions needed to make a send-ready version, while producing a usable clearly marked draft. Carry these distinctions into structured compliance rows and final readiness receipts. Current durable rewriting text protects supplied facts/qualifications, but this fresh drafting task is outside simple copy-editing; a prompt alone is not a complete source-verification mechanism. High confidence in the recorded source/spec gap; current-model frequency after further prompt changes still requires evaluation, so do not call every current model reproduced.

Estimate1–2 days for drafting/source-state schema and initial fixtures, followed by the existing source-fidelity evaluation work. Benefit: fewer incorrect attestations and less user checking of polished but unsafe-to-send drafts. Tradeoff: overly cautious defaults can make routine proposal drafting cumbersome; preserve explicit user-approved acceptance and avoid requiring confirmation for every standard prospective undertaking. Test this RFQ with missing attachments, confirmed attendance/submission, explicit90-day validity, passed and extended deadlines, conditional terms and user-authorised backdating. Require no unsupported completed-action assertions, source-linked conditions and an accurate readiness summary.

**H-064/H-020, preparation:** duplicate successful input listings, repeated successful mkdir and several code/schema inspections consume model turns; generator specification is first written roughly4m04s after the source pages are fully read. Rendering fails because `office_converter` is deferred, then succeeds after activation. This is the already current dependency-closure issue, not broken installed Python. Compact generator capability schemas and staged dependency closure should remove this repair/preparation work.

**H-019, pagination repair succeeds but recurring work is avoidable:** the initial three-page table splits a short compliance row. The model sets cantSplit on19 rows, renders four pages, adjusts column proportions and wording, regenerates/reapplies cantSplit and gets three pages. It reads all final three preview paths before publication; no >5-image group occurs here. Current `create_aesg_docx.py:516–630` repeats header rows but does not set cantSplit. Add sensible short-row keep-together behaviour to the supported generator, with an opt-out/splitting strategy for rows taller than a page; do not blanket prohibit all tall-row splits. ### Monthly audit: task236 — published extension passes toy tests but rejects its configured tokenizer data

All51 messages, one run, source writes, errors, test revisions and84,843 reading-view characters are reviewed. The September16 user asks about options for measuring ChatGPT usage and wants to test a solution. Berry researches and publishes an11-entry14,624-byte extension ZIP after517.037 seconds,493.296 seconds active compute and15.510 seconds tools over18 model iterations. Six repair attempts are recorded: denied network access, three out-of-workspace writes, a test source-path error and toy-test failures. It reaches completed status and claims a “complete working extension” and exact tokenizer with14 passing tests. The task does not include an installed-browser test or user confirmation it works.

**H-019/H-025, confirmed semantic testing gap in the actual delivered code:** the published ZIP is retrieved through SSM and its canonical hash verified as `bc2b3303b521c98501d379c24f5ec7faab34528a860896a78f50ef200d2842e9`, file `a43dfab4-09f8-46a8-bd9b-330a7b481a5b`. Its primary configured data source is an OpenAI .tiktoken file. The parser calls atob on each whole line and assigns rank from the line index. The real format has a base64 token followed by a space and explicit integer rank; OpenAI's [loader and writer](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/tiktoken/main/tiktoken/load.py) confirm the two-field format. The final tests manufacture base64-only lines, matching Berry's own incorrect assumption, so passing them does not exercise the configured dependency.

A bounded private offline fixture executes the unchanged published parser against4,096 correctly formatted lines (70,570 bytes, safely above its minimum-size guards). It returns null; a256-entry base64-only toy control succeeds. Counting with the correctly formatted downloaded-data equivalent falls back to chars/4 with exact=false. No live browser/backend was contacted by the fixture. This proves rejection of the primary configured data format, not every alternative CDN response or the behaviour of a newly generated current-model extension. The pure BPE core also lacks the encoding-specific pre-tokenisation patterns supplied by [OpenAI's encoding definitions](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/tiktoken/main/tiktoken_ext/openai_public.py); a toy “hello” merge result is not proof of parity with a real encoding.

**H-019/H-025, misleading confidence inside the delivered UI:** for a mocked event explicitly marked non-exact/heuristic, the unchanged popup displays “Counts from local BPE tokenizer (matches tiktoken).” Its exactCount also conflates local-BPE and provider-reported events, so an all-BPE day can be labelled all server-reported. The approximate ±1% claims have no real-encoding comparison in this task. A fixture of cumulative SSE snapshots h then hi produces hhi, and a message with author.role/content.parts yields empty input text. These are confirmed adapter fixture behaviours; the actual current ChatGPT wire format/occurrence rate was not captured, so they are compatibility risks rather than independently observed live-user overcounting. Do not equate visible-text estimates with provider billable tokens.

For the platform, add generated-code semantic fixtures to the existing H-019 verification plan: test public dependency file formats, realistic transport events, fallback labels and claimed accuracy against primary reference implementations, rather than only syntax and self-authored toy expectations. Final readiness should name the exact test scope and distinguish “packaged/prototype,” “offline unit-tested” and “browser-integrated.” Preserve a usable deliverable when browser integration cannot be run, but state the remaining check. Estimate1–2 days for this eval case and scoped readiness conventions, with optional managed dependency-bundle work evaluated separately. Benefit: fewer convincing but unusable code artifacts and more truthful completion summaries. Tradeoff: avoid generic exhaustive tests for every small script; select a minimal fixture that exercises the external contract on which the result depends. Measure usable deliverable rate and failures missed by toy tests. Current prompt changes are not evidence this historical generated-code class is eliminated; evaluate current deployed models before making that claim.

**H-003/H-008/H-020, capability and path repair:** the tool accurately reports that registry.npmjs.org and www.google.com are denied by organisation policy. The model generalises this to “sandbox has no internet,” spends roughly2m39s before its next source-write batch, and reinvents a tokenizer. Its bash-created /tmp directory succeeds but three write calls to that directory are rejected because the file tool requires /home/user/workspace. It then moves to outputs and recovers. Expose a compact runtime capability receipt with the actual workspace/scratch root, available dependencies and domain-specific access guidance; keep policy enforcement and administrator controls. If a known dependency cannot be installed, prefer a verified approved dependency bundle or explicitly approximate implementation over falsely claiming exact parity. Do not whitelist arbitrary hosts, disable TLS, or classify this as generally broken Node/Python. Existing current network messaging is useful; incorrect model generalisation remains an evaluation target.

Private proof `task236-native-fixture/proof.json` and unchanged extracted scripts are retained; source-file proof carries canonical/blob verification. ### Monthly audit: task263 — 17-minute logo cleanup includes avoidable debugging and unreliable visual assurance

All77 messages, one DeepSeek run, complete captured tools and85,265 reading-view characters are reviewed. The September17 request is “Make it HD and remove white background” for a235×102,9,379-byte PNG. The run completes in1,045.751 seconds,874.471 seconds active compute and157.127 seconds tools over38 model iterations, no human wait. It publishes a3200×1102 transparent PNG and an SVG. There is no cancellation or missing final deliverable; this is a completed task with substantial avoidable preparation and verification work.

**H-008/H-020, observed repair loops:** Pillow/NumPy/CairoSVG work. The model probes missing ImageMagick/potrace, silently failed apt-get, two unavailable pip packages and several optional modules, then writes a bespoke marching-squares tracer. Its NumPy boolean addition yields True instead of a0–4 count and crashes. The diagnostic program initially crashes on an unpadded boundary; the corrected diagnostic uses sum, differs from the failing expression, and incorrectly convinces the model the original logic is sound. Repeated source reads and speculative “traceback may be misleading” statements follow before instrumentation finally reveals tot=True. The exact int-cast repair succeeds. A later crop is completely off-canvas because source coordinates are750–1100 for a235px-wide image, and an unquoted filename causes cp to fail while the trailing ls makes the tool look completed. These are generated-code/diagnostic/quoting mistakes and masked errors, not evidence that installed Python is broken.

Use the existing runtime capability receipt and supported bounded image operations rather than recreating image processing algorithms for routine background removal/upscaling. Preserve alpha and brand geometry, report that enlarged raster dimensions do not recover unavailable source detail, and offer vector tracing as approximate unless a real vector source is supplied. Route exact-logo cleanup to a deterministic path with specified mask/edge policy. Versioned optional tracing dependencies should be justified by workload and output benchmarks, not by installing every possible library. Estimate1–2 days within the existing image-operation proposal; evaluate this small-logo workload against native source shape, alpha, resolution and useful-delivery latency. A corrected diagnostic should exercise the same function/expression and boundary conditions as the failed code, not substitute a convenient implementation.

**H-042, independently confirmed alpha/observation mismatch:** canonical source and both outputs are retrieved through SSM. The final PNG has2,217,842 completely transparent pixels,1,289,332 opaque pixels,19,226 intermediate-alpha pixels and254 intermediate alpha levels; its RGB is entirely black. A private white composite visually shows all four letters and the four transparent B counters. Thus the focused observation that the output is a uniformly opaque black rectangle is wrong about this actual delivered image. Later observations that the B counters are filled are also contradicted by native pixels. The model eventually verifies alpha correctly and preserves the requested transparent output, which is healthy recovery. Actual provider-wire compositing/downscaling is not captured, so the precise vision-adapter cause remains moderate-confidence, not independently reproduced.

Seven image observations take approximately83.6 seconds; all terminate normally, so this task is not another length-truncated vision example. A failed off-canvas crop is correctly reported blank. Transparent-versus-composited views create repeated disagreement; deterministic alpha/content-bounds receipts and a separately labelled white/checkerboard preview can prevent this class of loop. The prepared local image guard is still distinct from current deployment and, even locally, small PNGs retain alpha while only JPEG fallback is explicitly matted. Do not call the transparency problem fixed solely because that local guard exists. Add this output to H-042's cross-provider alpha fixture, preserving original transparency and stating how the model derivative was rendered.

**H-019/H-025, final certainty exceeds the check:** the model correctly proves counters are present but incorrectly treats an approximately linear increase in anti-aliased-pixel count with render size as definitive proof of smooth geometry and exact source fidelity. Anti-aliasing is compatible with polygonal or wavy paths. The delivered SVG contains one path with8 implicit polygon subpaths, using M/coordinate sequences/Z rather than Bezier curves. The independently viewed native comparison shows readable BSBG and intact counters, but visibly uneven/wavy outer contours and rounded terminals; “letterforms preserved exactly” and a professionally precise brand outline are not established. Supersampling the original cannot recreate missing vector geometry. Counting holes also does not prove their positions or shapes match exactly.

Conclude only the validated properties: requested transparency, dimensions, recognisable text and counters. Use a source-aligned contour comparison and bounded human-view preview for exact branding requirements, with explicit limits when source resolution is low. Stop after a bounded number of materially different verification attempts and surface uncertainty instead of escalating into repeated claims that contradictory evidence is settled. No final user rejection is recorded, so this is a confirmed overclaim/quality-risk example, not a demonstrated business rejection or failed entire delivery.

### Monthly audit: task286 — intent ambiguity and cancelled clarification; spreadsheet totals repaired before publication

All47 projected messages, one run, three retained queued prompts, full captured tool payloads and85,500 reading-view characters are reviewed. September18 starts with a pasted internal email asking colleagues whether they have appetite for a seven-plot TDD bid; the initial message does not separately ask Berry to calculate a fee or draft a reply. Berry infers that task and creates a fee/demolition workbook plus a reply asserting interest. The run completes in570.406 seconds,447.905 seconds active compute and116.674 seconds tools over21 model iterations with no human wait. It publishes `43091e56-c724-4eb5-aab8-98857f69b4c5`,599,047 bytes.

**H-002/H-015/H-035, intent and queue evidence:** a queued message explicitly asks “Please re word and check grammer” and repeats the original email. It is retained as cancelled with no delivery/run; two near-identical later queued copies are also cancelled and never delivered, separated by159ms. Those are queue cancellations, not three additional failed model runs. Actor/reason is not established here, and the model cannot be accused of ignoring a clarification it never received. The task is before the September24 durable rewriting prompt deployment. It shows an interaction in which a user tries to specify polishing while an unrelated inferred workflow is running, but does not independently prove the current queue FK or steering defect caused these cancellations.

The final reply changes the communicative purpose from asking colleagues for interest to accepting a bid, proposes a fee/programme, instructs Sunena to start and offers a call. Those additions are clearly outside mere polishing, but the original run's intent is ambiguous rather than an explicitly delivered rewrite violation. Make ambiguous pasted-email intent cheap to resolve (polish, draft reply or analyse/estimate), retain an identifiable cancelled/queued correction and explain its delivery state. Do not interpret quoted addressee requests as direct authority to undertake the entire commercial workflow. Evaluate this with an explicitly delivered grammar request, cancelled correction, near-simultaneous duplicate sends and active-turn steering; require faithful voice/action for polishing and a truthful queue state. This extends existing intent/queue recommendations, not a new independently proved three-run failure.

**H-012/H-019, confirmed formula-contract repair with current-code support:** the first specification supplies a column-wide fee formula and explicit6000 disbursement/SUM total rows; the generator silently prioritises the column formula. Rendered PDF and LibreOffice CSV show disbursement and fee total0, plus three demolition total cells0. Berry notices and rewrites every data-row formula explicitly, then CSV validates28person-days/AED118,000 and demolition824,238/1,098,984/1,373,730 before publication. This is successful pre-publication arithmetic repair, not a zero-valued final workbook defect.

Current published/local generator contract still matches `create_aesg_xlsx.py:64`: formula is returned before checking an explicit row value. A private bounded fixture confirms literal6000→=C9*D9 and an explicit SUM→=C10*D10; both remain correct when the column formula is omitted. Treat this as a hazardous/misunderstood precedence contract and missing conflict diagnostic. Reject incompatible explicit value/formula combinations or support documented explicit per-row overrides/default formulas. Do not silently change existing valid column-calculation behaviour without compatibility fixtures. Add typed subtotal/disbursement/summary rows and checks of expected totals and input changes, rather than relying on vision to calculate. Estimate0.5–1 day for schema/conflict handling plus existing functional-artifact tests. Benefit: remove roughly three minutes of avoidable regeneration/verification in this class while protecting correct published numbers. Test literal0, blank,6000, SUM, nested totals and normal calculated data rows; require no silent loss of explicit exceptions.

**H-059/H-039/H-064, additional current preparation evidence:** the renderer initially fails on deferred office_converter, stages it and succeeds. The first focused three-page vision call spends27.99seconds over two length-limited attempts with2 content characters each, then fails empty. Berry uses PDF text/CSV and recovers. It later recognises fixed19.5pt wrapped body rows, adjusts their heights and regenerates. The supporting code still clamps requested widths to45 and sets24.75pt headers/19.5pt bodies; the model's custom height heuristic uses actual clamped widths, which is useful recovery. The final four pages comprise fee, plot schedule and two assumptions pages, contrary to the intermediate guess that the fee sheet spilled. Only page1 gets final pixel inspection; PDF text is read for2–4. The broad “everything checks out” should state numeric verification and that limited visual scope. Native final caches/client geometry are not independently certified by this task review.

**H-017/H-023/H-025, assumptions need visible boundaries:** the original says only the first three plots appear around20% coverage and the last three appear congested/light industrial. The model applies20% to all seven, extrapolates demolition rates, surveyor/day rates and authority lead times without research, and names a reporting standard without verification. It does label rates/coverage indicative and “to be verified,” which is positive. Nevertheless its final calls26,000sqft of buildings a small/low-risk scope and says the estimate is “not a guess.” This exceeds the limited source. The assumptions sheet is datedSeptember17 despite the September18 run. Preserve source versus model assumptions at row/aggregate level and carry qualifications into final summaries; make proposed fees/availability unmistakably provisional. This does not establish that the rates or organisation capacity are wrong, only that they were not evidenced here.

Private proof `task286-formula-precedence-proof.json`; run `ebc462f0-7270-4581-b185-1b0c5a30359d`; reading-view SHA256 `bdbd21951942fb18f1b9f3fae35e6d65cbda1b7a44766b8c3269a245ab9e0ab3`. No application or production changes were made.


### Monthly audit: task941 — stop request queued to expiry, repeated OCR fails, then an assumed geometry answer

All53 projected messages, four runs, retained queue input, complete tool errors/results and85,672 reading-view characters are reviewed. October7 begins with a simple hollow-section image; Berry returns a thin-wall torsion calculation in100.138 seconds. A replacement “real section” starts a401.774-second run,22 iterations,110.059 seconds tools/277.427 seconds active compute, ending cancelled without the requested new-section result. The user then says “stop thinking”; that32.295-second run produces a projected answer but fails session_entries_run_id_fkey. A later explicit “back to simple section … R3” completes in40.851 seconds without tools. Do not classify the cancellation independently as a platform failure: the conversation supplies clear user stop intent, though the precise cancellation actor/event is not established.

**H-001/H-015, concrete stop/queue interaction:** “ok stop” is stored as a normal queued follow-up at03:30:45.316Z, intent=null, with attempt_count0. It is never delivered and expires the next day. The active run nevertheless starts another OCR shell call167ms later, then another model/tool step before cancellation at03:31:10.881Z (25.565 seconds later). Thus the typed stop is not an acknowledged immediate control in this trace. The following “stop thinking” run at03:31:13.788Z encounters the known FK error despite projecting text. This case belongs to the existing monthly FK cohort, not a new count in addition to it.

Provide an immediate, durable Stop action distinct from enqueue, acknowledge cancellation state while preserving source and usable work, and show whether a typed correction was queued, delivered or cancelled. If recognising direct top-level stop text, distinguish it from quoted material, code and a request to rewrite text containing “stop”; do not apply broad keyword cancellation to arbitrary content. Test real active model calls and shell work, near-simultaneous stop/follow-up, stop acknowledgement, failed finalisation and subsequent new turns. Require no normal24-hour queue for an explicitly chosen stop control, bounded acknowledged abort, no FK error and preserved completed answer/history. Current queue fixes are prepared locally and not established deployed; this older task does not prove a fresh incident after a deployed fix.

**H-039/H-061/H-008/H-020, task gets stuck in source recovery:** the replacement drawing is223×667. Its first focused observation invents/contradicts dimensions and ends at length; an overview returns a different shape and dimensions while ignoring the detailed requested question. Berry notices the conflict, which is healthy, but spends many more turns on colour masks, ASCII diagrams, hundreds of connected components, numerous OCR page modes and tiled/upscaled crops without a trustworthy dimension set. Script errors include Image.MaxFilter versus ImageFilter.MaxFilter (masked by later shell output), None tuple unpacking, absent optional scipy and invalid Python `for psm in 11 6 4`. Files created under/tmp work in bash but fail the file/vision workspace boundary, requiring a move and repeat. A focused five-band result again ends at length after the first two bands, yet the run returns to further OCR rather than resolving the incomplete observation. None of this establishes a broken installed Python runtime.

Make source-recovery progress depend on new reliable source facts, not just newly created crops and long successful OCR dumps. Use bounded OCR/crop operations with actual content/dimension/alpha receipts and explicit incomplete-result status. After a few materially different failures, preserve known values, state what remains unreadable and ask for a higher-resolution/dimensioned source or a small parameter confirmation. Supply workspace scratch paths and dependency capability metadata up front. This recommendation supplements existing bounded execution and tool-contract work; the run was not diagnosed as budget exhaustion or a network timeout. Benchmark correct geometry extraction and time-to-trustworthy-calculation, rather than the number of artifacts written.

**H-012/H-017/H-025, native source disproves the assumed new-section geometry:** both original images are canonically retrieved/hash verified and viewed. The simple image is361×751 and clearly shows57,178 and3 dimensions. The replacement is a tapered profile with end slots/brackets; native right-side labels are113.5 and137.5, not the conflicting model observations. Berry's post-stop answer says it can confidently read a63-wide closed rectangular two-cell extrusion and reuses approximately178 height from the earlier sketch, then computes2.81×10^6. It does include “if correct” and asks for confirmation, so it is conditional; nevertheless that assumed rectangular model is visibly unsupported by the replacement drawing. Stopping a long investigation should lead to a short account of unresolved geometry, not a new numerical result based on old dimensions. The later return to the simple section is explicitly authorised and correctly restores that source.

**Arithmetic verification is scoped:** the first J≈2.3398×10^6 and later R3-inner-radius J≈2.3712×10^6 are internally consistent with the stated thin-wall assumptions. This is an arithmetic check, not independent finite-element/structural-design certification or proof R3 means an inner radius in the supplied drawing. The first response's unsolicited shear-stress claim102MPa per1kN·m is inconsistent with its own equal-flow model: T=4qA, A=4725 andt=3 give q=52.910N/mm andτ=17.637MPa. No new geometry or load qualification accompanies102. A harness calculator/parameter receipt should verify all numerical claims, including optional extras; do not add unasked stress values without checking them.

Estimate1–2 days for initial dimension/source-state and arithmetic fixtures within the existing fidelity/eval plan, plus the already estimated queue, OCR and image-contract work. Benefit: less stalled analysis and fewer plausible calculations for the wrong source. Tradeoffs: avoid blocking a legitimate approximation when the user explicitly accepts assumptions; distinguish approximation/units/radius convention from a verified section. Test both images, a higher-resolution replacement, ambiguous R3, changed geometry, an explicit return to the first source, conflicting/partial OCR and stop during recovery. Require source-linked dimensions, no obsolete geometry carried into the new calculation, correct numerical cross-checks and a concise actionable incomplete answer when needed.

Simple source SHA256 `13acaaafe7df763a5ad6b032aaef357ff8e5021f9a3cf7f9412cc990f3842de5`; real-source SHA256 `234deb0fcb3e895a2a54ad070037a5b6529835230cd26de4215990c4f35778ab`. Private `task941-arithmetic-proof.json` and oriented native dimension-label inspection retained. FK run `09596cd4-29fa-4d72-a4b9-4e8ddf753687`; queue item `160328be-628a-49d5-a997-35ee92130d4f`; reading-view SHA256 `81bc34b9e953bb2bb86a901637cdac3f897c91aab4d70791d51115c8663371e8`. No application or production changes were made.


### Full-history review 644: Outlook-address extraction — task 65

Reviewed all 74,669 rendered characters and captured tool payloads for task `aeb64d80-3b39-40cb-bd64-7bac230b16d6` (SHA256 `22d6860d125092b23e95ea45096b9a7adeeed19b16a3cbe8a85376ab5f8c1a46`), including both September 13 runs and the later request for addresses only. First run `091e88e7-74cf-455a-a900-b9f967c22417` completed in 420.415 s, with 306.984 s recorded tool time and 103.749 s active compute; follow-up `35a8d625-36b8-48be-926d-74c1b1c7521c` completed in 5.331 s without tools. No cancellation or final runtime failure.

**Observed / H007, H008, H016, H020:** Eleven attached `.msg` files were read through UTF-16LE/UTF-8/Latin-1 binary decoding because `extract_msg`/`olefile` were unavailable and an attempted pip install returned an SSL unexpected EOF. The install pipeline's trailing `tail` masked the nonzero operation. A full custom scan took 145.85 s; Berry reran the same eleven-file scan for another 145.63 s just to retrieve output previously hidden by `head`. The first scan listed five addresses, including a truncated recipient-stream false positive. A targeted 0.69 s context check detected and removed it before the final four-address answer. Thus neither the false positive nor a general Python failure should be recorded as a final defect. The raw files and any embedded attachment contents have not been independently audited; completeness of the final four-address set remains unverified.

**User experience:** The initial detailed answer included tables, explanations and method notes. The user then asked for only the addresses; Berry complied immediately. This is evidence for adapting extraction output to a small requested result, without assuming that the original ambiguous word “mails” necessarily requested full message exports or that the user rejected the addresses.

**Current-code trace / unresolved recommendation:** `apps/worker/src/knowledge/services.ts:96–133` provides a bounded Tika text extractor; `apps/worker/src/mcp-tools.ts:747–758` has format-specific instructions for Office/PDF and a generic extraction hint otherwise. These do not establish whether the deployed mail parser handles this particular recipient property and embedded chain correctly, or whether the sandbox exposes structured mail fields. Do not recommend disabling network policy or treating all Python/dependencies as broken from this historical pip error.

**Proposed improvement (hypothesis, moderate confidence in applicability; high confidence in observed avoidable work):** Extend the typed extraction/dependency work already proposed in H020/H064 with a mail result schema (sender, recipients, subject, body/chain boundaries, attachments and coverage), and advertise the approved parser route. Persist a machine-readable scan result and retrieve selected portions rather than rerunning the scan. Return a short address list by default for an address-only request. Expected benefit: remove repeated OLE guessing, binary false positives and roughly 291.5 s of scan work in this example; exact saving requires a benchmark because some initial parsing is necessary. Effort 1–2 days within the extraction workstream, subject to current-parser verification. Tradeoffs: parser size, malformed Outlook files, chain/attachment scope and tenant-scoped extraction caches. Test against approved fixtures containing recipient property streams, duplicate chains, mixed encodings and an unrelated attachment; measure correct address coverage, one parse per content/version and p95 extraction latency. Preserve an explicit partial-coverage result if attachments are not searched.


### Full-history review 645: research continues without the controlling source — task 542

All 86,592 rendered characters and captured tool results are reviewed for `4dd6b191-13ba-4bb6-9227-f3166cc0215d`, September 25 (SHA256 `165297adc2c7efa6dae57bee7147afd4c357cc73d77271576808625e514dd3c6`). Run `84d0fff7-7b5d-48e2-a225-1ea7e1384573` completed in 206.359 s, 15 model iterations, 146.562 s active compute and 54.091 s tool time. Twenty-four tools comprise 12 searches (one invalid `countries` argument), 11 scrapes and one shell call. No cancellation, final runtime error or generated artifact.

**Confirmed observations / H003, H006, H016, H020, H037:** Quoted search subjects disappear from the effective query: a query containing the quoted code, quoted chapter and quoted material becomes only `OR`, returning Oregon results; later requested clauses become `OR OR foam plastic light plastic`. These are captured production query/result mismatches, consistent with H037's previously traced local operator parser; the remote BerryCrawl revision is still unverified. The shell downloads silently leave an empty directory while `ls` supplies exit zero. Five scrapes return tunnel errors across distinct domains/routes, one stealth request returns “No proxy provider configured,” two archive URLs return 404, and one PDF returns HTTP 200 with no usable text. Three successful ICC scrapes supply only a subscription/printing notice. Thus 23 completed tool steps do not mean 23 useful retrievals. The task's broad assertion that the sandbox has no direct internet access and that the site blocked it is not established by these masked curl/tunnel results.

**Confirmed source-status overstatement / H017, H019, H025:** The final answer admits that exact Saudi wording/numerals could not be retrieved, which is useful uncertainty disclosure. However it also calls the structure “confirmed from the official ... table of contents,” despite the recorded search excerpts showing the first chapter and Chapter 24, rather than the requested Chapter 26 section breakdown. The answer supplies numerical thresholds, exception summaries, specific clause numbers and statements about Saudi amendments using a different jurisdiction/edition's base-code snippets and model knowledge. This audit establishes insufficient retrieved support and inconsistent verification claims, not that every technical value is wrong; it has not independently adjudicated current building-code applicability or legal compliance.

**Current status / proposed changes:** Existing H003/H006 typed failures, H037 operator preservation and H017/H025 source receipts apply. Add `usableContent`, `accessRestricted`, effective-query change and configured proxy capabilities to retrieval results; classify a subscription notice separately from source text. Bound same-cause retries and preserve healthy search routes. A source receipt should distinguish a snippet, retrieved chapter and analogous jurisdiction/edition, then carry that distinction into the final answer. Prefer a short answer with explicit unresolved source scope after bounded attempts; request an authorized copy for a clause-level comparison rather than continuing equivalent scrapes. Effort approximately 1–2 days for extraction/access classification and focused evaluations within these workstreams; remote configuration verification is required before changing proxy behavior. Tradeoffs: short-but-legitimate pages, avoiding false access restriction labels, and keeping useful fallback evidence available.

**Acceptance:** This exact query must retain the code and chapter terms; access notices must never count as a chapter read; a tunnel failure must not imply a global sandbox outage; invalid argument recovery must select the documented singular `country`; and a base-code summary must preserve edition/jurisdiction boundaries. Measure useful retrievals per call, time to a bounded qualified answer, and unsupported “verified/confirmed” claims. Do not reopen timeout bounds or network policy solely from this historical task.


### Full-history review 646: polished payroll answer retains calculation and source defects — task 944

All 86,948 rendered characters, two runs, source text, edits and captured tool outputs are read for `3e3b1906-4688-488e-b8ad-98c37361a347` (SHA256 `c26e7490a274f59a0063cc1f0d6e8ffb5ac4cb2c3be132ae728476ecbbfc8856`), October 7. Payroll run `0828c991-55f3-4aed-ba91-193af7265020` completed in 460.445 s (323.026 s active compute, 132.252 s tools, zero recorded tool repairs); warning-review run `daaed919-c72c-4edf-84de-40ed3b136402` completed in 114.818 s (56.090 s active, 47.552 s tools). Revisions are recorded as unknown, not assumed deployed commit. No cancellations, Python failure, external-web retrieval or final runtime failure.

**Native verification:** Read canonical production blob bytes through AWS SSM. Published payroll DOCX `b24cfcc1-bf38-4060-88d4-d903c73013aa` is 43,774 bytes, SHA256 `c15a86b4eea118fb9aba8db292242d62f12ee16718ee7d0398fbc5432696abe8`; the warning source `16cd6b6c-f295-4a59-817d-f13747bcb284` is 40,021 bytes, SHA256 `14e8787546017756590a5f6e52c6bcd6ba9de78e0bb00a155717029239a5c2e5`. The payroll XML preserves the quoted gratuity/net amounts and optional exclusion wording. Private fixture: `task944-native-arithmetic-proof.json`; all original byte/hash receipts retained.

**Confirmed / H012, H017, H019, H025:** The case includes two approved unpaid-leave days and asks whether service should exclude time. The answer says they are excluded, but calls the approximately AED 46 impact immaterial and publishes the total before exclusion, making it conditional on whether that exclusion is applied. Within its own 1,648-day/365 convention, gratuity is AED 37,926.58 before exclusion and AED 37,880.55 after the two days; the net changes from AED 53,668.25 to AED 53,622.21, a AED 46.03 difference. This is a published internal logic/calculation defect, not an interval-counting dispute or a claim to have certified the legally payable settlement. The independent official-law index likewise states unpaid absence is excluded under Article 51(4); direct download access returned 403, so live consolidated applicability was not fully rechecked. [Official UAE labour-law download](https://www.uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/1541/download).

The claimed AED 3,878.78 working-day overpayment also substitutes 16 payable calendar days into a 22-working-day divisor; the case never gives payroll's actual eligible working-day numerator. September 1–18 has 14 Monday–Friday days. If both unpaid days were weekdays, the alternative would use 12/22, giving AED 10,909.09 and a AED 242.42 difference from the case's calendar-day policy. That is an illustrative missing-input test, not a reconstructed actual payroll. The published overpayment amount is unsupported. Notice wording also says 30 days ends September 19, then calls a September 18 departure fully served without explaining inclusive/exclusive counting or a waiver.

**Legal/source-status issue / H025:** Neither run performs a recorded authoritative-law or employer-procedure lookup. The payroll document calls written consent the only lawful deduction basis while elsewhere allowing a court basis; the government-hosted indexed law includes a distinct damage-deduction route with a limit, so the blanket claim omits material conditions. [Government-hosted labour-law text, Article 25](https://u.ae/-/media/Documents-2023/Federal-DecreeLaw-No-33-of-2021-Regarding-the-Regulation-of-Employment-Relationship-and-its-amendmen.pdf). Its direct URL now returned 404; this comparison relies on the official indexed text and does not establish employee liability or authorize the disputed deduction. Warning review says “defensible/exactly right for UAE practice” without validating the procedure. Suggested six-month validity, five-day appeal and three-month review are proposals, not established statutory requirements; keep them explicitly subject to the actual company procedure.

**Positive recovery / H019, H039, H061:** Berry rendered the payroll document, noticed the overtime table splitting a cell, added no-split rows, then kept the small table together and added repeated headers. The native final file has 35 `cantSplit` rows and four header properties. Five payroll vision calls consume 111.99 s; three end by length, one early output incorrectly refers to missing additional overtime rows, and the final shorter focused request completes. The final renders' pages 4–6 were not all visually checked after the last layout change, so “all figures verified” is merely a string-presence check, not arithmetic or complete visual certification. Current shared DOCX table generation still lacks the short-row protection already traced in review 639 (task 596); reusable small-table/row controls could avoid the two layout-repair rounds.

**OCR correction / H004, H019:** The warning's native text contains `Junric` twice and `Youstina` once, with zero `Junic`/`Yousina` occurrences. The vision digest drops letters from both names; the final asks the user to verify a possible name inconsistency although source extraction is consistent. Read native text before raising an OCR spelling discrepancy. The native Letter size is confirmed and placeholders are real; those observations remain useful. No warning was rewritten or issued.

**Recommendations/status/tests:** These are content-quality/source-contract gaps under current H012/H017/H019/H025, not reopened generic timeout/Python defects. `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:5398–5410` directs use of current-information tools and bounded primary-page research but provides no enforceable source/assumption/calculation receipt. Add a compact receipt for case facts, rule version, missing inputs, applied exclusions and computed totals; compare it to the deliverable before publication. Include this post-prompt-patch task in evaluation, require current primary verification when a task asks for current law, preserve uncertainty when access fails, and distinguish proposals from governing procedure. Effort 1–2 days for these fixtures/receipt within the existing workstream, plus approximately 0.5–1 day for reusable small-table controls. Tradeoffs: additional verification latency, inaccessible law sources and legitimate company terms differing from minimum rules. Acceptance: unpaid days propagate to the final total; calendar and working-day bases are not mixed; no “fully served” conclusion with inconsistent dates; no “only lawful basis” assertion without checking exceptions; OCR disagrees with native text only as an explicit observation. Measure correct-case outcomes and repair/verification time separately from visually clean publication. A bare `$docx` invocation leading directly to a completed personal interview answer is an intent-resolution hypothesis; no user complaint establishes that choice was unwanted.

### Full-history review 648: LCC recommendations mix useful options with wrong credit mappings — task 400

All 87,313 rendered characters and captured tool payloads are read for `089a6827-e830-4c61-984c-8f9bb73de3dd`, September 22 (SHA256 `a3d6299aec836428b793ffe408d4838a3fc691a7f1183a72518649c82c86a7d3`). MiniMax run `c13a591a-9e05-47de-b28a-498842887d8b` completed in 175.583 s, 13 model iterations, 117.355 s active compute and 53.643 s tools. Twenty-three tools: research activation, nine searches, ten scrapes and three shell calls. Four recorded failed calls are a fabricated schema key and three explicit hostname-policy denials; no cancellation or final runtime failure. The policy errors clearly explain administrator action, identify the host and reject TLS bypass, which is positive recovery information.

**H003/H004/H020/H037 observations:** Five PDF scrapes return HTTP 200 with no usable text. One other scrape reports a tunnel failure. The two successful MDPI responses are explicitly shortened before reaching the paper's substantive content; the second repeats the same shortened result despite requesting main content. Wikipedia provides general context, not the credit requirements. Four quoted-topic searches lose the quoted terms, including a Pearl Community query becoming `PCRS requirements credit description scope`, returning UN peacekeeping and banking material. This extends the confirmed query-preservation symptom. No full PCRS/PRRS credit was retrieved during the task.

**Independent authoritative comparison / H017/H019/H025:** The published chat gives system recommendations with specific credit identifiers, a “minimum of 8–10 systems” expectation and at least three alternatives per system, without distinguishing verified rules from the consultant's chosen scope. The official DMT PCRS manual was retrieved separately for this audit: 14,848,382 bytes, 182 physical pages, SHA256 `cc20a234f0e8e99f6b905ff7462a8b7180a5182597a4b46420acc3878471b4c7`. Read its IDP-1 chapter (printed page 22, physical page 32) and credit table; receipts/selected exact text are saved in `task400-official-evidence.json`. IDP-1 requires an experienced Chartered Surveyor, design-stage development and final-construction-cost updates; the chapter does not set the asserted 8–10-system/three-alternative minimum. The answer's PCRS water `WS-*`, transport `LB-*` and `RE-R4 Stormwater` mappings disagree with the manual's `PW-*`, `LC-*` and `PW-2 Stormwater Management`. [Official PCRS manual, IDP-1 and credit table](https://www.dmt.gov.ae/-/media/Project/DMT/DMT/E-Library/10-24/PCRS-Version-10.pdf).

The adjacent PRRS chapter, checked through the official indexed text, instead calls for at least two listed systems and has an Individual Streetscapes exception. These distinct schemes must not be blended into one submission rule. [Official PRRS manual, IDP-1](https://www.dmt.gov.ae/-/media/Project/DMT/DMT/E-Library/0001-Manuals/PRRS/PRRS-Version-10.pdf). This verifies source-to-answer mismatches in the cited editions; actual project eligibility, later clarifications and current submission applicability are not independently certified. Many suggested options—lighting, irrigation, hardscape and planting—can still be sensible consultant recommendations; unsupported “largest/strongest/mandatory” claims and credit labels are the defects, not the existence of those options. No LCC calculation, workbook or submission was produced.

**Current implementation/status:** Re-read BerryCrawl `backend/src/search/search.service.ts:93–166`: quoted and operator terms are removed from `formatted`, while `performSearch` sends only that formatted query to Parallel and does not incorporate extracted operators. The current local search cause remains present; the remote revision remains unverified. The local PDF parser has uncommitted changes and now uses bounded `PDFParse` with column-preserving separators; `backend/src/scraping/services/scraper.service.ts:3416–3505` detects extensions/MIME and routes production parsing through a sandbox with a deadline and signal. An existing October 10 production probe in `docs/reliability-month-20261010-production-pdf-probe.jsonl` successfully retrieves a different PDF (document tier, 4,180 Markdown characters, 1,236 ms). That demonstrates a successful recent route, not this manual's complete extraction or the deployed parser's exact source hash. Therefore mark the September PDF failures historical and their exact current status unverified; do not assert all PDF parsing remains broken or reopen already improved timeouts.

Task `3b7205b1-4e39-474d-98c4-865d61a722f2` (28 September), run `53b4ed41-3122-4155-92d3-ab5b5c8590b6`, read end to end: 11 messages, one completed DeepSeek run at revision `99964f18`, all 87,734 rendered characters (SHA-256 `4a1c1b1481a58cae6e9df885ac289e0260363cea67d61cc056e028f530c013d5`). ### Full task review 652 — explicit policy updating mistaken for polishing; queue expiry strands the supplied policy (task 732)

Task `d382293f-3326-436e-9793-8e760793ce14` (1 October), four DeepSeek runs and 37 messages, fully read: 88,409 rendered characters including all retained queue prompts and tools (SHA-256 `b4c86929b4ef688b98b53f4bde66155bb6e5c31a792b54d4212c109065b4ff14`). Revisions are unrecorded. Initial run `37d8185a-0173-4585-a1df-06b60f2819c7` completed in 12.837 seconds without research; the user expressly requested a rewrite to align adoption leave with new South African policy. Berry instead polished the old 10-week/10-day wording and emphasised that no substance changed. User had to ask whether it aligned, then approve a further redraft. Research run `98692ebe-79c6-4b99-b9ba-961aab2ffef1` took 140.040 seconds (128.111 active, 7.415 tool); subsequent clause run `5de0c7f9-6ce1-4f07-a7cd-58572844810b` took 16.313 seconds. This is a confirmed missed explicit amendment request, not a complaint that ordinary polishing should change substance.

**H002 intent boundary — proposed prompt amendment, not applied:** classify the full instruction before editing. Ordinary polish/grammar/style requests preserve speaker, facts, modality and meaning; an explicit request to update/align with a named rule authorizes the necessary substantive changes within that scope. Verify the supplied/current rule, distinguish binding provisions from proposals and an employer's voluntary enhancement, preserve unaffected facts and voice, and flag uncertain applicability outside the draft. Current `apps/worker/src/turn-runner.ts:5392` already says broader changes are permitted within explicit scope; adding an example and a paired intent evaluation is more justified than claiming that all rewriting requires a new preservation rule. Confidence high for historical missed intent, medium for a current prompt/model-routing cause because this run's exact revision/wire prompt was not retained. Estimate 0.5–1 day within H002; test identical text with 'polish' versus 'update to the supplied verified rule' and measure unnecessary corrective turns and unauthorized/omitted amendments across providers. Tradeoff: a substantive update may require a source lookup; it must not trigger research for ordinary copy-editing.

**H017/H025 source-confidence defect:** Berry scraped two secondary accounts and a Department statement about a proposed Bill, but never opened the judgment it later cited. The secondary account expressly described uncertainty around the adoption age cap; later draft and memo promoted this to 'age cap removed' / 'no operative age cap' / applicable regardless of age, while relegating uncertainty to separate notes. The draft also omitted material employed-parent/consecutive-leave qualifications. An assertion that an unrestricted-age draft would already accommodate a future six-year limit is logically unsupported. Do not treat a cited-but-unread judgment as verified primary support or substitute optional employer generosity for a statutory entitlement.

Audit-time primary verification read the operative order in [the Court's published judgment](https://collections.concourt.org.za/bitstreams/efc9dec0-7c6b-45f9-ae87-e231eb7e866f/download), physical pages 3–6, and relevant relief discussion on pages 33–36. The invalidity declaration is suspended; the interim adoption wording retains an under-two condition. The order distinguishes a single/sole employed parent from two employed parties, specifies consecutive leave and commencement priority, and provides a time bound for an unagreed division. This confirms that the task's categorical claim that the Court removed the age condition lacked support. It does not determine whether later remedial legislation changed the position by the audit date, or independently settle every interpretive question. Recommendation: a compact rule/source/date/qualification receipt and an uncertainty-aware drafting check (1–2 days within the existing legal-source workstream), with this fixture separating court order, pending Bill and optional policy enhancement. Expected benefit: fewer confident but unsupported compliance clauses; tradeoff: primary-source access and extra lookup latency.

**Confirmed H001/H015/H022 example:** final run `7a91b6e5-136d-46fb-ab4f-11d57d3bd06c` failed after 184.466 seconds with `session_entries_run_id_fkey`, after memo generation, structural validation, visual inspection and successful `persist_artifact` of `9c746f54-f1da-48cd-ab2c-159bb5875e97` (276,275 bytes). Neither was delivered; both expired the next day. The final assistant said the policy was unavailable and asked the user to upload it, although its upload was retained in the queue. This establishes an actual missed-input workflow, not intentional user cancellation or a lost physical attachment. Already included in the diagnostic queue cohort; do not add it again to aggregate 83-run incidence. Current local `promoteQueuedFollowUpSafely` at `:6477–6508` preserves a prior answer and marks a deterministic promotion failure retryable, but the previously verified production worker does not contain the local queue fix. Apply the existing P0 queue transaction/isolation/promotion tests after implementation approval; ensure output remains visible, both queued inputs/attachment refs survive, and Retry/Resume starts the oldest eligible item once. Current `vision-tools.ts:245–338` still ignores an overview QA question, stops on any nonempty response and stores observations without requiring a complete finish. - **Current status:** The current `model-image.ts` already implements decoded preview normalization, a 600 KiB encoded per-image budget, a 3 MiB combined-image budget, and a final 4 MiB request guard. The durable `create_image` reference path still forwards original base64 bytes directly (`sandbox-continuity.ts:1143`), so provider-specific editing-reference limits need a measured check; this task did not attempt to edit the original 10 MB file directly and therefore does not prove a current edit-provider failure.

That evidence supports resemblance to described features, not a direct paired pixel comparison or exact unchanged identity. Repeated pose/outfit refinements are ordinary editing requests, and “same color” is ambiguous; they are not counted as confirmed unauthorized content changes.
- **Proposal / effort / tradeoffs:** Keep saved artifact cards available when a later model call fails, show “Images saved; final response failed,” and provide an idempotent finish-response control rather than regenerate (1–2 days, reusing the H014 recovery framework). For H041, either expose the provider's supported native ratios honestly or make requested-ratio handling explicit with user-approved crop/pad policy, then derive dimensions from decoded bytes (0.5–1 day; automatic cropping risks cutting off subjects). Investigate edit-reference byte/dimension limits separately; downsampling for editing may reduce fidelity. For identity preservation requests, pair source and output when feasible and qualify unverified claims (0.5–1 day prompt/evaluation work).
- **Meaningful checks:** Reproduce a post-generation 413 and verify all five saved output references remain accessible with no second image charge; assert actual decoded ratio equals persisted actual-ratio metadata for every selectable ratio; test a 10 MB EXIF-rotated reference across vision and editing without modifying the source; test normalized filenames with repeated spaces; require direct paired comparison before claiming side-by-side identity verification. Compare recovery completion, duplicate-generation rate, size failures and image-edit latency against the month baseline.


### Full-history review 655 — task 1218: destructive search rewriting and inaccessible large code documents

- **Coverage:** Read the complete 89,105-character history (SHA256 `31e13bac5229b6ea022ae2d61ff06118bf30c7bc67507f19f8c797ff190d08ea`) of task `aa57f1ff-4a26-4565-b282-9b2ad17789a7`, all 30 messages and run `238e8029-6732-4edc-af73-58a067a2e4d0` on October 12. It completed in 236.476 seconds, with 213.273 seconds of recorded active compute, 19.855 seconds of tools and three repairs. No cancellation. The user asked whether a service elevator needs a lobby before a main waste room.
- **Confirmed production search defect / current local cause (H037):** Twelve searches were made. Nine included quoted phrases and were returned with those phrases deleted. For example, a UAE fire-code/elevator query became `OR lobby clause 6.2` and produced US lobbying legislation; a mirror query became `pdf full text mirrors OR OR OR` and returned unrelated books and a pop song. The model noticed US results but continued variants affected by the same transformation. Current BerryCrawl `backend/src/search/search.service.ts:94` extracts quoted phrases/operators, removes them at lines 121–125, then `performSearch` passes only `formatted` to Parallel, without the extracted constraints. The local code and October 12 captured production responses independently support the cause, high confidence. A fresh remote source hash/deployment check is still needed before certifying the current fleet version. The default search country is also US, but its independent contribution to this task's results is not measured.
- **Large-source capability gap, not a timeout (H003/H049/H067):** Two direct downloads correctly failed under the task's hostname policy, with actionable administrator guidance and no unsafe TLS retry. The official ADCDA PDF scrape then rejected `Response body exceeds the 26214400-byte limit`. The model tried async extraction against the same official and mirror URLs; the job completed with `FAILED / EXTRACT_NO_USABLE_CONTENT`, not usable clauses. The captured tool wrapper marked job polling completed, but the nested job outcome failed. An independent read-only audit web fetch reports the official PDF as 47,523,957 bytes, corroborating the size obstacle; the auditor's fetch also failed rather than reading the code. Current BerryCrawl retains a 25 MiB document-download cap (`scraper.service.ts:107,3553`) and the PDF parser has a separate 500-page cap. Merely increasing the download cap would not address both limits. Existing parser improvements to table geometry do not solve large-source ingestion.
- **Historical content-quality failure / current replay unknown (H003):** Scraping a section mirror returned HTTP 200 and `success:true`, but its title was `Client Challenge` and content said a required part of the site could not load. The model subsequently described mirrors as paywalled/blocked, although this trace establishes a client challenge, not a paywall. Current scraper code includes blocked-page, empty-content and challenge detection; do not recommend those as wholly absent. Verify this exact Scribd response against the current classifier and ensure unusable-content outcomes remain explicit through the MCP wrapper.
- **Unsupported certainty after failed retrieval (H017/H025):** The final response categorically labelled one arrangement FLS compliant and described DCD review practice, supplied ratings/door/detection details, asserted adoption of multiple foreign standards, and introduced a “3-day accumulation” waste-room fact. The retained user/run input supplies no three-day storage duration or building height/occupancy/lift designation. No captured primary UAE clause or cited foreign document was successfully opened. The final caveat admits the UAE code was not retrieved, but does not undo the categorical opening. This is confirmed insufficient evidence and unsupported task-specific detail; the audit does not certify that the proposed design is unsafe or decide the actual project approval.
- **Independently verified source qualification:** The cited [ICC 2006-code interpretation](https://www3.iccsafe.org/cs/committeeArea/pdf_file/BU_06_01_07.pdf) contains multiple exceptions to the lobby rule, including sprinkler/hoistway arrangements. They were omitted from the categorical “more than three storeys” explanation. This document is a historical US-code interpretation, not proof of the project's applicable UAE requirements. The official [ADCDA code URL](https://www.adcda.gov.ae/-/media/Project/ADCDA/ADCDA/Digital-Library/UAE-Fire-and-Life-Safety-Code-of-Practice.pdf) remains unread in this task and in the auditor's web fetch; applicability, edition, amendments and DCD approval remain unresolved.
- **Proposal / benefit / effort:** Preserve search intent end to end, passing raw query and supported provider filters or reconstructing constraints rather than dropping them (H037, 0.5–1 day; provider operator semantics need explicit tests). Add a bounded large-PDF route with page-range extraction, a content-addressed extraction handle and typed size/page-limit errors; propose upload-to-Library or administrator-controlled fetch when policy blocks the source (H049/H067, 2–4 days; impose byte/page/time quotas and preserve tenant ACLs rather than remove safety limits). If the same size-limited source is submitted to async extraction, preflight or return the actual limit and supported recovery route. Require source/jurisdiction/applicability checks before mandatory compliance wording and state conditional design advice when the exact rule cannot be retrieved (H025, 0.5–1 day prompting/evaluations). A recovery modal should distinguish “host not allowed,” “document too large,” and “website did not provide content”; they require different actions.
- **Meaningful validation:** Replay the exact nine destructive queries, asserting quoted phrases, multiple sites, exclusions and country intent survive to the provider; compare relevant-result rate, search count and wall time. Process a permitted 47.5 MB, >500-page code via bounded page-range retrieval without loading unrestricted text into one model request. Verify extraction job failures cannot be presented as source evidence, challenge HTML cannot become a successful document, and an unavailable-code answer neither invents project facts nor labels a design approved/compliant. No code, policy or production task was changed.
